The list scrolls inside the body now with the foot fixed under it (`.qcfoot`: the count beside the button at a desk, above two equal halves on a phone), and the ticks are kept per booking for as long as the page is open (`qcKept`) and cleared the moment the release goes through, because a booking sent back for changes is a different file and is checked from the top. **What is recorded is who checked and when** (`campaign.qc` in the activity record, labelled and filed under Creator Campaigns in both copies of the map): the step itself is the evidence the check was made, because nothing else opens this gate, so the record carries the name rather than a second copy of the list. Every other step still moves on the press — this is the one that hands work to somebody else. **Confirm creators is reached from three places** (2026-09-25, reported missing by the user): under the Selection list, under the bookings on the Creators tab (`#campLock2`, pressing the same act), and from the header's warn line, whose **Review and confirm** (`#campNextGo`) opens Selection on the button. **Confirm creators moved to the Client selection pane** in the same batch: it stood above the tab strip and was therefore drawn over Overview, Schedule and Finance, where it acts on nothing, and on Finance that made three weighted controls on one screen. It sits under the selection it commits. `tests/prod.js` drives the sheet, the hold, the last box and the record. |
| The quality check is a gate, and a second pair of eyes is optional (`supabase/migrations/2026-09-21-qc-is-a-gate.sql`, `js/campaigns.js`) | **The nine checks were a speed bump on one page, and the database knew nothing about them.** `advanceOption` moved the booking with a direct table write, so a sheet somebody closed was the whole of the gate: nothing recorded that a check had been made, and "the second may not be the first" is a rule only a server can hold. Asked for by the user on 2026-09-21 ("qc should actually be a gate … if unsure always pull in another person to review too"). **Release goes through `campaign_qc_pass(p_option, p_want_second)`**, which asks `campaigns.campaigns` Work, refuses anything not at `submitted`, files the check in `option_qc` and moves the booking; and a **trigger on `campaign_options`** refuses any move into `reviewing` without one, because RLS on that table is still the permissive open finding and a gate only the function holds is one a PATCH walks past. **Nobody is assigned.** The user's rule: "just make it requires a second team member to review, could be anyone as long as its not the person who requested a second person (no self-approval)". So asking for a second sets `qc_second_wanted` and holds the booking at Submitted, and anybody else — an admin, a manager, whoever is at a desk — completes it. A colleague who leaves or is on a shoot can never strand a booking behind their own account. **No self-approval is a unique constraint, not a check somebody wrote**: `unique (option_id, team_member_id, round)` with `on conflict do nothing`, so the same person pressing again is the same check and the count does not move; the page says so before the press (Release is shut for the person who asked, and `Ask a second reviewer` is gone once somebody has) and the database says so whatever the page allows. **A booking sent back is a different file and is checked from the top**, keyed on `revision_round`, and `campaign_qc_reset` clears the ask with it. **The card says what it is waiting for, where the button that will not move it is** (`.qc-hold`, warn: `Checked by {name}. Waiting on a second reviewer.`), because the next step is a verbal ask across the desk and the person walking past has to know what to ask for. No notification, at the user's request. **The record is the evidence**: `campaign.qc` already named who checked and when, and now names the second reviewer too. `tests/qc.js` drives the page against the stand-in and `tests/sql.js` §19 drives the function, the trigger and the constraint against real Postgres. |
| A satisfied ask does not deadlock the people who answered it (`js/campaigns.js`, 2026-09-21) | **A asked for a second reviewer, B checked and released, somebody reverted Client review — and then neither A nor B could release, each was told it was *they* who had asked, and a third person was needed to repeat what two people had already done.** Reported from production. **A revert is a plain write of the state** (`stepBack`), so it leaves `qc_second_wanted` set and both checks standing at the same `revision_round`: it is the one path that puts a **satisfied** ask back in front of the sheet. The page then read the flag alone — `qcGo.disabled = short \|\| (qc_second_wanted && qc.already)` — which treats the ask as permanent rather than as something that gets answered, so everybody who had checked was blocked for good. **The database had it right the whole time**: `campaign_qc_pass` moves the booking at two checks whoever calls, so the page was stricter than its own gate, which is the wrong direction for a page to be wrong in. Who asked is the **first row by `checked_at`** — the ask is made in the same call as that person's own check, so the earliest row is the asker and nothing has to be stored to know it — and once the second review is done the note names both and says so. Nothing else changed: `stepBack` still writes only the state, because with the reading corrected a revert leaves a true statement about the file (two people checked this exact cut) rather than a deadlock. `tests/qc.js` §7 drives the reverted state from **both** sides, asserts neither is told to wait, presses Release as each of them, and asserts the one-check hold still holds. |

| The term adjustment is asked for, never applied by itself (`js/money.js`, `js/crm.js`, `supabase/migrations/2026-09-21-the-term-adjustment-is-asked-for.sql`) | **A line typed at RM 400 over a three month term printed RM 444.44.** Reported by the user on 2026-09-21 ("my rate is 400, but short term adjustments should be an option for user to add manually not automatically"). `rateFor()` divided every line that had a term by the term's factor without being asked, so the rate somebody typed was not the rate on the screen and they had to work backwards from it to find their own figure. **The factors are unchanged** and still live in `money.js` alone; what changed is that applying one is a tick on the line (`client_services.term_adjust`), offered only where the term carries a factor and named for what pressing it does (`Apply the 10% short term adjustment`, `Apply the 5% long term discount`). Off, the rate is billed as typed and the line says nothing, because there is nothing to explain. **Only an explicit `false` turns it off** (`rateFor(rate, months, adjust)`, `termNote(months, adjust)`), and that asymmetry is load bearing: an issued letter is a snapshot taken before the column existed and carries no flag, so it redraws at the figure it printed. Nothing is left to the default — the column defaults to `false`, the form stores it on every save, `issue_letter` writes it into the snapshot and `get_portal` sends it with the line, so the console, the letter and the client's page cannot print three different figures. The migration backfills every existing line whose term actually prices it (3, 12, 24 months) to `true`, guarded on the column being new, because those lines were quoted with the adjustment in them. `tests/crm.js` drives the tick, `tests/sql.js` §20 the snapshot, the payload and the backfill run twice. |
| Console: Services (rate card) | Two tables, Services and Add-ons, categories as sub-headings **carrying their count**; admins edit, everyone reads. **One heading, Price, over the amount and the unit**: the unit qualifies the price (`RM 360.00  Per post`), so Rate and Unit were two headings over one value; the cells stay two tracks so every amount keeps the same right edge. **Active costs no column and no accent**: nearly every line is active, so the row is the name and what it costs, `Inactive` is a neutral chip beside the name with the row faded, and Set inactive / Set active sit in the ⋯. The schema **seeds the whole card once**, into a database that has none, and **seeds `detail` once** (`where detail is null`): the card is edited in the console by the people who own it, deletions included, and a seed that ran on every re-run would put a deleted line back and overwrite their corrections the next time a schema change shipped. A line added to the seed list therefore never reaches an existing database; a new service is added on the Services page. `tests/sql.js` runs the seed twice against a real Postgres and asserts a deleted line stays deleted. |

five creators each carrying a filled blue forward button made a pane where nothing said which action mattered, and broke the one-blue-action law the audit counts. `tests/prod.js` asserts the player, the two shapes and that only Release to client is blue. **The team can hand a file in for the creator** (`Upload for {creator}` on the draft step, `teamDeliver()`, at the steps `creator_can_deliver` allows: pending draft, changes requested, submitted; The file lands in the same hand-in the creator's do, so the card, the release and the client's page cannot tell whose hand put it there. **The ceiling is 1 GB a file** (`ADSPACE_CONFIG.s3.maxUploadMB` 1024, raised from 300 on 2026-09-22; the signer's own ceiling is 2 GB).
| Console: Creator Campaigns (`js/campaigns.js`) | **Next means next.** The rail's Key dates took the earliest shoot of every live booking and called it the next one, so a date that had passed went on being promised — 12 Sept still reading as the next shoot in October. It is the earliest date from today onward; where every date is behind us there is no next one and the row reads **Last shoot** rather than claiming something that is not coming. The dates sit in the `--sunk` chip `.readfield` uses, because a flat list of labels gave the figures the same weight as the words beside them. **Pending visit is a step, not the absence of a date.** `nextAction()` read every booking at `pending_visit` as "N shoots have no date yet", so a campaign with every shoot in the diary told the team two were unscheduled. It counts the undated ones (`no date yet`), else the dated ones ahead (`2 shoots are scheduled, next on 20 Sept 2026.`), else the ones that have passed and were never moved on (`… have passed and still read Pending visit.`); `delivery` / `deliveries` for a seeding campaign. `tests/six.js` sets every booking to Pending visit with a date and asserts the line names the next one, then blanks one and asserts it is the one counted. **A date field is a field, not a button** (`.input[type=date]`, `.input[type=time]`, in `css/portal.css` beside the file field): `appearance: none`, the value left aligned through `::-webkit-date-and-time-value`, `min-height: var(--ctl-h)` so an empty one keeps its height on iOS, the flex centring putting the value on its middle, a calendar or clock glyph on the left and no picker mark on the right under a coarse pointer, Chrome's mark kept and quietened at a desk. The phone shoot row is `minmax(0, 1fr) 122px`, measured against `10:00 AM` at 16px behind the glyph. **A date nobody has set says so** (`.sched-one.is-unset`): an empty `input[type=date]` draws nothing at all on iOS, no `mm/dd/yyyy` and no caret, so an unset Publish date was a blank pill under a label with nothing saying it was a date field. The hint replaces the browser's own placeholder (`::-webkit-datetime-edit` at `opacity: 0`) rather than printing over it, and leaves on focus. **It hangs off `.sched-field`, a wrapper around the field and nothing else.** Hung off the cell it was centred on the label as well: on a phone `top: 50%` of a 65px cell is 10px above the centre of the 44px field inside it, so "Not set" printed across the box's own top border. The phone rule written to correct that re-anchored to `bottom: 0` and restated the height from `--ctl-h-sm` — a second guess at a box already on the page, 6px out at the desk where the field is 38px and the token is 32, and silently wrong wherever the first guess was. A wrapper needs neither guess and the original `top: 50%` is then simply right. `tests/sch.js` measures the hint's centre against the field's at 390, 768, 1280 and 1440. **A narrow Schedule row is two lines of fields, not three.** Each booking stacked Shoot, Draft due and Publish one per line with an 11px label in a 72px track beside each, so five creators drew fifteen full-width date boxes and the pane read as a form rather than a schedule. Draft due and Publish share a line, every label sits **above** its field as it does everywhere else in the portal, and the shoot keeps its date and time together under one label. The cells are `align-items: start`, or a field stretches to a row sized by its neighbour and draws a 90px box. **The booking register clips its own rows** (`.bookreg { overflow: hidden }`): every `.kcard` inside carries a white background at `border-radius: 0`, so the last row painted a square edge over the register's two rounded corners and an open row's sunk step backgrounds painted over them again. Rounding the row's last child chased the symptom and kept missing whichever element actually owned the bottom edge. Nothing is clipped by it, because `ADspaceMenu.place()` puts every row ⋯ on the viewport. **One bulk-date panel, moved to whichever pane asked for it**: `#bulkBox` is markup inside the Creators pane, so Schedule's own Bulk dates opened it inside a pane nobody was looking at — the button appeared dead and the form was waiting on the Creators tab afterwards. `bulkOpen(btn)` moves it under the pressed button's `.viewhead` and opens it there; a second press on the same head closes it. Schedule's copy of the action hides with `#bulkToggle` when nothing is in production. **Add creators runs two jobs and names both**: `Existing creators` (a labelled search filling its section, a count of what is still addable, and a bordered scrolling list) and `New creators` (Add creator, **folded shut**, because it is the rarer path; a restored draft opens its own fold, or the half typed creator is there and invisible). Every input in it carries a name, including the per row rate and the profile link. **The word is Creators List, never "roster"**, on screen and in the section labels; the identifiers (`rosterView`, `loadRoster`, `?tab=roster`) are addresses, not copy, and stay. **A missing profile link is asked for where it is missed, and the tick is what opens into it**: ticking a platform the creator has no link for grows that box into the field (`.pbox` > `.pbox-tick` + `.pbox-link`, `needs-link`), and Add saves what is typed into `creator_profiles` through the same `readProfile()` parser the Creators List uses. Before it, ticking a platform meant leaving the campaign, adding the link on the Creators List and coming back, so the tick went in and the link never did; the first fix put the field in a labelled row **after the Add button**, which is downstream of the control that sends it and reflowed the whole row on every tick. One card per creator, folded; Confirm creators; steps Confirmed → Pending visit → Pending draft → Reviewing → Changes requested → Scheduled → Posted → Completed, each gated by its data (draft link, publish date, posted on or after the date) with Revert; withdraw / replace / reinstate in the ⋯; In production is derived, not stored, so reverting, withdrawing or replacing the last creator puts the campaign back to Open for selection; bulk dates; campaign due date; the Invoice fold appears only once a creator is confirmed and leaves again if the last confirmation is reverted, one Save / Cancel covering the number (`AINV` + six digits) and the PDF together, with Remove PDF and Undo; `get_campaign` withholds `invoice_no` and `invoice_url` until then, so the client cannot read an invoice it has not yet caused; results as a table grouped by platform; Publish to client / Unpublish. |
| The way out is last in its row (2026-09-20) | **Thirty-one action rows put the primary first and three put Cancel first**, so a hand that had learned a position pressed the wrong control when it moved quickly — reported by the user on a phone and at a desk. `DESIGN.md` had stated the order since it was written (Save / secondary / Cancel); the three that disagreed were the two Team sheet feet and every `.changebox-actions` (`js/ask.js`'s note, the post editor in `js/admin.js`, Request changes on `/review/` and `/creators/`). All four are primary-first now. **`uxaudit`'s `order` rule measures it by x, never by markup order**, because a row can reverse itself in CSS and what somebody presses is what they read; it judges only a row holding both an action and a way out, and on a phone a wrapped foot line by line. |
| Every action row starts where the last one did (`css/portal.css`, `admin/index.html`, 2026-09-21) | **The order was fixed a day earlier; the anchoring was not.** Nineteen sheet feet in the console sat in two places: `.formsheet` and `.askcard` anchored their pair to the **right** of the foot, every plain `.sheet-card` (the task, the letter, the document, every void and delete) at the **start** — so the hand learned a position on one screen and had to find it again on the next, which is the fault the order rule exists to stop. Reported by the user on 2026-09-21 from five screenshots, and their instinct was right while the theory named was not: the serial position effect governs a list somebody scans (it is already spent on the nav rail) and a pair of buttons is not a list; what governs a pair is motor memory, Fitts and Jakob. With the order fixed at primary-first, **right is the wrong anchor**, because it leaves the way out in the corner every platform reserves for the confirming action. `.sheet-foot` states `justify-content: flex-start` once, the two `flex-end` overrides are gone, and `.changebox-actions` and `.zone-actions` went with them. **A count in the foot follows the actions** (`.lpicksum { order: 1 }`, `.qcfoot > #qcGo`'s `margin-left: auto` retired): it explains the button beside it so it stays on the line, but the control somebody presses is what must not move; on a phone it keeps its own row above them (`order: 0`). **And the short link's Cancel was a bare `.btn`**, the only sheet Cancel with no modifier class: `.btn` is `flex: 1` at the base, so it measured **446px** against every other Cancel's 114 and read as a slab with the primary stranded beside it — the position was never that screen's fault. It is `.btn-quiet` like every other, and `.sheet-foot > .btn { flex: 0 0 auto }` stops the next one. `tests/geom.js` measures **every** sheet in the console at 1280 and 390 rather than the ones a walk opens, and asserts the mechanism: the primary starts the foot at x=0, no foot button has `flex-grow` above zero at a desk (so `Void document` at 118px against the 116px floor is correct and a filled row is not), and on a phone they are equal halves. Fourteen of those checks fail with the CSS put back. |

The track is **150px**, measured against `Nurul Ain binti Abdullah` (134px at 13px) with room over it; a longer value ellipsises the way `.log-person` always has, and the full one is in the Activity record's own three-column table. Both copies of the template (the `is-narrow` rule and the 640 media query) carry it, because the second is the one that gets forgotten. |
| A sheet opens on what there is to change (`js/sheet.js`, 2026-09-20) | **No form sheet focuses a field any more.** On iOS a field taking focus raises the keyboard *and* zooms the page, so the reader landed on a form scrolled and magnified past most of what they opened it to read, already typing into a field that is rarely the one they came for; correcting a phone number began by deleting a half-typed name. The `.sheet-card` takes focus instead (`tabindex="-1"`, no ring, `.sheet-card[tabindex]:focus { outline: none }`), which keeps everything focus was there for: Escape closes, the trap holds, the dialog announces itself. `o.focus` survives for a sheet whose whole purpose is one value and is passed by nobody. The same removal covers the Team member and group sheets, Add lead / Edit client, contacts, service lines, calls, request replies, the rate card line, the short link, the Add creator sheet, the campaign form and the creator picker's search. A `.focus()` **after a failed save** stays, because the standard asks for the first invalid field. Asked for by the user on 2026-09-20. |
| Seven more forms are sheets (`admin/index.html`, `js/crm.js`, `js/admin.js`, 2026-09-20) | **One record, one way to edit it, on every route.** `crmAddBox`, `crmContactBox`, `crmServiceBox`, `crmTouchBox`, `crmReplyBox`, `svcBox` and `addLinkBox` were `.panel` blocks that unfolded at the top of their section, so on a phone Edit put the form a screen away from the row and read as nothing having happened; the client form was worse and was **moved into the record in place of its head**, hiding the client while their own details were corrected. | One shape for editing a record (`js/sheet.js`, 2026-09-20) | **A colleague, a user group and a creator are edited in the same card.** The Team page unfolded a `.panel` at the top of the section, so on a phone pressing Edit on a row put the form a screen above it and read as nothing having happened; the user asked for the creator's shape everywhere on 2026-09-20. `#teamAddBox` and `#groupAddBox` are `.sheet` > `.sheet-card.formsheet` (620px, narrower than the 780 a picker needs), head with a close mark, a scrolling `.sheet-body`, and a foot carrying Cancel quiet and the primary, equal halves below 560. **The scrim does not dismiss a sheet that holds typed changes** — the fault the creator sheet had, where a click a few pixels wide of the card threw the form away; the close mark, Cancel and **Escape** always close, because a dialog a keyboard cannot leave is a dialog nobody can leave. Focus is trapped and handed back to the control that opened it. On a phone the card comes up from the floor, is promoted for the move (`will-change: transform`), measures itself in `dvh` where that unit exists (`vh` on iOS is the tall viewport, so a sheet measured in it starts its foot — with Save in it — below the fold), and pads its floor by `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`. |
| The phone type scale, and the iPhone focus zoom (`js/chrome.js`, `css/portal.css`, 2026-09-24; supersedes the 16px floor of 2026-09-20) | **iOS zooms the page when a field under 16px takes focus and never zooms back**, and the answer from 2026-09-20 was a 16px floor on every field under a finger, which the user sent back on 2026-09-24: every select and filter on a phone was huge beside the text around it. **`js/chrome.js` adds `maximum-scale=1` to the viewport on iOS alone** (iPhone, iPod, iPad, and an iPad reporting Macintosh with touch points): since iOS 10 Safari ignores it for a pinch and honours it for the focus zoom, so the zoom stops and the reader's pinch zoom stays, which is WCAG 1.4.4. Android does not zoom on focus and would honour the value for a pinch, so it never gets it; `user-scalable=no` is never used. Under a coarse pointer a field is 15px in 44px, a small control 14px and a status select 13.5px, both at `--ctl-h-sm` 38px (the stray `.select-sm { min-height: var(--ctl-h) }` coarse rule is gone). `uxaudit`'s `zoom` rule measures that floor at 390 and `target` holds a small control to 38px under a finger. |
| Console: Creator roster (`js/campaigns.js`) | Creator · Profiles · Fee · ⋯ on the console's own record row, never the Short Links `.slink` it used to borrow (which set a person's name in the slug's monospace face and pushed two icon buttons onto a line of their own). Edit / Set inactive / Remove in the ⋯; `creators.active` is surfaced at last, so the refusal that says "Mark them inactive instead" now points at a control that exists. A profile is a `.plink`, the same outlined button with the leaving-the-page icon that the client's own page uses, never the 999px `.pchip` pill it was: a pill is the shape this portal gives a state, so a link drawn as one read as a status somebody had set rather than as something to open. It shows its handle only where the handle reads as a name (18 characters or fewer), because rednote stores a profile id there. The name is stored the way it is compared, so the spaces nobody typed on purpose never reach the database; the warning names who holds it while the form is still open, and the save refuses and puts the cursor back in the field. **The name check now runs whether or not a profile link has been typed**: an empty set of links returned early, so the commonest way to key somebody in twice — their name and nothing else yet — was the one path that said nothing. A space *inside* a name still makes it a different name and stays a caution, because two people can genuinely be told apart by one. **The record cell counts and dates; it does not name the campaign** (2026-09-20, the user sent the name back the same week it shipped): a title is longer than the column and arrives truncated, and the names are on the creator's own card one press away where every one is legible. **Built for four hundred, not for three**: a platform filter cuts the list on the axis a campaign fixes first, fee bands (`Up to RM 300`, `RM 301 to 500`, `RM 501 to 800`, `Above RM 800`, `On quote`) divide it as `.svc-cat` sub-headings with counts, and inactive people are their own band at the foot. **Creator · Profiles · Campaigns · Fee · ⋯, a table with columns.** A list whose every value is stacked against the left margin has to be read line by line before it can be scanned, and `#rosterList` had no `.crm-table` at all, so the rows sat straight on the page ground with no card behind them. The **monogram disc is gone**: it was there to make a row findable by eye and on this roster it cannot, because most names are Chinese and a first character gives 是yy呀 and 是甜甜啊 the same circle, which is the identical failure that had already ruled out an alphabetical band. The **record** (`4 · last Aug 2026`, `None yet`) is counted in `loadRecord()` off `campaign_options` from `confirmed` onwards (`DONE_STATES`, **`submitted` included**: it was left out when the step arrived, so a creator whose draft was waiting on release showed — and came back to `1` on release, which the user reported on 2026-09-22), and is what differs between two rows on a roster of one fee and one platform. There is deliberately **no "on a campaign" mark**: a creator is often on several at once, so the chip was true of nearly every row. The **profile is a link on the row** (`.plink-bare`: the platform, the handle where it reads as a name, and the mark that says it leaves the page) and not an item in the ⋯, because a creator is looked up constantly. On a phone the row is two lines (name and fee, then the links with the record at the end of the same line) at 80px, and the ⋯ carries a negative block margin so its 38px target stops setting the first line's height. **The two lines share one right margin** (2026-09-20, the user reported uneven spacing from a phone screenshot): the ⋯ spanned the first line only (`"name rate act" / "links rec rec"`), so the record line ran to the card's padding at 358 while the money above it stopped at 302, and `.cr-rec` is a flex box, so the `text-align: right` it carried moved nothing — `—` sat at the **left** of a 148px track while `1 · Sept 2026` filled it and looked right aligned. The ⋯ spans both lines now (`"name rate act" / "links rec act"`), the meta track is 104px measured against `12 · Sept 2026` at 12.5px, `.cr-rec` takes `justify-content: flex-end` (what actually moves a flex child), and both meta cells are `align-self: start`, because centred the record floated to the middle of a links cell that had wrapped to two rows. |
| Creator portal (`creator/`, `js/creator.js`) | **A finished booking asks how it went and stops asking for money.** Payment details are named at `scheduled` and `posted` and leave at `completed`, because a standing call to action on a closed job reads as something still owed; in its place is **Your experience**, five stars written to `campaign_options.creator_rating` by `creator_rate` (1 to 5, refused at any other step, changeable while the booking stays completed, never shown to the client). **A submitted file is an attachment line, not a tile** (`.filepins` / `.filepin`: the paperclip, the name, the size): the page can play none of it once it is handed in, so a wall of 9:16 squares showing a file type and a truncated name spent the screen saying nothing. Tiles stay while the creator is still assembling the hand-in, where they are things to look over and take back off. **The draft countdown is amber, and red once it has passed** — it was blue, which is this portal's word for "press this", spent on a date nobody can press. **The Sign out button is named and hidden until there is a session**: it was created empty and labelled only when a booking painted, so the sign-in cover carried a nameless button. **The one party in this operation with no page of their own.** Drafts arrived in a Google Drive folder somebody had to find and paste a link to; "when am I shooting" was asked on WhatsApp. Signing in is **a code, not an account**: `creators.access_code`, eight characters from a 31 character alphabet with no 0/O/1/I/L in it (31^8, six orders of magnitude past the six digit code a bank sends by SMS), carried in the link so one tap is enough and kept in `localStorage` so it is typed at most once, on a new phone. Not SMS OTP: the agency holds phone numbers rather than addresses, a message costs money every time, and a freelancer has no password to remember. Reset in the creator's record, which is the only place the code and the link are shown; the old link stops working and it asks first. **A creator sees their own booking and nothing else**: the campaign, the brand, the brief, their platforms, their fee, the dates and where their own work has got to. Never the client's stage, the campaign's commercial state, the client's amount, who else was offered it, or the team's notes on the row, and never a campaign still in `draft` or an offer they have not been confirmed on — withheld by `get_creator`, never only by the page. **Handing in** is open at `pending_draft`, `changes` and, since 2026-09-22, `submitted` (`creator_can_deliver`): a creator who pressed Submit after one file found the box gone with the second still on their phone, so the hand-in stays open until Release to client moves it on, the button reading **Update submission** at `submitted`; taking a file back off shuts at `submitted` (`creator_can_retract`), so a submission under review cannot be emptied from the creator's side. **A file's kind and stored type fall back on its extension** (`typeOf` / `kindOf`): a `.mov` picked from Files can arrive with no type, and a video recorded as `file` was drawn in the console as an attachment that downloaded when pressed; the console's `mediaKind()` reads the name too, so a file already recorded as `file` plays. Files go straight to S3 by the same signed PUT the console uses (`sign-upload` takes a creator code and an option id instead of a session, checks them with `creator_may_upload`, and builds the key from the option it verified so a real code cannot be pointed at another booking's folder), `creator_submit` moves the step to Reviewing and keeps the caption they wrote, and a file can be taken back off until it is handed in and not after. **The Draft step names both routes into it**: a `?` hint that opens by itself the first three times and then retires behind its own mark (`hint()` / `bumpHint()`, counted in `localStorage`), because an instruction is right while a screen is new and furniture ever after, and a `title` tooltip cannot be reached on a phone. The paste field is labelled as the fallback it is (`Or paste a link, if they cannot upload`), and **Copy portal link is an icon button beside the ⋯ on each creator's card**, because sending a creator their link is the everyday action and it was three steps inside their record. **A value that is only read is not a field to type in** (`.readfield`): the creator's code, their link and the client links were readonly inputs drawing the same box as every editable field, inviting a change that cannot happen and hiding the one thing anybody wants to do with them. **AP01 is named on approval, not on submit**, because a creator who has to reshoot would otherwise have filed payment details against work nobody accepted and the correction lands in the ledger. English and Chinese; covers for the code, an invalid code, a creator stood down, and a page that could not load. **The upload reports itself**: up to **1 GB a file** (300 MB until 2026-09-22) (`ADSPACE_CONFIG.s3.maxUploadMB`, refused by name before a byte moves, because a file turned away after a ten minute upload is a file uploaded twice), several files a booking, the progress Content Review already draws (what is going up on the left, how far on the right, `Saving` once every byte is out and S3 has still to answer), Submit shut while anything is in flight, one bad file never abandoning the rest of the batch, a two minute silence ending the attempt with a reason, and the outcome written onto the card **after** the repaint that follows it rather than before, or the redraw throws the one line explaining what happened away. It failed in production twice on two defects that compounded: `creator_add_file` declared a variable called `id` and then looked the booking up with an unqualified `where id = p_option`, which PL/pgSQL raises **at run time and not at create time**, so the schema applied cleanly; and the page threw that error from inside a fulfilment handler whose sibling rejection handler cannot catch it (`.then(ok, bad)` — `bad` never sees what `ok` throws), so nothing was said, the bar stood at 100%, and Submit then refused because no file had been recorded. `tests/sql.js` runs the whole delivery path against a real Postgres and `tests/creator.js` makes the database refuse a save and asserts the page names the file. |

a profile another creator holds is refused without naming them; a creator cannot empty their set; the same set again files nothing; and **every change is filed twice**, a `creator_profile_changes` row with the set before and after and an activity row (`creator.links_self`, `creator.links`, `creator.links_restored`) naming every link that went and came in full. **Link history** in a creator's ⋯ on the Creators List lists the changes newest first, marks the creator's own, and **Restore** puts back the set a change replaced through `creator_restore_profiles`, which is itself a filed change. The fee is not on the page and not in `get_creator`. On a phone the chrome's Forget this device keeps its glyph and gives up its word, so the name beside the mark is no longer cut to "S..". `tests/cprofile.js` drives both sides; `tests/sql.js` proves the functions against real Postgres and compares `get_creator` and `activity_section` with the migration. |

The function no longer sends `rate`, and `currency` went with it (it existed only to format that figure and names the client's market, which is a fact about the client). **The withholding is the function's, never the page's**, which is the rule every other creator-facing fact already followed. The page drops the fact row, the `fee` word in both languages, `money()` and `js/money.js` itself, which it now loads for nothing. **The client's page is untouched**: `get_campaign` still sends the rate, because the client is the party being quoted. The field grows out of Approve instead — the `.pbox` pattern, a control that creates a need answering it in place: `.namebox` > `.btn-approve` + `.namebox-field`, `max-width` 0 to 100% because a stated end animates and `auto` does not. | Client-facing creators, the page opens on the work (`creators/`, `js/creators.js`, 2026-09-21) | **The detail card folds, and it is shut when the page opens.** The client came to choose creators, and the creators are below it, so the brief, the purpose and the figures stood between them and the thing to do; the purpose sat *outside* the card altogether, under the title, where it read as a caption on the page rather than as the card's own content. `.engage-card` > `.engage-head` (`#engageToggle`, the label as the control, `#engageSum` at the far right) + `.engage-body` > `.engage-inner`, which runs `grid-template-rows` 0fr to 1fr with the opacity at `--t-slow` / `--ease` — the same move `.crm-group-body` makes, driven by a class because `hidden` is `display: none` and cannot animate. | Client-facing creators (`creators/`, `js/creators.js`) | **The decision is two buttons and nothing else.** Approve and Request changes; the note box opens only when Request changes is pressed and refuses to send empty; the name is asked once by prompt at the moment somebody decides (`adspace_reviewer`, the key `/review/` uses) rather than sitting as a field on every card, because a reader who is not deciding anything was being asked to fill one in. **A decision leaves a record.** Approving used to move the booking to Scheduled and say nothing at all: no activity row, and nothing on the card naming who had approved it. `review_draft` now writes to `activity_log` under the reviewer's own name, `get_campaign` sends the last `option_reviews` row with the booking it is about (withheld with the draft, like the files), and the card keeps `Approved by Wei Ling on 16 Sept, 10:47.` — with the note under it when changes were asked for. **A draft is decided on the card, never behind a window.** The client reads the creator's work in place (`draftPreview`: the video or image at 9:16, the caption, and the pasted link where there is one) and Approve and Request changes sit under it, so the decision uses the same `.approve` component Content Review uses — the same blue Approve, the same quiet Request changes, the same note box opening under them. It is not the same list as the profile buttons beside it, which are where the creator is looked up. The console stores the placement by its printed name (`rednote, Instagram`), so `platsOf()` maps it back to the key before looking the word up, and a Chinese reader sees 小红书 on the selection row and the booked card alike. Selection with optional backups (recommended, never enforced); `View rednote Profile ↗`, `View Instagram Profile ↗`; same status words and colours as the console; foldable amount card with View PDF; results readable on a phone without sideways scroll; English and Chinese. |
| Asking for one value (`js/ask.js`) | **A browser dialog is not a control this portal has**, and it was reached for eight times anyway because it is one line of code and the right control is twenty. Those twenty lines, written once, in two shapes chosen by what the value **is**. `rename(host, btn)`: the value is already on the screen (a content set's title), so the thing itself becomes editable where it sits and the pen becomes the tick that saves it — Enter sends, Escape puts it back, an empty name keeps the field open, and leaving the field neither saves nor discards, because walking away is not a decision either way. The tick takes the **ink** fill and not blue, since Publish is two controls along in the same bar. `inline(btn)`: the value is not on screen yet and one control needs it, so the field grows out of that control and it confirms — the `.namebox` the client pages use, with a seed that may be a function (the month a new set is named after). `note(after)`: several lines somebody else will read, so a textarea opening under the control that sends it, which is the shape `/creators/` Request changes already had. **There is deliberately no sheet in it**: where an act has a consequence that must be stated before it is agreed to, a field growing out of a button hides exactly the part that matters. `tests/sets.js` drives the content set flow — which nothing covered, which is why two prompts lived in it for months — and fails on any browser dialog opening at all. |
| Asking before an act (`js/confirm.js`) | **The console asks on the page, and a browser dialog is not a control this portal has.** `js/ask.js` covers asking for one value where the value is the whole of it; this is the other half, and the rule that divides them is already written: where an act has a **consequence that must be stated before it is agreed to**, the question is a sheet, because a field growing out of a button hides exactly the part that matters. Until 2026-09-20 the console honoured that rule with `window.confirm` and `window.prompt` **thirty-one times**, which cannot be styled, cannot be translated, and on a phone is a system sheet that takes the reader off the page they are acting on. `ADspaceConfirm.ask({ title, body, go, tone, field | fields }, onYes)` builds one `.sheet` > `.sheet-card.askcard` (440px, not the 780 a form needs) and keeps it: Escape and the scrim cancel, focus is trapped and handed back to the control that opened it, a destructive question opens on **Cancel** so a stray Enter costs nothing, the go button is `btn-danger` / `btn-warn` / `btn-primary` by `tone`, and `cancel: false` makes it a refusal being reported rather than a question. **A value is asked for in the same breath as the question**, not in a second dialog after it: `field` for one, `fields` for the two that belong together (setting a service line by hand needs the state **and** the reason, and it used to be two prompts of which the first took the state as free text). `match` is the typed-back name a delete asks for, so removing a client from Content Review is one sheet rather than a confirm followed by a naked prompt. **The way back never asks**: reinstating a QR code, setting a colleague active again, restoring a creator. `#askGo` and `#askCancel` are stable ids, and every suite that used to auto-accept a browser dialog presses them instead; `tests/prod.js` keeps a dialog listener as a tripwire, so a `confirm()` put back anywhere in that walk is an error rather than a hang. |
| Copy (`js/copy.js`) | **A copy button copies, and says so.** The fallback for a refused clipboard was `window.prompt`, which put a browser dialog with an OK and a Cancel in front of somebody who had pressed a button labelled Copy, and copied nothing unless they also selected the text themselves. It fired far more often than "outside a secure context" suggests: the Clipboard API rejects whenever the document is not focused, which is every press made while another window had focus. `document.execCommand('copy')` over an off-screen textarea is the second path — no permission, every browser, synchronous — and whichever one works the button says Copied. No browser dialog. |
| Short Links (`workers/links/`) | **No Status column.** Live is true of nearly every row, so a STATUS heading stood over a cell that was empty almost always and read as something broken rather than as the accent rule working. The rate card had already settled this: where a state is the default for its list, the row says nothing while it holds and names the exception beside the name. `Paused` is a chip next to the slug and the row fades, exactly as an inactive service does; four columns, not five. **The list is live.** `hi.adspace.me` is a Cloudflare Worker on the ADspace account (`adspace-links`, custom domain on the `adspace.me` zone, which is what creates the proxied `AAAA 100::` record and the certificate); a row saved in the console redirects as soon as it is saved and a row paused there stops redirecting. The "Not live yet." `.routenote` under the table is gone, because a standing fact that is false is worse than no line. **The Worker holds no key that can write anything**: it calls `link_resolve(p_slug, p_qr)`, a security-definer function granted to `anon` that answers one exact slug with one destination and one state — no listing, no search, no prefix match, no second column — so the public key is enough. The alternative, written into the schema for months, was a service role key in a Cloudflare secret so that a redirector could read one column. What this gives away is what a short link gives away by definition. **Four answers, because they are four pages**: `ok`, `missing`, `paused` (the team turned it off), `revoked` (a pulled QR code, turned away while the typed link keeps working, which is why `link_qrs` carries its own identity). **302 and never cached**: the reason these live in a table is that a destination can be corrected, and a 301 would mean the correction never reached anybody who had already followed the old one. Parameters a client appended to a printed link are carried onto the destination, minus `q`, with the destination's own parameters winning. **The host is one value, `ADSPACE_CONFIG.linkHost`**, read by `js/admin.js` and written into the field's prefix; it was typed into the script and into the markup and the two could disagree. **`go.adspace.me` is deliberately not retired and is not served by this Worker**: a QR code encodes the whole address, so the twenty or thirty already printed on slides keep working for as long as that host redirects. `tests/links.js` drives every answer against the module with `fetch` stubbed, because the sandbox's network policy refuses `hi.adspace.me` exactly as it refuses `digital.adspace.me` and `curl` proves nothing either way. |

Reported by the user on 2026-09-21, who looked for it in the form. It is the ⋯, where every lifecycle flag in this portal lives (`Pause` / `Resume`, `links:work`), never a field in the form and never a select on every row: the row already names the exception with a chip, which is the shape a rate card line and a colleague take. **Pausing says what it costs before it goes** (a warn sheet: `/dp stops redirecting, wherever it is already printed, posted or sent. Resume puts it back.`) and **resuming asks nothing**, because the way back never asks. The write takes `.select('id')` and names a refusal, since PostgREST answers a refused update with no error at all. **The card the row moves into is opened** (`ADspaceGroup.keep`), or a pause takes the row off a screen whose Paused card is shut by default and empties itself, with nothing saying where it went. The act is `shortlink.updated` with `Paused` / `Resumed` as the detail rather than a tag of its own, because a new tag has to be added to `ACTION_LABEL` **and** to `activity_section()` in SQL in the same breath or it lands in the record with no word and no section. **Both hosts in the quiet line open** (`hi.adspace.me`, `go.adspace.me`), drawn the way the Register's routenote draws `/verify` — underlined, a new tab, no external mark — because the fastest way to know a redirector is alive is to follow it, and the bare host answers rather than refusing (the Worker sends it to the website, since somebody who types it has half a URL). `tests/run.js` drives the sheet, the pause, the record, the silent resume and the two links. |
| Access: a level per section, and per part (`supabase/schema.sql`, `js/admin.js`, `js/team.js`) | **Access is a level per section, not a switch per verb.** `team_roles.access` / `team_members.access` is a jsonb map over eight sections (clients, review, campaigns, links, register, services, team, activity) with four levels ranked **none < view < work < manage**, and `allowed(section, level)` is the predicate every policy asks. The old model was six section booleans plus one global `can_remove`, so the authority to destroy could not be granted for one section without granting it for all. **The levels are drawn on reversibility, not on add/edit/delete/share**: add and edit are reversible and so is publishing (Unpublish exists), a permanent deletion is not. **A part is a key under its section** (2026-09-22): the panes and lists a section is made of, keyed `clients.billing`, `review.sets`, `campaigns.finance`, `register.hr`, and nothing finer. Clients has contacts, billing, services, documents, requests and calls; Content Review has sets and settings; Creator Campaigns has campaigns, creators and finance; the Register has documents and hr. **A part answers with its own level where the group set one and with its section's where it did not**, in `allowed()` and in the page's `may()` alike, so a group that never opened the Parts fold is exactly where it was and only an exception is stored (`{"clients":"work","clients.billing":"none"}`). The policy spec keys each table to its part (`client_contacts` → `clients.contacts`, `batches` → `review.sets`, `creators` → `campaigns.creators`, …), and the two parts that live in columns of a row other parts also write are guarded by trigger: `clients_billing_guard` refuses a change to the billing columns below `clients.billing` Work (skipping a cascade from a deleted billing contact, `pg_trigger_depth() > 1`) and `campaigns_finance_guard` the invoice columns below `campaigns.finance` Work. **HR letters were a section (`hr`) and are the part `register.hr`**: the backfill moves the level across once, `none` included, guarded on the old key. **The Billing switch is gone** (`can_billing`, retired 2026-09-22 at the user's request): the letters in Documents print the registered name and billing address anyway, so the switch hid a pane and not the facts; Billing is the part `clients.billing`, the pane draws at View and its fields are read only below Work, and `verify_letter` asks `clients.documents` now. The columns stay, unread, as `can_doc_void` does; `allowed('billing')` refuses, which is what a retired switch should do. `can_doc_void` was folded into **Clients: Manage** the same day. The policy block reads the levels directly — select is `view`, insert and update are `work`, delete is `manage` — so the four statements are the four levels. **The one-argument `allowed(flag)` survives for `admin`, and a bare section or part name in it means `work`**, so a call site missed in the cutover refuses rather than quietly granting a write; `allowed('remove')` is gone. On the page, `may(key, level)` and one body class per section and per part (`no-manage-review`, `no-work-clients-billing`, `no-view-campaigns-finance`, …; a part hyphenates the dot) replace `no-remove`, **every control names its own part** (`data-need="clients.documents:manage"`, listed in full in `css/portal.css`), and a tab carrying `data-part` draws only where the part is readable, with the address falling back to Overview. The Team panel is **one block per section**: the section's own select on its head line as the main control and its parts directly under it, each a select starting at `Same as section` (the user's own structure, 2026-09-22: a part is read where its section is, never in a second list that names the sections again), then the Admin tick, and the row reads back grouped by level with the exceptions in brackets (`Work: Clients (Billing: No access, Documents: View)`). **The panel names every section as the nav names it**: Documents, not Register (the key stays `register`, which is an address and not copy), and **My Work**, whose key is `ops`. **A granted part's unset option reads `No access`, not `Same as section`** (`GRANTED_PARTS` in `js/team.js`), because the four operations parts do not inherit and the other label would be a promise the database does not keep. **And it is said once, with only the levels the database asks for** (2026-09-24, the user found Performance reviews offering No access twice): `PART_LEVELS` restates what `ops_granted()` and `allowed()` actually check (the whole team's queue and the reports View; templates View and Work; another person's hours Manage; performance reviews View, Work and Manage; every Activity record tab View), a level stored before the narrowing is shown as the level it grants (`offered()`: Work on the queue reads View, Work on the hours reads No access) and is saved that way, and a part is a row with its name left and its select on the section select's own right edge, 200px at a desk and 160 on a phone, rather than a four-across grid whose wrapped labels stood the selects at three heights. `tests/perms.js` checks every select in the panel at 1280 in both themes and 390. **The levels are backfilled once from the booleans each group already held**, guarded on emptiness. `tests/levels.js` proves the ladder, the fallback, a part above a shut section, the retired ticks, the admin, the stood-down colleague, the stranger, the backfill (twice) and the HR move (twice) against a real Postgres. |
| Team (`js/team.js`) | **A person carries their Department, Position and Role standard** (2026-09-26, the user: "change / update all the roles, department under teams for each member … and it populates over to Performance"; `supabase/migrations/2026-09-26-department-and-role-on-the-team.sql`): `team_members.department` (Creative, Marketing; a segment) and `role_family` (the four Performance role standards) beside `designation`, which the sheet calls **Position**; the words are `ADspaceWords.dept` / `.roleStd` in `js/words.js`, the only copy, read by Team and Performance alike. The row's line is `AD026 · Creative, Production Executive` (Employee ID, then department and position), and the search reads it. The pane travels in the address (`tab=` for a client, `pane=` for a campaign) and **pushes** a history entry, so Back and Forward walk the record while everything else in the console still replaces; Overview is the default and stays out of the address. A pane is the disclosure, so opening Billing opens the fields, and the refusal that blocks Active lands there with the first missing field focused. Re-opening the same record keeps the pane somebody was working in. **The campaign's next internal action is derived on every repaint**, never stored, so reverting a step cannot leave it lying. **Schedule, Deliverables and Client selection read the bookings and never write**: they are views over `campaign_options` and `campaign_deliverables`, not second stores. **Activity keys on the subject** `activity_log` was written with, which is the client's or campaign's name at the time, so a rename leaves older entries behind; that is stated in the code rather than papered over. **The account leaves the sidebar**: who you are, the register and Sign out sit behind one control at the end of the console bar, opening on the address, the theme and Sign out, closing on Escape or a click outside. **A creator is added or edited in a sheet**, so four hundred rows are not pushed down the page. **`/creator` is a work queue and one open booking**, ordered by what has to be done, marking the one waiting on them, with the booking in `location.hash`. **`/creators` is a comparison on a wide screen**: named columns (Creator, Posting on, Profiles, Fee, Backup), every track stated so a column starts at the same x on every row, and back to the stacked row below 760. **`/review` keeps the gallery and adds the Review Canvas**: the mockup at size on a stage, and the status, the copy in full, the note and the one decision area in a rail, with prev/next, the arrow keys and Escape. The canvas **moves** the card's own blocks and puts them back on close, so there is one approve control and it cannot drift. **`/client` takes the same rail**, with Overview and Request change heading the facts they name. |

One prominent blue action per view, two at most, counted by `uxaudit`'s `accent` rule over `.btn-go`, `.btn-primary` and `.btn-approve`. Hover, pressed, `focus-visible` and disabled are all stated, and every `.btn-*` hover sits inside `@media (hover: hover)`. **The campaign record opens on the work**: the same identity area the client record uses (client's mark, name, real state, Edit, a meta line naming the client and the person in charge), an Overview of flat titled sections over the bookings, the schedule and what has been handed in, and a rail of one block per question (Selection with a bar, Amount right aligned with the total under its hairline, Key dates, Client link, Details). The six statistic cards are gone; what the campaign is waiting on us for stays the warn line under the identity and is deliberately not repeated in the rail. **The bookings are one register** (`.bookreg`): five stable cells on a stated grid, a header over them, hairlines between rows, `.kcard` keeping its steps but losing its border. **Creator Campaigns and Content Review are registers**, not card grids, with the campaign states as `.crm-band` divider rows. **Every console route opens on its command bar**; a heading that only repeated the bar or the tab above it is gone and `.console-body` carries a 20px step so every route starts at the same y. The Short Links notice is a `.routenote` line under the register. **The client portal opens on an identity area** (mark, name, state, one blue action) with the rail's blocks flat under eyebrow titles and the reach links de-pilled; Portal access is green and Main contact neutral, matching the console. **The creator's queue is one surface** with a warn edge on the booking that needs them. **Creator selection is one summary band**, and its profiles column is left aligned so every row starts at the same x. **The client record's one-per-pane section action is the blue primary** (Add contact, Add service, Issue letter, Add entry), superseding the outline rule, which was written when the record was one column and four of them ran down its right edge. **A rule about something inside the record pane keys on the pane's own width** (`ADspaceState.fit` → `is-narrow` ≤640, `is-tight` ≤460, from a `ResizeObserver` on `.console-body`, `.rec-pane` and `.rec-rail`), never on the window's: at 1280 the pane is 591px, so every `@media (max-width: 640px)` rule inside it was false exactly where it was needed and the Services name track collapsed to 59px with the description one word per line. Container queries are deliberately **not** used, because `container-type: inline-size` implies `contain: layout` and would become the containing block for the `position: fixed` row ⋯ menus. The services row states `minmax(180px, 1fr)` for its name and stacks below the width that can honour it; the booking register is one line down to 460 and two below it, never three; the tab strip never wraps and scrolls instead, with `flex: 0 0 auto` on each tab; the account menu states its own 260px width, `flex-direction: row` and left alignment, outranking `.kmenu`, which comes later in the file; Copy link and Preview are neutral, because blue is spent on the one thing to press. `tests/geom.js` asserts all of it at 1440, 1280, 1024, 768, 390 and 320. |

Measured at a 900px window: the × at y=434, the bar that offered the file back at **y = −223**, entirely off screen. The file vanished from under the pointer and nothing visible was offered, which is what "there is no safeguard" describes on a control that has had one since it shipped. `undoBar(text, undo, host)` takes the element the act belongs to and draws an `.undobar-here` directly after it, removing it again when it is used or times out; the handed-in file passes its `.handedin` block and the invoice PDF passes `#invMsg`, because the Finance pane is inside the record too.
