The × **arms** the file instead and the confirm takes its place, naming what it costs (`Remove? The creator would have to upload it again.`) with a red Remove and a quiet Cancel; Escape, Cancel or a press anywhere else in the card puts it back, one file armed at a time, and the ×'s `aria-expanded` says which. On a 9:16 card the question covers the card, over the work itself, so it cannot be missed by an eye on the video; on an attachment line it sits at the end of the row, because a line has no room for a card's worth of words and the row already names the file. **The soft remove and the Undo stay behind it**, so there are three steps between a stray click and lost work. This supersedes the earlier "there is deliberately no confirm", which reasoned that the Undo taxed the everyday case less — true only while the Undo is seen, and an unnoticed press is the case it cannot cover. `tests/undo.js` drives both shapes: the arm, the words, Cancel, Escape, the confirm, the Undo behind it, and a refused update; the walk carries the armed card. |
| Row ⋯ menus (`js/menu.js`) | `ADspaceMenu.place()` and `ADspaceMenu.onScroll()` are the only copy; `crm.js`, `team.js`, `portal.js` and `campaigns.js` keep their own click wiring (what a menu holds and what it is scoped to differ) and call in for the rest. Four copies of the same twenty lines had already drifted, and the fix below had to be made three times and still missed the fourth. Placed on the viewport by their button so the table cannot clip them; **opened upwards where the room is above**, or a ⋯ on the last row opens past the bottom of the window. The creator card inside a campaign was the one menu that never called `place()` at all, so it stayed absolutely positioned in its card and a creator low on the list opened a menu running off the bottom of the window; `tests/keyin.js` could not click the third card's menu item and had been red on `main` since. The scroll that closes an open menu ignores **the scroll the browser fires to reveal the button it just focused** (`held` / `scrolledAway()`: a scroll that has not moved the anchoring button by 2px is not a reason to close). Without that guard the ⋯ closed itself the frame after it opened, and on a phone the menus on the lower rows could not be opened at all. |
| Activity record: the client's and the creator's side (`supabase/migrations/2026-09-21-…`) | **The record answers a dispute, and it held one side of every conversation.** A client approving a post wrote to `reviews` and nothing else; a client confirming a set of creators wrote to `campaign_confirmations`; a creator handing work in and rating a finished booking wrote nothing at all — so the one screen anybody would open to ask "who approved this, and when" showed only what the team had done. Five security-definer functions now log what they already do, under **the name the person typed** as the actor: `submit_review` (`review.approved` / `review.changes`, with the note), `confirm_selection` (`campaign.confirmed`, with how many), `creator_submit` (`campaign.submitted`, with how many files), `creator_rate` (`campaign.rated`) and `portal_withdraw` (`request.withdrawn` / `request.reinstated`). **`save_selection` is deliberately not among them**: it fires on every tick as an autosave, and a record full of half-made selections is one nobody can read — `confirm_selection` is the commitment and the one that carries a name. No table, column, policy or permission changes and no behaviour changes: each writes one extra row and returns what it returned before. `tests/trail.js` runs all five against a real Postgres, asserts the row each writes, that a refused call writes nothing, and **that every tag written is one `ACTION_LABEL` names**, so a row can never land in the record with no label and no section. |
| Activity record: a section at a time (`supabase/migrations/2026-09-22-activity-by-section.sql`) | **The record is read a tab at a time, so its access is a part per tab** (`activity.clients`, `activity.ops`, `activity.team`, `activity.review`, `activity.campaigns`, `activity.links`, `activity.register`, `activity.services`), asked for by the user on 2026-09-22: opening the campaigns log to the team used to open every client's billing change and every letter with it. The ladder already had the shape — a part answers with its own level where one is set and its section's where none is — so a group that never opened the fold is exactly where it was, and `activity.campaigns: view` over `activity: none` reads one log and no other. **What the policy needed and did not have is the map from a tag to the section the console files it under**, and that map cannot be derived: about thirty of its hundred-odd entries do not follow their tag's prefix (`client.handles` is Content Review, `service.override` is Clients, `client.removed` is Content Review). `activity_section(p_action)` restates it in SQL and `tests/sql.js` §21 reads **both** copies — `ACTION_LABEL` out of `js/admin.js` and the `when action in (…) then '…'` arms out of `supabase/schema.sql` — and fails on any difference, the guard the operations schema and its migration are held to. A tag nothing names answers `other`, which has no part, so it falls back to the section and a row written by something added next year is read by whoever can read the record rather than hidden from everybody. `ops.deleted` is labelled and the record gains a **My Work** tab. Each tab carries `data-need="activity.{part}:view"`, the Activity link draws where **any** tab is readable, and `tests/team.js` drives a group with one part. |
| Activity record | **Every tag a function writes is one `ACTION_LABEL` names, and every everyday write leaves a row** (2026-09-22): nine tags had been written for months with no label (the QR events, four Letter of Offer events, the client's draft decision, a service override), so those rows arrived with no word and no section; Content Review wrote nothing for a set created or renamed, posts added, a post edited, the client's handles, logo or access code, or the Drive folder, and it does now, an edit naming the fields it changed. **Documents is a filter tab** and every `document.*` and `register.*` row files there. **A campaign's events are filed under the campaign, not the creator.** The pane reads `activity_log` by subject, and every per-creator event — a step moved, a shoot or publish date changed, a fee corrected, a creator withdrawn or reinstated — was written with the creator's name as the subject, so none of it ever reached the campaign's own Activity pane: it showed the invoice, the lock and the bulk dates and nothing else. `logSubject()` is the campaign's raw title, which is the value the pane queries with, and the creator is named in the detail. `tests/prod.js` opens the pane and asserts the step moves are in it, because nothing opened it before. Table (when, tag, subject, detail, who) grouped by day, filter tabs per section (Clients, Team, Content Review, Creator Campaigns, Short Links, Services), opened from a link in the top bar. **The section filter is the record's own tab strip** (`.rectabs` > `.tab`, the shape Overview / Contacts take on a client record), asked for by the user on 2026-09-22 in place of the `.acttabs` pill row: one strip, one look, and it scrolls sideways where the sheet is narrower than eight names rather than wrapping. |
| Letter lifecycle: void and delete (`js/documents.js`, `js/crm.js`) | **Voiding and deleting are two authorities and two acts.** `letter_set_void(p_doc, p_reason)` requires **Clients: Manage** (`allowed('clients', 'manage')`; it was its own `can_doc_void` switch until 2026-09-22), applies to a **verified** letter only, takes a reason, records `voided_by` and `void_reason`, and reverts **only the service lines that letter alone was holding confirmed** (`letter_sole_services`: a line another verified, unvoided letter also maps stays confirmed). `letter_delete(p_doc, p_confirm, p_reason)` is gated on the portal's existing `can_remove` (an admin's by default), takes the **serial typed back** and a reason, applies the same safe reversal, and leaves a `client_document_deletions` row carrying the former id, the serial, the actor, the time, the reason and the service ids — and **no document content**. There is **no restore and no un-void**: a void is a recorded reversal, not a switch. There is **no storage side and no quarantine**, because the letter PDF is never stored and there is no signed upload: the row is the letter, so the sheet says the deletion is immediate and cannot be undone. Both are sheets, not `confirm()`, because each needs something typed. Both menu items carry `data-need="clients:manage"` and the database checks the live permission again when the button is pressed, so a permission taken away while the sheet is open is a refusal and not a deletion that already happened. |
| Documents Register (`js/letters.js`, `js/register.js`, `js/verify.js`, `verify/`, `supabase/schema.sql` "THE DOCUMENTS REGISTER") | **One engine, four kinds, one letterhead.** `js/documents.js` exports the pen (`pen()`, the primitives and the letterhead the Letter of Offer draws with, plus `embedFonts`, `embedLogo`, `letterDate`), and `js/letters.js` draws the quotation cover, the letters to clients and the HR letters on it, so the two cannot come out on two different sheets of paper. A row in `documents` is the whole snapshot (recipient, body per language, signatory, languages) and the file is redrawn on Download, never stored. The page follows the Word templates: PRIVATE & CONFIDENTIAL, Our Ref and Date, the recipient (To, address, Attn; for HR the name, the position, `{staff code} / {NRIC}` and TO BE OPENED BY ADDRESSEE ONLY), the title in capitals, the salutation, the body (one block per language with a hairline between, Chinese through `ADSPACE_ORG.fontCjk`, refused **by name** when that face cannot be fetched rather than drawn as boxes, and **embedded whole when the face is CFF based**: pdf-lib subsets a CID-keyed CFF font such as Noto Sans TC with the glyph order wrong, so the first live cover printed `! " # $ %` where its Chinese should be; `isCff()` reads the OTTO tag and turns subsetting off for that face alone, a TrueType face still subsets, and `tests/docs.js` routes a CFF face in and measures the file), the closing, ADSPACE PLT, and for a signed kind a signing space over the signatory's NAME and designation. Every page foot carries the reference, the page count and `This is a computer-generated document. [No signature required. ]Verify document authenticity: go.adspace.me/verify`. `ADSPACE_ORG.fontMed` (`/css/SlateMedium.TTF`, uploaded by the user on 2026-09-18) heads the letters and falls back to Slate Regular wherever the file cannot be fetched. **The serial is the database's to make, per family**: a quotation cover takes the accounting portal's typed (`AQT2607003`); a client letter is `AD/[SA/]{client_code}/{code}` (SA where the client has ever been Active, Paused or Past, or holds a confirmed line); an HR letter is `ADHR/{staff_code}/{code}{YYMM}`; a built one takes `-2`, `-3` where the same base is spent, a typed one is accepted for any family, and `serial_taken()` spans `documents` and `client_documents`, so a serial is never handed out twice **while a document holds it**. **A deletion no longer spends it** (2026-09-20, at the user's request): it used to, and a client whose first two letters were issued in testing and deleted started at `03` for ever. The cost is stated rather than hidden — the deletion row is the only record that an earlier document ever carried the reference — and it arises only for a reference whose document no longer exists. `doc_types` seeds once (quotation cover EN/ZH/MS unsigned; Thank-you letter SC; Letter to client GL; Confirmation of employment E; HR letter GL) and is the team's to edit. **HR is its own part of the ladder** (`register.hr`, beside `register.documents`; it was a section until 2026-09-22): `register_may(family, level)` answers HR to `register.hr` alone and client documents to `register.documents` or `clients.documents`, `documents_read` is that predicate, the Register nav shows for either `view`, and `issue_document` logs an HR letter under the subject `HR` with the kind alone, because the Activity record is read by more people than HR is. **The Register is the one global list, Letters of Offer included** (2026-09-20): `client_documents` is read beside `documents` and mapped into the register's row shape (`asOffer()` in `js/letters.js`, `family: 'offer'`), because the verify page answered a reference the Documents section had never heard of and the team kept two lists in their heads. The two tables stay two tables — a letter of words and a priced snapshot are not one shape — and the register is the view over both. An offer row answers to `clients.documents` alone (`mayFamily`, `needOf`), is read only where that is held so a permission nobody has is never a refused read, downloads through `ADspaceDocs` rather than `ADspaceLetters` because it is redrawn from its snapshot, and carries **Open client record** in place of Void, Delete and Reissue: every act on a letter turns on which service lines it holds, which the register has not loaded and must not guess at. A superseded letter reads `Superseded`. **The Register** (`?s=register`) is one register banded Quotation covers / Letters of Offer / Client letters / HR letters / Other documents, rows Document · Brand · Recipient · Issued · ⋯ (`.reg-row`; the brand is the client's name, the recipient the entity the letter was addressed to, because the two differ; the client record's pane drops Brand, `.reg-row.is-own`, since every row there is that client's; 2026-09-26); **the HR Letters card heads Team in place of Brand** (`teamOf()`, the user, 2026-09-26): the colleague as the Team directory names them, found by the letter's own `member_id` or, for a row imported from the old list, by a segment of the reference that is somebody's Employee ID (`ADHR/AD014/E2601`, `AD004-P2405001`), with the recipient the name the letter was addressed to; a candidate's offer (`ADHR/EMP…`) carries no ID and names nobody; **the client picker on the issue sheet and on Add entry offers every record in Clients**, in the directory's bands (Leads, Clients, Past clients) and order (newest Client ID first), **the ID leading the line** (`AC190 · Newer One`) so the numbers stand in one column, and a pick fills To / Recipient with the registered name, which the person may type over and which the next pick never replaces; **a formal name takes title case** (2026-09-26): the kinds, bands and filter read Quotation Cover, Client Letter, HR Letter, and a kind typed on Add entry is saved and shown through `ADspaceForm.title`, so `service agreement` reads Service Agreement), search on reference, kind and name, a kind filter, `Add entry` (a serial made elsewhere: `register_add`) and `Issue document`, which opens `#docSheet`: the kind decides the fields (client or colleague, To/Address/Attn or Position/NRIC, the language ticks for a cover, the signatory for a signed kind), the type seeds the title, salutation and body with `{first name}` and `{role}` filled, a body somebody has edited is never overwritten by a later select, and the signatory is the signed-in person's name and `team_members.designation`. The client record's Documents pane carries **Issue document** (client kinds only, the client fixed, the main contact on Attn) and lists the client's register rows under its Letters of Offer. Valid is the ordinary case, so there is no state column: `Void` is a chip beside the reference and the row fades; Void and Delete are sheets (`#rvoidSheet`, `#rdelSheet`, the serial typed back; the item reads **Delete**, never `Delete permanently`, because the menu has named the noun and the sheet states there is no restore), behind `register.hr:manage` / `register.documents:manage` (`clients.documents:manage` on the record) and re-checked by `document_set_void` / `document_delete`. **A portal document is corrected by reissuing it** (`Reissue` in its ⋯, Work level, `document_reissue`): the issue sheet opens filled from the version being replaced with the kind, the client or colleague and the reference fixed and everything else editable; on Reissue the earlier version is voided with the reason `Reissued` and kept, and the new version takes **the same serial** and points back at it (`documents.replaces`). The unique index is therefore over the documents that stand (`documents_serial_live_idx`, `where voided_at is null`) while `serial_taken()` still refuses the serial to anything else, a version that already has a successor is refused (`not-current`), and the same press twice is one reissue. On the Register the replaced version's chip reads `Reissued` and it is offered no second Reissue; **the verify page answers the version that stands, Valid, and never says reissued** (`verify_serial` orders the live version first), because the reference on the paper in somebody's hand is the reference of the document that stands. Decided with the user on 2026-09-22. **The reference on a Register row is a copy control** (`.serial-copy`, a button in the row's own face, `ADspaceCopy.to`, named `Copy {serial}`; it clears the control floor with the negative block margin the ⋯ uses so the line keeps its height), because copying the reference into a message or the accounting portal is the everyday act on this list. **A hand-added row's second line is its kind and nothing else**: no `Added by hand`, no issuer, because how a row arrived is not a fact about the document and an import is not a person. **A hand-added row is edited in place** (`Edit` in its ⋯, the same sheet as Add entry with the serial read only, `register_update`, manual rows only: a portal row is a snapshot); **on that sheet an HR entry names a team member and never a client** (`p_member`, `supabase/migrations/2026-09-26-an-hr-entry-names-its-colleague.sql`; the kind decides which picker is drawn, a pick fills Recipient), **and the document type is chosen, not typed** (`#regAddKindPick`: the seeded types and every name that kind's documents carry, title-cased and said once, with Other (new type) opening the field for a name nobody has used; the user, 2026-09-26); its date may be blank (`issued_at` nullable, `supabase/migrations/2026-09-18-register-edit.sql`), because a serial imported from the old list is known before its details are. **The old list was imported by a SQL file handed to the user, never committed**: it names employees and clients, and the repository is public. `team_members.staff_code` (`^[A-Z0-9]{3,8}$`, unique) is the **Employee ID** on screen, the team's own name for it (2026-09-22); it and `designation` are typed on the Team page and read on the row. **The section is named Documents in the nav** (2026-09-22, chosen over Register because it is what the section holds and the client record already calls the same rows Documents; the address stays `?s=register`, the identifiers stay, and the Activity record filters it as Documents with every `document.*` and `register.*` row filed there rather than under Clients, because an HR letter is not a client event). **The kinds are named as the team names them** (`Quotation Cover`, `Thank You Letter`, `Letter to Client`, `Confirmation of Employment`, `HR Letter`; `supabase/migrations/2026-09-22-document-kind-names.sql` renames the seeded rows and the documents that carry them), **the issue sheet runs in the letter's own order** (reference and date, To and address, Attn and designation or position and NRIC, title, salutation, body, signature), and **the Register sorts newest first** with Oldest first and By reference in the bar. **`/verify/`** is the public page every foot names: one field, `verify_serial()` granted to anon, answering an exact reference with the kind, the date and Valid / Void (Replaced for a superseded Letter of Offer only), never the recipient, an HR serial as "HR letter" and a Letter of Offer number too; `?s=` prefills. English and Chinese. **`/verify` and `/verify/` are the same page**: the root `verify.html` (the Jotform form) moved to `drafts/verify-jotform.html` on 2026-09-18 at the user's request, so Pages answers `/verify` with a redirect to the folder; `drafts/` is excluded from the published site in `_config.yml` and is kept in the repository only. The `go.adspace.me/verify` link is repointed by the user. `tests/docs.js` drives all of it and reads the PDFs back with pdf.js; `tests/sql.js` section 20 runs the functions against Postgres. Storage on S3 with expiring public links is batch 2. |
| Letter: the signatory (`issuer_name_ok`) | **A letter is signed by a person, never by a permission.** `issued_by` is `team_members.name`, and a production row named `Superadmin` printed that word under ADSPACE PLT on a client's letterhead. `issue_letter` refuses when the name is blank, contains `@`, or is one of the reserved role words (superadmin, admin, administrator, team member, marketing, sales, account, user, root, owner, staff, system, support, test), and the console says to set a real name on the Team page. No designation field: the name alone signs, under `For and on behalf of ADSPACE PLT`. |
| The rail's foot, the bar's switch, and a dark that is actually dark (`admin/index.html`, `css/portal.css`, `js/admin.js`, 2026-09-21) | **Three things the user asked for in one breath, and a fourth the first of them broke.** **(1) The Activity record is at the foot of the rail**, under a hairline and below the two chunks of four (`.sidebar-foot`). It sat at the end of the console bar with the bell and the account, which are the three controls that act on *you*; it is a **place**, so it is in the rail — and it is not a ninth section, so it is not in the list of them: no route, no selected state, and it opens a record over whatever was on the screen. `.sidebar-nav` gained `min-height: 0` and `overflow-y: auto` with it, or a short screen pushes the new foot off the bottom instead of scrolling, which is the automatic-minimum-size fault `.rectabs` already had. **(2) The theme switch took the slot it left** (`.themeswitch`), icon only like the bell beside it, naming the register it moves *to* the way a light switch does, with the word on `aria-label`; the item in the account menu is gone, because one fact is stated once. **(3) The console starts from the device.** The old rule said light everywhere and never `prefers-color-scheme`, and its whole stated reason was a client deciding on a proposal in a register nobody chose — which is about the client pages, and they are untouched and still carry no dark at all. **Switching to the register the device already shows clears the stored choice** rather than pinning it, so two presses put the console back to following the machine and a two state control carries a three state model with no third press to discover; a `matchMedia` listener follows the device while nothing is stored and is ignored the moment something is. **(4) `.navitem` is a section, not a row shape.** The Activity button borrowed it for its geometry and inherited its wiring: `navItems()` reads every `.navitem` and puts its `data-section` to the ladder, the new row had none, and `level(null)` threw — so the console drew nothing **for everybody except an admin**, because `level()` answers an admin above the line that reads the key. The box is `.railrow` now and the meaning keeps its name; `navItems()` selects `.navitem[data-section]` so the dependency is stated; and `level()` returns 0 for a missing key **above** the admin branch, because a permission check refuses where it cannot answer and never takes the page down. Caught by `tests/setdel.js`'s fourth case, the one that signs in as Marketing — an admin-only fixture would have shipped it. |
| The dark palette is neutral where light is neutral (`css/portal.css`, 2026-09-21) | **The user reported dark as brownish against another dark application, and it measured exactly that.** Every non-neutral token in the dark block sat at **hue 70 to 100 degrees** — yellow-green — while light's warm marks sit at **42**. A faint warm cast on `#f5f5f5` reads as a considered off-white; the same chroma on an olive axis at `#161814` reads as mud. Dark had been written once and never held to light's own documented rule, **the grounds are neutral and the warmth is on the marks**. So `--page` `#171717` and `--card` `#1f1f1f` are exactly neutral, as `#f5f5f5` and white are (`--sunk` and `--line-soft` already were), and the ink ramp and borders keep their warmth **on light's axis** rather than a second one: `--ink` `#f2efec`, `--ink-soft` `#cdc8c3`, `--ink-mute` `#ada8a3`, `--line` `#3e3b38`, `--line-ctl` `#898480`, `--fill` / `--on-fill` and `--chrome` following. **Every replacement holds the luminance of the value it replaced**, so no documented contrast pair moves — `--ink` on `--page` 15.60 to 15.65, `--line-ctl` on `--card` 4.39 to 4.46 — and `uxaudit` measures all of them on the real page in this theme. The standing lesson: a palette written in two places is two palettes, and a rule is only true where nothing later disagrees. |
| The account control has no outline (`css/portal.css` `.acct-btn`, 2026-09-20) | **It was the last outlined thing in the console bar.** Beside an Activity link and a bell that carry nothing but ink and a fill on hover, the person's own name in a `--line-ctl` ring read as the one action up there — which is the fault the bell's own ring had, reported on 2026-09-22 and corrected the same way. The border is gone in both shapes (the disc when only an initial fits, the pill when the name does), and what says it is pressable is the `--line-soft` fill on hover and while the menu is open. Asked for by the user on 2026-09-20 against a phone screenshot. |
| The serial fills the gap a deletion left (`supabase/migrations/2026-09-20-the-serial-fills-the-gap.sql`) | **A deleted reference came back only if somebody typed it.** `serial_taken()` stopped counting deletions earlier the same day, but the automatic path still used `client_document_seq.next_val` outright, so the client whose first two letters were issued in testing and deleted went on starting at 03 — the complaint that began the whole thread. `issue_letter` builds the number from the **lowest free slot** in that client and month instead. The counter is kept and still advances for one reason: its upsert takes the row lock, so a second issuer blocks there until the first has committed its letter and the two can never read the same gap as free. It is the bound the scan runs within, not the number. **What is deliberately unchanged**: a typed reference still spends no sequence number and is still refused where a standing document holds it; `serial_taken()` is untouched, so a voided, superseded or refused letter still holds its own reference and only a deletion frees one; nothing renumbers a letter already issued. **The cost, stated**: the counter stops meaning "how many letters this client was issued this month" and becomes the bound on the search, and where somebody has typed references over the same range the scan can run past it, capped, refusing with `no-serial` rather than looping. `tests/sql.js` drives the gap being filled, the counter still climbing, the standing letter's reference still refused, and the hundredth letter of a month widening — that last one had to be rewritten to spend the ninety-nine slots below it for real, because the old test had simply set the counter to 100. It also compares the migration's `issue_letter` against the schema's, bounded at `end $$;`. |
| Documents (`js/documents.js`) | The Letter of Offer (kind `offer`; earlier rows `intent`, `cover`), issued by sales to the client from the record, numbered `AQT/INT/YYMMXXX` (sequence per month, file name swaps `/` for `-`), drawn in the browser with pdf-lib on the ADspace letterhead (the reference is the End Thank You Letter): wordmark in Optima, Co. Reg. and address left, the monogram and phone, email, website right; PRIVATE & CONFIDENTIAL; Our Ref / Date (12th September 2026) / To (registered name in capitals) / Attn (billing contact, role); the subject LETTER OF OFFER; Dear {contact}; an opening paragraph; the **To quote lines only** (enquired lines are not priced yet, confirmed lines are past this step) as Description / Rate / Amount, each line carrying its inclusions from `detail` then its pricing basis, period and note, with `per month` mute under the Amount; priced by the month when every line shares a term, so Subtotal, SST 8% and the bold **Payable monthly** are all one month's invoice, and the whole commitment is disclosed as a sentence in the TERMS block naming the term and the figure (a one off or mixed letter reads Total instead and carries no term sentence); the TERMS block holds that, the billing basis and the validity (30 days); `priceOf()` computes the stored snapshot and the drawing alike, and the stored `subtotal`/`tax`/`total` remain the whole commitment for the record and the pipeline; Yours sincerely, ADSPACE PLT, the signed-in person's team name; an acceptance block the client signs (Signature and company stamp, Name, Designation, Date), kept on one page with the closing and with the sentence naming the reference, the date, the page count and the figure being accepted; the reference in every page foot and an initials line on every page but the signed one, so a first page cannot be swapped after execution; Page x of y on every page (the monogram is the letterhead's, top right only; the foot's copy was dropped on 2026-09-25). Once the client signs, the formal quotation and invoice are issued outside the portal. Stored as issued in `client_documents`, Void then Delete. **The reference is typable** (2026-09-20, `p_serial` on `issue_letter`): blank is the everyday case and the database numbers the letter, a typed one is refused where a document still holds it (`serial-taken`) or where it is not 3 to 40 of `[A-Za-z0-9/._-]` (`serial-shape`), and **typing one spends no sequence number**, so filling a gap a deleted letter left never costs the next letter its place. | Client portal (`client/`, `js/portal.js`) | One sign-in by email link (`signInWithOtp`, redirect `/client/`; not BRN and name, no typed code), one client on the page. Access is one switch on a contact (`client_contacts.portal_access`), set from the contact's ⋯ as **Enable portal access** / **Revoke portal access** with Undo, and marked on the row by a green `Portal access` chip because a sign-in is a live state. Enabling opens a sheet naming the person and the sign-in address with **Send invitation email** **unticked**: access is the decision, an email is a separate one, and the client can sign in without ever getting one. **Send invitation** stays in the ⋯ (`invite-member`, `notify: true`) for when they ask. Undo on a revoke emails nobody. **Access alone is enough to sign in**: the `portal-login` edge function (Verify JWT off, unauthenticated by design) makes the login on the way in, for an address that is a live contact with `portal_access` and for no other, so nothing depends on a call the console made days ago. Sign-ups stay closed on the project, which is what stops the page minting accounts; `signInWithOtp` carries `shouldCreateUser: false` and `portal-login` is what puts the account there first. **One address can hold access at several clients** (`portal_clients()` returns every one), so a group of companies is one login with a company select on the page. The page reads and writes only through `get_portal`, `portal_request` and `portal_withdraw` (security definer, keyed on the signed-in email); a client account never touches a table. Sections in order: Overview (registered name, registration no., billing address, market, account manager, status chip; contacts read-only; one action Request change), Services (confirmed and quoted lines with chips and totals; enquired lines never shown; Upgrade / Downgrade / Cancel in the ⋯ of a confirmed line), Requests (appears once one exists; Withdraw while Requested, with Undo), Letters (Download redraws the snapshot), Engagements (Content Review and each campaign opened by their own token links; hidden when there is nothing), Payment (only once `ADSPACE_ORG.bank` is set), **Portal access** (Person · Sign-in email: the client's own people who hold a sign-in, named for that rather than "Account", which read as the viewer's own settings). English and Chinese. Covers: Client sign-in / Check your email / Access denied / Unable to load. |
| The console while it is loading (`js/admin.js`, `.console.is-booting`) | **Signed in is not allowed in, and the in-between is still the console's shell.** The console stays hidden until `me()` answers, because its rail names every section of the tool — but what stood in its place was the shared page bar over an empty page, which is the composition `/client/`, `/creators/` and `/review/` use, so a refresh read for a second or two as somebody else's portal (reported 2026-09-22). The shell, the rail and the bar are the console's own from the first paint; the nav's names, the bar's controls and the route bodies are not drawn (`visibility: hidden`, `display: none`), and `#consoleBoot` is a skeleton of what is coming. `applyAccess()` takes the class off, and a refused team row still falls through to the Access denied cover. Nothing about what this person may open is on the screen before the database has said it. |
| Client portal shape (`client/`, `js/portal.js`) | **The console record's head, cell for cell.** It was `.crm-title` with the meta as a grid sibling, so the registered name and the account manager printed in the third column — to the right of a filled button — instead of under the name they belong to; on a phone the state and a 150px action took the row and the name was squeezed to sixty pixels. It is `.rec-mark` + `.rec-who` (name, then meta) + `.rec-ctl` (state, the company select, Request change), and below 640 it takes the console's own two-row template (`"mark . ctl" / "who who who"`) keyed on the **window**, because here the head's width is the window's. **One heading size on the page**: the rail headed its blocks at 15px and the pane headed its sections at 16px, side by side. **An empty section is `ADspaceState.emptyLine`**, not this page's own `.empty`, which is a bounded card — a client with no services, no letters and no requests read three white slabs down the page. **A sign-in address is read, not written to** (`.acc-email`), so Portal access no longer draws a mailto pill in a table cell. **A lone reach link is not a banner** (`.is-narrow .ct-row .crm-reach .plink:only-child`): Engagements borrows the contact row for a name and a single Open, and the equal-widths rule that three reach links need made that one button a slab across the phone. The head's step and the step between sections are 24px, on the scale, not 20 and 28. |
| Console: Requests (`js/crm.js`) | On the client record after Documents, shown once a contact has portal access or a request exists: Request · Fee · State · ⋯; states Requested → Reviewing → Approved or Declined → Applied (select, tinted; Withdrawn as a chip); Reply in the ⋯ sets a fee and a reply the client reads; every change logged with who decided. Approval never changes a service line; a person applies it. |
| Team-only policies (`is_team()`) | Clients have logins too, so `authenticated` no longer means the team: `team_members`, `team_roles`, `services`, `activity_viewers` reads, `activity_log` writes, the `content` storage bucket, `delete_client` and both edge functions (`sign-upload`, `invite-member`) check an active team row. The team list is never derived from `auth.users`: the cutover sweep runs only while `team_members` is empty and skips any address recorded as a client contact, and the repair at the foot of the schema stands down a contact the old sweep let through. **One address is never both**: `no_team_client_overlap` refuses portal access to an active team address and refuses the team to a live portal contact (on insert, and on the update that would move a row into the overlap, so a legacy row stays editable), and `portal_clients()` returns nothing for a team address, so even a legacy overlap cannot open both. |
| Money (`js/money.js`) | The only formatter, and the only place a price is adjusted. RM for MY, S$ for SG; SST 8% unless `sst_applies` is false; two decimals on every total. `TERMS` holds the term factors (3 months `1/0.9`, 6 the baseline, 12 `×0.95`, 24 `×0.9`); `rateFor()` gives the rate a line is billed at, rounded to the cent where it is charged, and `termWord()` names the adjustment. The console, the letter and the client portal all read it, so they cannot drift. |
| Onboarding the team (`js/admin.js` `INTRO`, `.routeintro`, `.routenote`) | **Each console route says what it is for, once, and then gets out of the way.** The user asked for it on 2026-09-22: the portal is opening to the whole team and most of them do not know what the sections are. One line per route under its command bar, in official register (`Client records, from first enquiry to active engagement. …`), 13.5px in `--ink-soft` with **Hide** at its end, in the instruction pattern the creator page and the draft step already use (`adspace-hint-intro-{section}` in `localStorage`, open the first three entries, retired after that or on Hide, brought back by the `?` control in the command bar, `.cmdbar-help`, a 32px `.btn-sm`, drawn inside `.cmdbar-end` directly after the count). It was first drawn as the 18px `?` ring with 12.5px mute text against the table's edge, which the user sent back the same day as badly sized and spaced; it was then left as a loose item in a wrapping bar, which is the fault below. **The command bar's tail is one element** (`.cmdbar-end`, holding the count, the `?` and the route's one or two actions): loose in the bar they wrapped by whatever was left over, so every route composed differently — the count and the `?` mid-row on Services with the action stranded underneath, four things on Documents' last line, My Work's action alone at the left margin of a fourth row — which the user reported on 2026-09-22 as "everything being positioned everywhere". On a phone the bar is three stated rows: the search, the filters two to a row at equal halves, and the end group at `flex-basis: 100%` with the count and the `?` left and the actions right. An empty count is not drawn (`:empty`), or it spends the group's gap indenting the `?` beside it. A fact that stands every day is a `.routenote` under the register instead: the Register names the public verify page as a link (`/verify/`, printed as `{host}/verify`), and Short Links names the host links redirect from (`ADSPACE_CONFIG.linkHost`) and that codes printed with `go.adspace.me` keep working. The Team panel folds each section's parts under that section's own head line (`.permsec-toggle`, the name is the fold), opening a section that holds an exception; the shape came from the accounting portal the user showed, and its level codes and per-state ticks were deliberately not taken. |
| The command bar on a phone (`js/cmdbar.js`, 2026-09-22) | **One row, and the filters behind one button.** At 390 the bar was the search, the view segment, two rows of selects, and the count with the action: four rows and 430px before the first record on a 700px screen, which the user sent back ("three to five rows above before viewing my tasks"). Below 640 the bar is **two marks on the left and what the list holds on the right**: a **search** mark, a **Filters** mark, then the count and the primary action, with the view segment as a second row where a route has one. **Search is a mark that grows into the field** (`.cmdbar-search`, the `.namebox` move the client's Approve makes: `max-width` 0 to 100% at `--t-slow`, everything else in the row collapsing the same way rather than by `display: none`), because it is the widest control in the bar and is used after somebody has scanned, not before; it shuts again when it is left empty, stays open while it holds a value, and the mark carries the ink edge while it is shut, because a filtered list has to say why on the screen. The open field takes a **whole-row basis**: at `auto` it is the input's own 100% plus its padding and wrapped itself onto a second line, and at 0 the view segment joined the collapsed row and took all 358px of it. The collapsed right group is `flex-wrap: nowrap`, or squeezed to nothing it wraps inside itself and the bar stands 73px. The selects come up in a sheet from the floor (`#cmdSheet`, the portal's own `.sheet`), each select under a label taken from its own accessible name, Done and Clear at the foot. **Nothing is duplicated**: the selects are the same elements with the same listeners, moved into the sheet on open and put back into their slots on close, so the page repaints exactly as it does from the bar, and Clear sets each to its default (`data-default`, else its first option) and fires `change`. **The badge counts the filters off their default**; `data-nofilter` marks a select that is not a filter and is neither counted nor cleared (`#workWf`, the board's axis; `#workScope`, because whose work is a view and Clear leaves it alone on the desk too), and a hidden select is never counted. **The primary keeps its fill and gives up its word** (`.btn-word` hidden, the word kept as `aria-label`); **a second action goes behind a ⋯** beside it (`.cmd-more`, each item pressing the real button, placed by `ADspaceMenu`), so Documents and Short Links are one row too; a bar with no selects draws no Filters button and a lone non-primary action (Manage clients) keeps its word. **The two icon controls are one shape** (card ground, `--line-ctl` edge, `--radius-ctl`), because the ⋯ first drew in the row menu's borderless shape beside an outlined Filters. `.iconbtn:hover` moved inside `@media (hover: hover)` for it, which the law had asked for all along. Above 640 nothing draws and the bar is what it was. `tests/cmdbar.js` drives My Work, Clients, Content Review and Documents at 390 and the desk at 1280; the walk carries the open sheet and the open ⋯; the matrix drives the sheet from the keyboard. |
| The section's name is what explains the section (`.console-title`, 2026-09-22) | **The `?` is gone from the command bar and the route's name in the console head is the control.** It sat in `.cmdbar-quiet` with the count, among the list's own controls, and it is not about the list; the user asked for it beside the section title on 2026-09-22. `.console-title` is a `<button>` carrying the route's word and a 14px info glyph, `aria-expanded` on it, `aria-label` `{Section}, about this section` set by `showSection`, and one handler in `js/admin.js` that opens whichever route's `.routeintro` is on the screen. **The glyph costs 14px beside the word and not a 44px mark of its own**, which is what lets it survive a head that already holds four controls; **below 400 the glyph gives way and the name never does** (`.console-head` drops to an 8px gap there, or "My Work" clips to "My W…" in the 52px the 12px gap leaves). **Only the glyph answers**: a fill on the open state made the route's name a selected chip on every screen the line is open on, which is the first three visits to every route. `.cmdbar-help` is retired. |
| The console installs as an app (`admin/manifest.webmanifest`, `admin/sw.js`, `admin/offline.html`, `admin/icons/`, 2026-09-25) | **A PWA for the console only, and a worker that caches nothing but its own offline page.** Asked for on 2026-09-14 and set aside then ("skip pwa"); the user raised it again on 2026-09-25. The manifest's scope and start are `/admin/`, so the client pages, the creator page and the user's root site are never under it and never carry a manifest. **The worker is not a cache of the portal**: every script and stylesheet carries a `?v=` stamp, and a worker that served them from its own store would serve yesterday's console after a release, which is the risk named when this was first proposed. So every request goes to the network as it always did, and only a page load that cannot reach the network is answered, with `/admin/offline.html` (`No connection` / `Check the connection, then try again.` / **Try again**), the portal's cover drawn from its own inline tokens because `css/portal.css` is not stored. The store holds that page and the wordmark and nothing else. **The icons are the ADspace wordmark** in Optima on white (the user, 2026-09-25: "we're now all using wordmark"), not the monogram; the maskable icon keeps the wordmark inside the safe zone; the home screen icon is the console's own file, so an install never depends on the CDN. The worker registers from `js/admin.js` on load; a failed registration is silent and changes nothing. `tests/pwa.js` runs its own server and stops it, because Playwright's offline switch reaches the page and not the worker's own requests. |
| Phone edges, refresh and sign-in (`js/refresh.js`, `js/captcha.js`, `js/form.js`, `docs/SIGN-IN-SECURITY.md`, 2026-09-26) | **From the user's brief "UX, PWA and Login Security Fixes", batch 1 only; desktop unchanged.** Every portal page carries `viewport-fit=cover` (without it `env(safe-area-inset-*)` read 0 and a sheet's Save sat on the iPhone home bar); the sheet, the foot, the confirm bar, the console body and the rail pad by the inset, and the bars take the side insets in landscape. **My Work's view strip scrolls sideways on a phone** (every view the same 14px padding and its whole word; the edge with more beyond it fades by a mask, `is-more-start` / `is-more-end`; a choice scrolls the chosen view into sight; the sliding thumb adds the track's scroll offset). **My Work, Clients uses the console's own command bar** (`.cwbar`: search mark, Filters, count, New month behind the ⋯, New task), never the old full-width filter row, which the user reported as the pre-revamp bar; a Filters button over selects that are all hidden is not drawn. **New report**: Month and Custom period are never both live (opening Custom disables Month, shutting it clears Start and End), End's `min` is Start, an empty date reads `Select date` (`data-hint`, `ADspaceForm.hint`, `.datefield`), and month fields share the date field's height, left alignment and glyph. **Refresh app** in the account menu unregisters the worker and empties Cache Storage, never localStorage, IndexedDB or the sign-in; **pull to refresh** runs only in the installed app, only on a list (no record in the address, no sheet or menu open), calls `registration.update()` and reloads. Money fields take `inputmode="decimal"`, counts `numeric`. **Sign-in never says whether an address has an account**: `/admin/` and `/client/` both read "If {email} is registered, …", `portal-login` answers `{ ok: true }` to every address, the console asks `shouldCreateUser: false`, and a rate limit is named in our words. A Turnstile captcha is wired to all three emailed sign-ins; the site key is set in `ADSPACE_CONFIG.turnstileSiteKey` (2026-09-26), a token rides every sign-in call, and where Cloudflare cannot be reached the email still goes without one. Supabase enforces it only once its CAPTCHA switch is on; the secret lives only in Supabase. **The Cloudflare box sits under the row it belongs to, once a card, in the page's register** (2026-09-26, reported with two photos): `hostFor()` puts it after the row where the button shares one with its field (the client's email and Send link, which it had squeezed to a sliver), a card with two senders carries `data-cf` so both share one box as its last item (the console's Sign in and passkey had stacked two), and `theme` is the page's own (`data-theme`), never the device's, so a client page is never drawn black. An autofilled field keeps `--card` (an inset shadow over Safari's yellow and Chrome's blue). `tests/phone.js`, `tests/signin.js`. |
| Cancel on the left, the action at the right edge; required fields marked (`css/portal.css`, `js/form.js`, 2026-09-26) | **Supersedes primary-first, anchored left (2026-09-20/21).** The user pressed Cancel while creating something: the hand leaving the last field moves right and Cancel stood right of Create. Every action row now reads Cancel / secondary / Save from the left, anchored to the right edge (`.sheet-foot`, `.changebox-actions`, `.zone-actions`, `.qform-acts`, `.filearm-acts`, `.row.acts`: `flex-direction: row-reverse`), and on a phone Cancel is the left half and the action the right. The markup keeps the primary first, so Tab and Cmd + Enter still reach it first; a count in the foot follows the pair to its left, and on a phone sits above it. A plain `.row` holding buttons takes `acts`. **Required fields carry a red asterisk**: the field states `aria-required="true"` (what a screen reader announces) and `js/form.js` marks its label `.is-req`, so the mark and the rule the save checks are one fact; the asterisk itself has no spoken text. `uxaudit`'s `order` rule and `tests/geom.js` measure the new side by x; `tests/phone.js` the lead sheet's order and marks at 390 and 1280. |
| A contact reached by WhatsApp username (`js/crm.js` `waUser` / `waHandle` / `waLink`, 2026-09-26) | **WhatsApp lets a person hide their number, so a contact may be reachable by a username only** (the user: "for username it will now be wa.me/@username the @ needs to prefill"). The contact sheet and the lead intake carry **WhatsApp username** beside **Phone**, the `@` printed in front of the field (`.slugfield`); a typed `@` or a pasted `https://wa.me/@name` is taken whole, and anything but letters, numbers, full stops and underscores is refused by name. **No column was added**: `client_contacts.whatsapp` holds `@name` for a username and the number otherwise, so the WhatsApp button opens `wa.me/@name` for one and `wa.me/60…` for the other, and the Overview names the username on a WhatsApp row. The client portal still builds its WhatsApp link from the phone (`get_portal` sends no `whatsapp`), so a username-only contact shows no WhatsApp button there. `tests/crm.js` drives the prefix, the refusal, the pasted link, both links and the edit. |
| A campaign's internal timing (`js/campaigns.js` `paintCampTiming`, `supabase/migrations/2026-09-26-campaign-clock.sql`, 2026-09-26) | **Under Key dates, a Timing block says how long the campaign sat in each stage** (the user: "from created until confirm until in progress, until completed each stage total hours"): Setting up (created to published), Client selection (published to the client's confirmation), Confirming creators (confirmation to production), In production (to the last booking completed), and the Total, each as hours under two days and days and hours after, the running stage reading `so far`. **Read off what the database stamps, never stored by the page**: `campaigns.state_log` (every state move, a trigger), `campaign_confirmations.created_at`, each booking's `confirmed_at` and `completed_at` (a trigger stamps it on Completed and clears it on a revert). A stage the record cannot date is left out, never guessed: campaigns from before the clock have no log of when they were published. Team-only, like the rest of the rail. `tests/camptime.js` and `tests/sql.js`. |
| No date before the company existed (`js/form.js` §5, `js/ask.js`, 2026-09-26) | **Every date, month and date-time field in the console starts on 14 Aug 2023**, the day ADSPACE PLT was registered, and ends on 31 Dec 2099 (the user: "it is possible to key in wrongly like 0003 for years"). `js/form.js` sets the floor as `min` on every such field it scans and on any field reached later (the Timeline's in-place pickers, a sheet a script built), raises a lower `min` and leaves a later one (a meeting from today, End after Start), clears a value outside the range when the field is left and refuses it on Enter, with one line under the field (`.date-note`, `Choose a date from 14 Aug 2023.`); `ADspaceAsk.rename` refuses to save such a date. A field that genuinely reaches further back (a date of birth; none exists today) carries `data-any-date`. Page-side only: the database takes what it is sent. `tests/datefloor.js`. |
