# Part 3. New entries (from 2026-09-26)

- **2026-09-26 — The instruction files were slimmed.** They were about 160,000 tokens and re-sent on every turn. The rules stay in `CLAUDE.md`, `DESIGN.md` and `STANDARD.md`; the history moved here and to `docs/DESIGN-NOTES.md`. Each fact now lives in one loaded file. The test gate became tiered. Asked for by the user.
- **2026-09-26 — Moved here from `STANDARD.md` §6**, where it had been written by mistake (it is history, not the general standard):
  **2026-09-16** — Two acts over a letter, Void and Delete, both re-checked server-side on every call through `allowed()`; no parallel permission system and no hard-coded role check. **Superseded on 2026-09-22 for the authority**: both are the Clients section's Manage level. `can_doc_void` was a separate switch and the user asked for it folded in, because the person trusted to delete a client's letter is the person trusted to void one, and two switches for one level of trust left groups with Manage on Clients and no void. The columns stay, unread; `supabase/migrations/2026-09-22-void-is-clients-manage.sql` redefines the two functions.
- **2026-09-26 — One gate command.** `tests/gate.sh` runs the named suites (or `all`) with the browser suites three at a time and the Postgres suites in their own lane, then uxaudit and matrix, and ends on one `gate:` line. It exists because a sweep watched suite by suite woke the session dozens of times, and a wait written as `until … ! pgrep -f sweep1.sh` never ended: the loop's own command line contained the pattern. It hung for three hours and forty minutes before it was noticed.
- **2026-09-26 — The Cloudflare Pages project `adspace-form` is deleted.** It built a preview of every push (728 deployments since July 2025) for a site served by GitHub Pages, and nothing pointed at it: no custom domain, no DNS record (`digital.adspace.me` is a CNAME to `adspaceplt.github.io`). The user chose to delete it. Cloudflare refuses to delete a project with that many deployments, so the builds were switched off, the deployments deleted through the API (all but the active production one, which goes with the project), then the project. The `adspace-links` Worker (`hi.adspace.me`) is separate and untouched. PRs no longer carry a Cloudflare check.
- **2026-09-26 — Every picker leads with its code, A to Z.** The HR colleague picker read `Xue Yi · AD014` and the client pickers outside Documents carried no ID at all, and Documents ran newest first. The user asked for the code first, "so it's easy to read and find", and for the sequence A to Z. `ADspaceForm.named(code, name)` and `sequence(key)` (`byClient`, `byStaff`) are now the one label and the one order for every client or colleague select in the console: Documents (client, colleague, Add entry), My Work (every Task Owner picker, the step hand-off, templates, the month's manager and meeting owner, the client and engagement pickers, the Clients view, the owner filter, bulk assign), Creator Campaigns (client, Person in charge), Clients (Person in charge and its filter), Reports (New report), and Team's stand-down reassign. Typing a code in a native select now finds the line. The Clients directory keeps newest first, which is a list read by recency, not a picker. The client portal's company select is left alone: the Client ID is the team's number. Verified by `docs: ok` and the gate.
- **2026-09-26 — A calendar always opens inside the window.** The performance review's Review by field is the last field of its sheet, so on a laptop its calendar opened past the window's foot, under the Dock, and could not be reached. Every date, month and time field at a desk is now lifted before its calendar opens until 340px (a calendar's height) sits under it: the field's own scroller scrolls, and where it has nothing left to scroll a `.pick-room` spacer is added at its end and removed when the field is left. A mouse press that had to lift the field opens the calendar itself (`showPicker`), because the field moved out from under the pointer; a field with room under it is left to the browser; a phone's own picker is untouched. Fixed once in `js/form.js` rather than per sheet, since any sheet ending on a date has the same shape. Verified by `datefloor: ok`.
- **2026-09-26 — The performance record is for keeping, not for signing.** The printed monthly record lost its footer's `Version n` and its seventh section (Acknowledgement, two signature lines and the sentence under them): the user wants the PDF for the record and for reading at the review, and acknowledgement given digitally in the portal, which the Acknowledge step already records. A plain internal-document head without the letterhead was drawn and withdrawn the same hour at the user's word; the letterhead stays. Verified by `perfui: ok`, which reads the file back with pdf.js.
- **2026-09-26 — Performance records are stamped, listed and verifiable; the review list is chosen.** At the user's direction: the monthly record's download is filed before the file is drawn and the file prints a Record of this document (released, acknowledged, finalised, downloaded; name, email, server time in MYT; Document ID) and names the downloader on every foot, "since performance is strictly confidential and proper procedures are required"; a downloaded record is listed under HR Letters for holders of both HR Letters and Performance and answered by `/verify/` (the user typed the file name's dashed form, so every reference is now found either way); History says Downloaded and names each scorecard a save changed; the Activity record gained Performance after Team (steps only, no scores, no master code, the user's choice) and its tabs became My Work's sliding view strip; My performance unlocks with a passkey (Face ID, Touch ID) with the emailed code beside it; and the review list is chosen rather than everyone, with the people the old default reviewed written onto it once. Also: Add task shuts on save and marks the new row (it stayed open and empty and the list looked unchanged); Report no longer leaves Workload on the screen; the QC sheet has Tick all; the Schedule fields read First draft, Final due, Scheduled publish. **To run by hand**: `supabase/migrations/2026-09-26-performance-records-trail-and-check.sql`. Verified by `perf: ok` (Postgres), `perfui`, `perfguard`, `docs`, `qc`, `work` and the gate.
- **2026-09-26 — My Work stages in one order.** The user: the stage listing "keeps jumping around", Planning and the content meeting are the client's month and not a task's, Repeat and Duplicate read as one thing, and the ⋯ had grown to eleven items. Every task now carries one select (row, board card, task head) listing its whole workflow in the order the work runs, the current stage chosen and an unreachable one greyed rather than left out; going back, skipping ahead, cancelling and reopening ask Why? under the control. The ⋯ is Open full record, Change Task Owner, Make a copy, Repeat on a schedule, Delete; Revert, Move to another stage, Mark blocked (On hold replaces it), Cancel task and the timer left it. One next-step button, named "Move to X". Migration `2026-09-26-my-work-stages-in-order.sql` retires Planning, Content meeting scheduled, Changes requested and Published (`retired`), renumbers the content workflow in two passes because (workflow, position) is unique, opens On hold and Cancelled from every open stage, moves open Planning and meeting tasks to Ready to start with an event, and lets `ops_transition_task` go back with a reason. Verified by `ops: ok` and `work: ok`.
- **2026-09-26 — Workload is the Report's first section.** Workload and Report read as the same screen. Report now opens on Open work by person for `ops.all`, then the figures for `ops.reports`; either opens the view, and `view=load` lands on it. Time records dropped "In this stage" and "Since created", which restated the table's current row and total; the table runs in workflow order with Visits always shown and a Total row.
- **2026-09-26 — The Filters sheet focuses its card.** On a phone the Filters sheet focused its first select: the ring stayed blue and iOS does not open a select that already has focus, so the first tap did nothing until another field was tapped. The sheet now focuses its card, as every other sheet does. Verified by `cmdbar: ok`.
- **2026-09-26 — Records read as one line each.** The user: the histories were "too brief", "too airy", and "all terms used are different" across the Activity record, a client's Activity, Recent activity and a task's history. They are now one component, `js/records.js`: one 12.5px line an entry reading time, who, what, on what and what changed, under a heading a day. A run of the same act by one person on one thing within ten minutes folds into one line with its details in the order made and a count; Performance, HR, voids, deletes, removals, billing, rates, invoices, access and group changes never fold. A task's Created line carries its first Task Owner, and a completion or cancellation is the line of its move. The line parts are `rl-*` because `.rec-who` already named the record head's name block, which broke each entry onto two lines. The old tables (`.act`, `.log-row`, `.tact-row`, `.raillog`) went with their styles; the stand-in now files a task's first owner as the database does. Verified by `crm: ok` and `work: ok`.
- **2026-09-26 — Every save records what it changed.** The user asked that a record say what changed, with the example "Edited ADHR/AD021/AOR: type Acceptance of Resignation → Offer Letter; date not set → 12 Sept 2026". One helper (`ADspaceRecords.changes`) writes "Label: old → new" for each field a save moved, and a save that moved nothing files nothing. Client details, brand, contacts, rate lines, team members and user groups name their values; access changes read section by section (`Clients: View → Work`). Billing names the fields only, because the Activity record is read by people who may not hold `clients.billing`. A Documents edit is filed by the database (`register_update`, migration `2026-09-26-document-edits-name-their-changes.sql`); an HR row keeps its rule and is filed under `HR` with neither serial nor colleague, naming its type, date, note and file only, which is the example's content without the reference that identifies the person. Verified by `sql: ok`, `crm: ok` and `team: ok`.

- **2026-09-26 — Layout consistency, measured.** The user approved a measured pass over every console screen. `MEASURE=1 node tests/uxaudit.js tests` writes each heading, panel, table head, label, value and control's computed size, weight, colour, padding and margin across the walk to `tests/measure.jsonl` (not committed), and the 1280 rows were grouped by component to find the outliers. Fixed at the component: the task's next-step title and the lock card's title were 17px against the queue's 16 for the same kind of title (both 16 now); the creator portal's name was 21px against 19 for every other record name; the Short Links and Performance tables' header rows took their rows' 10/10 pad instead of every other table's 12/8; the labels beside values read in three sizes and two inks (13.5 mute in the Overview, 13 mute in a task, 13 soft in the rail) and are 13 mute everywhere; the row gaps in those lists were 6, 7, 9 and 10 and are 8; a rail title stood 10 above its block against the 8 of a card title and a read group's heading 10 against the sheet's 12. The Billing and Brand read groups keep their 190px label track, because their labels are longer than a contact's (recorded with the rule). Verified by the full gate.
- **2026-09-26 — The month card on a phone.** The user sent an iPhone screenshot of a month in My Work's Clients view ("The spacing layout here is no good"). The card repeated its heading's month as its own title and its heading's Ready chip beside its own Ready select; Change stood alone on a line under the meeting, which had wrapped; the link and the message rows each sat on their own margin and gap. The card now starts at its facts and controls, the heading's chip hides while the card is open (`.eng-mark`), and the meeting, link and message are one grid of label, value and controls (`.eng-row`) 8px apart, which on a tight card puts the label and its buttons on one line and the value full width under them. The same screenshot read "25 Sep": Safari's en-GB short month is "Sep" where the portal writes "Sept", so every English short-month format now maps it, and My Work's own date helpers build the month from a fixed list.
- **2026-09-27 — A passkey proof never signs anybody else in.** The user signed in with a test account by email code, opened My performance, pressed Unlock with passkey, and Touch ID offered the work account's passkey stored on the same Mac; once verified the console carried on as the work account. The unlock called Supabase's passkey sign-in, which takes whichever passkey the browser offers (the passkeys are discoverable, so every account's on the device is offered) and replaces the session with that account's before it returns. Worse, `gate()` ignored a second sign-in once the team row was loaded, so the console kept the test account's access map, lists and menus over a session the database read as the work account. Nothing was reached that the passkey's owner could not already reach (the server's lock reads the proof in the session's own token, so the work account's reviews opened, never the test account's), but the attribution of anything done next was wrong. Three rules now: the passkey is offered only to a person who holds one of their own (`ADspacePasskey.mine`); a proof runs through `ADspacePasskey.prove`, which holds the console's auth events, revokes another account's answer on this device only (`signOut({ scope: 'local' })`, so its owner stays signed in elsewhere) and puts the person's own session back (`setSession`), or signs out where it cannot; and a console whose session turns into somebody else's restarts from the top. Supabase's passkey list carries no credential id, so the browser cannot be told to offer only the person's own passkey; the check after the ceremony is what holds. The stand-in lists a person's own passkeys only, keeps sessions by token for `setSession`, and revokes on a local sign-out. Also noted, not changed: the console's Sign out uses Supabase's default scope, which signs the person out on every device.
- **2026-09-27 — One strip, a swipe, the Report's periods, and records that fold only on the same thing.** The user sent four phone screenshots. A campaign record's rail sat hard against its tabs: the last block above the tabs gave up its rule because it was also the last block of all (a campaign has nothing after the pane); on a phone it keeps its rule and step. The record's underlined tabs and a route's tab row now take the view strip's look the user pointed to in the Activity record (the tonal track at its own width, the white thumb that slides, faded edges where it scrolls): two looks for one control were one too many, and on a phone the raised thumb is easier to find than a 2px line. A sideways swipe presses the tab beside the chosen one on every strip (`js/swipe.js`), with the guards that keep a swipe from being something else: the system's edge gesture, anything that scrolls sideways, fields, drags, an open menu, a sheet, and anything not plainly sideways. The Report's period line lost its space because `.routenote`'s own margin came later in the stylesheet; the periods are now This week, This month, Last month, Last 7 days, Last 3 months, Last 6 months and This year, Last month ending where this month begins. The records: three different references with no client name folded into one line with ×3, because the fold compared subjects and an empty subject matched another; a change and its undo read as two contradictory changes; ×2 was not a word; and pre-26 September entries that list a whole access map ran six lines on a phone. A line now folds only on the same thing (a document's reference; the record a pane belongs to), a field changed twice reads as its path, a run reads "· 2 times", a long line shows three lines and opens on a press, and register entries read Document added / Document edited. Older entries stay as they were written: the record is an audit trail and is never rewritten.
- **2026-09-27 — The foot stands on the page.** The user sent two iPhone screenshots (the console's sign-in and a Content Review page): "Footer not optimized, spacings and color". The foot was a white band with a rule above it, so on the grey client pages it was a white stripe between grey content and the grey a phone shows under the document; and its bottom pad was 16px plus the home bar's inset, which set the two words high in the band on an iPhone. The foot now takes the page's own ground under its rule, and its bottom pad is the larger of 16px and the inset. `tests/chrome.js` asserts a transparent ground and equal pads at a desk.
- **2026-09-27 — A content month's stage follows its work.** The user asked for the month's status to move by itself ("whenever the stage changed to ready it allows to create the meeting info … once it passes the meeting date & time and also there are tasks being created, the stage changes to In Production … if all tasks under the content month being mark completed fully, show a dialog … to ask whether it is really completed … and confirm / add tasks") and approved the rules proposed back. This supersedes "Ready and In production are claims the database checks" and Mark planning complete (2026-09-23, 2026-09-24): a status a person had to set by hand was a second copy of facts the month already held, and it went stale the moment a meeting passed. The stage is now worked out on every load (`engPhase()`): Planning until the readiness ticks are answered and the meeting is set or not needed; Ready until the meeting has passed and the month holds a task; In production while any task is open; Ready to close once every task is finished and one is done, where the card asks Add task or Complete month in place (a question on the card, not a dialog that opens by itself). Completed, Cancelled and Reopen are the only stages stored, because they are decisions; Completed is refused while a task is open. The counts come from `ops_engagement_counts`, because a colleague without `ops.all` reads only their own tasks and would see a month nearly done that is not; Bulk add's "already added" reads the same count for the same reason. A task's move into production now reads the ticks and the meeting instead of the stored word, which stays Planning for an open month; otherwise no content task could have started. Stored Ready and In production values from before are left as they are and read as open: nothing rewrites the table.
- **2026-09-27 — The creator reads the client's note (hotfix).** Reported by the user as extremely urgent: a client had asked for changes and the creator's page said "Please revise as noted below and submit again." with nothing below. The client's Request changes files its note in `option_reviews` (`review_draft`); `get_creator` read `drop_reason`, which only the team's own send-back fills. Shipped as a one-function migration ahead of the wider review-loop batch, because the creator's page already printed any note it was sent: the fix reached creators the moment the migration ran, with no page to deploy.
- **2026-09-27 — A typed link never widens the page.** The user sent the creator's page and then the client's creator selection page from a phone, both wider than the screen: the bar and foot stopped short of the right edge, the handed-in player ran off its card, and the facts fell into three columns with one alone. The cause was data, not the push before it: a creator's caption carried a 120-character Google Form link after Chinese text, with no space anywhere in it. Both cards sit in a grid with an `auto` track (`.cwork` on the creator's page, `.client-draft-preview` on the client's), and an `auto` track grows to its longest unbroken run, so the card, the player sized to the track and then the page grew by 105px and 58px at 390. The tracks are `minmax(0, 1fr)` now, and every block of typed text on the two pages breaks anywhere; `break-word` would not have done it, because only `anywhere` lowers the width a track is sized to. The walk seeds the same link on both pages, and its orphan check now reads the booking facts, whose odd last fact takes the row below 640 as `dl.facts` does.
- **2026-09-27 — The review loop reaches everyone.** The user sent the creator portal showing "Please revise as noted below and submit again." with nothing below, and then found the console the same: "unable to see the changes requested", "unable to see the caption submitted and revert for changes". An audit of every field the client, creator and console pages read against the column its flow writes found the cause and five more. (1) The client's Request changes wrote its note to `option_reviews`; the creator page read `drop_reason`, which only the team's own send-back filled, and the console read neither, so three live requests were on record and on no screen. Every decision on a draft is now one row, the client's and the team's, told apart by `source`; the creator page and the console card read the open one, and the card keeps it once the creator hands in again so the check reads the new draft against it. (2) Revert out of Changes requested was refused by the release gate (`qc-required`): the client's request had moved the round the check counts. `campaign_revert_changes` puts the round back to the one last checked, marks the request taken back, and refuses once the creator has handed in the next round, because Client review would then show unchecked files. (3) A team send-back counted on the client's page as their own round ("Revision 2 / 2"); the client's page counts their own requests only. (4) An empty caption drew nothing, and one live draft reached the client with no caption past a check that asks about the caption; the field is always drawn now, with "No caption." in warn, and the check reads the caption first. (5) A visit's location and site contact were sent to the creator and client pages but nothing in the console set them; the Shoot step does. (6) The client portal listed a report under its stored name "Social Media Report" while its cover said Social Media Accounts Report. Checked and found right: every other creator and client field, letter downloads, meetings, /verify/ and the review page. The reviewer's name was already kept per browser (`adspace_reviewer`) on both client pages and asked for once.
- **2026-09-27 — A lone booking, and a month the filter emptied.** Two layout slips found while fixing the overflow: a creator with one booking had no queue, so the card sat in the queue's narrow column with the pane's width empty beside it (it now runs the head card's width); and a month in My Work's Clients view whose tasks were all finished read "No tasks this month." under the Open filter, which hid them (it now reads No matches, with Show all).
- **2026-09-27 — Seven suites now fail on their own errors.** `prod`, `backup`, `client`, `cprod`, `qr` and `state` printed their error list and exited 0, and `run` never counted its list, so the gate passed them whatever they found. `prod` had been reporting "the quality check lost its ticks on close" since Tick all joined the list: it counted Tick all as a tenth check, so every tick kept read 10 of 9. The count now reads the nine checks, every one of the seven exits non-zero on an error, and `run` leaves out the sandbox's unreachable fetches as the others do.
- **2026-09-27 — No round count on the client's page.** The user, with the client's creator page showing "Revision 2 / 2" on two cards: "No need show revision rounds". The fact is gone from the card in both languages; the note and who asked for it stay.
- **2026-09-27 — One line per creator in a campaign's record.** The user sent a campaign's Activity with three change requests by one reviewer to three creators, read as one line with three notes joined: "Possible to log each creator comments or remarks as one log … combined is hard to read". The rows were keyed on the campaign alone, so one reviewer's decisions on three creators within ten minutes folded. A client's decision on a draft and a creator's hand-in are now sticky (each its own line, whatever the time), and every other booking entry keys on the campaign and the creator its detail names first, so a run of team steps folds per creator and names them once. The stand-in's `review_draft` now writes its row as the function does (creator, decision, note).
- **2026-09-27 — Two rows an entry in every history.** The user sent a campaign's Activity on a phone, where lines like "Stage changed harleyyyanggg_ · Pending / draft" broke at the edge of the time column: "the analysis would be to split two rows would be better? Split from interpunct onwards? Or u suggest whats best … Then applies to all others as well". Split between who-did-what and what changed rather than at every interpunct, because a detail holds its own ("WhatsApp Video.mp4 · uploaded by …"): the first row is the time, who, the act and what it is about, the second the detail in the soft ink, cut at three lines with a press to open. On a campaign's own page a booking's entry is about its creator, so the creator moves up to the first row. Words the first row already says are dropped from the second, on old rows as well: "quality checked by Qiao Rou" under Qiao Rou's Quality checked, "schedule updated" under Schedule updated. The last two tags nothing named were found by the same look: `campaign.dates` (read as "campaign dates" in lower case) and `client.deleted`, both filed `other`, so a group reading Creator Campaigns' or Clients' record through its part alone never saw them. They are named in `ACTION_LABEL` and filed by `activity_section` (migration `2026-09-27-two-tags-filed.sql`).
- **2026-09-27 — The first charts: My Work's Report.** One chart component (`js/chart.js`) in the portal's tokens, no library, the figures folded under each chart as a table, as agreed on 2026-09-26. Open work by person is bars, most open first, with the overdue count beside the name; its six counts fold under the bars. Stage duration is the median as a bar with the slowest 10% as its mark. On-time delivery by month is a line over six months, read with one `ops_report` call a month so it can never disagree with the period's own figures. Open work is not drawn against capacity: capacity is hours a week and open work is a count of tasks, and the board's capacity strip already sets hours against hours. Found on the way: the Report opened from its address (a refresh, a link) never asked for its figures and said there were none; it reads them on arrival now.
- **2026-09-27 — A task names its campaign or content set, and the record lists the task back.** The user: "for files and links is it possible to add one more inside, link to which creator campaigns or content reviews etc. it works like a backlinks kind? Is it useful here", then "loving the #2 proceed build as long as it doesnt break client-facing / creator-facing structure of course also internal admin structure". A link on a task may now name a record (`ref_type`, `ref_id`, kind `record`) instead of an address, through Record in the link's Kind select; the database takes only the task's own client's campaign or set and only once while the link stands (`ops_link_record`), files it on the task and in the record's Activity, and refuses to retype it as an address. The campaign's Overview and the set's panel list the tasks naming them (`ops_record_tasks`), withheld from a group without that record's section and never a task the reader may not see; the list is drawn only where a task names the record, so a campaign nobody links reads as it did. The link shows the record's name as it is now, since a set is renamed in place. Where a set is named and the task still owes its draft, the link form opens on Draft with the client's review link written in (prefilled, not fixed): making a set a draft on its own would have meant a fifth copy of the Client review gate. No client or creator page reads any of it. Migration `2026-09-27-work-links-its-records.sql` (named to sort after the day's other migration, because the suites take the newest file that restates `activity_section`). Verified by `ops: ok` (§32, Postgres), `reflink: ok` and the gate.
- **2026-09-27 — A ⋯ opens under its button wherever it is.** The user sent an iPhone screenshot (installed console) of a creator card whose ⋯ menu opened over the Shoot fields, far below the button: "Seems like an anchoring issues". Desktop Chromium placed it correctly; the menu is `position: fixed` and placed from the button's viewport position, and on an iPhone, after the date picker has closed, Safari can hold fixed boxes some way from the viewport the button is measured in. The only case handled before was a transformed sheet card. `ADspaceMenu.place` now reads where a fixed box actually lands with a probe at 0,0 in the menu's own container and takes that origin off, whatever caused it, so the sheet case needs no rule of its own. `reflink.js` wraps a card in a transformed container: 458px off before, under the button after. The same walk found the content set panel's review link and asset link fields had no names; both are named now.
- **2026-09-27 — The live post is a button, and the padlock left the tab strip.** The user sent the client's creator page with a posted booking: "The link to posted post is too not obvious. Need to be something like View post on Instagram / rednote / etc. based on their platform. Is it necessary to keep the approved there". The post was a bold platform name with a small mark inside a results table which, before any figures, held only that name and the date the card had already given. A live booking now carries one View post on {platform} button a platform (查看小红书笔记 in Chinese, where a rednote post is a 笔记); the table appears only with figures, dates each post only where the platforms went out on different days, and then the card's own Posted leaves. The approval line leaves once the post is out: it tells the client why the card moved on while it waits to go live, and after that the post is what they come back for; who approved it stays in the console's record. Placements are stored by their printed name ("Instagram") or, on older rows, the key, and both resolve to the same word. The same afternoon, from the Team page on a phone: the Performance padlock inside the tab strip "looks like its a third tabs"; it now sits at the right end of the row, outside the strip, and `perfui` measures that.
- **2026-09-27 — The quick sheet's link form on a phone.** The user sent an iPhone screenshot of My Work's quick sheet with Add link open: Address and Kind sat side by side with each label a field's height above its box, and Kind ran past the card's right edge. Below 640 the form's row turns to a column, but every field in a `.row` carries `flex: 1 1 180px`, and in a column that basis is a height: each field grew to 151px with its box pushed to the foot (`margin-top: auto`), and the row wrapped sideways. The column now keeps each field at its own height and full width, and the field after the row stands 12 from it, as the row's own fields do. It predates the record links; `reflink.js` now measures the form at 390 (89px between label and box before, 6 after).
- **2026-09-27 — The Schedule holds the plan; the team confirms for the client; selection closes when full.** Four asks in one message. (1) The creator card's Time was a text box while the Schedule's was a time picker, and "the schedule also has the same info as Creators > Shoot. Isnt it the same thing?" The Schedule is now the one place a booking's plan is written: date, time picker, location, contact and phone (tracking no. for seeding) on each row, saved on change and filed with each field's move; the card reads the plan (`planFacts`) with Edit in Schedule and keeps its notes, drafts and approvals. The Posted gate reads the Schedule's publish date and says where to set it. (2) The Results fields sat in a white band across the recessed step; they are the step's own field grid now, the platform read rather than a disabled field. (3) "Add one Confirm internally button for the creator draft (even after revisions)" for a client who agreed by word of mouth, the client's page showing "Proceed by {internal user} {timestamp}". `campaign_proceed` files a team approval under the colleague's name and moves the booking to Scheduled; the client's page reads Proceeded by (by_team from `get_campaign`, the team's note never sent); Revert out of Scheduled is `campaign_revert_approval`, which keeps the approval, undone. Confirm internally takes the place of the plain step forward at Client review, so every approval is on the record. (4) "Only show confirmed creators under Creators tab once confirm by our team"; asked whether Reopen selection gates the client, the answer was no: the client page followed free slots alone and never read the campaign's state. The user chose a mix: while a slot is free the client keeps choosing, even after confirms; the last slot filled closes selection (trigger-stamped `selection_closed_at`, enforced in `save_selection` and `confirm_selection`); a slot freed later (withdrawn, replaced, more slots) waits for Reopen selection, which is offered only then. Backups stay namable where opened and full. The Creators tab folds the unpicked under Not selected while closed.
- **2026-09-27 — Creators report their own results.** "Once approved by client, they can fill in ... the posting link, and data within the period given (this should be in sync with admin portal, one special thing is that the period date to date should be set by the admin only ... So we dont need to go back whatsapp communicate and then come back fill in again." Decided with the user: numbers only (no screenshots), and the post link moves the booking to Posted by itself; the team can Revert. The creator's page carries Post and results from Scheduled: per placement the link (the platform's own host, refused in their words otherwise), its date, and views, engagements and impressions once the team has set the count period; `creator_post_save` writes it and files `campaign.results` (Results entered). The console's Results step replaced Window (days) with Count from / Count to, prefilled from the publish date for a week until saved, and reads who entered the figures; setting the period alone leaves the creator's entry theirs.
- **2026-09-27 — Every post button the card's width on a phone.** A lone View post on {platform} kept its own width while a card with two stretched both, so down the list the same button looked different from card to card ("Why are the buttons different"). On a phone every post button now runs the card's width, one or several; at a desk they keep their own width. `cprod` measures the lone one at 390.
- **2026-09-27 — The high security finding was already closed; the helpers below it were not.** The user approved the "team-only RLS" fix. Checked live first: every public table has row level security on, no policy reads `true`, and every policy on the sixteen CRM and campaign tables asks `allowed(section, level)`, which returns false for anybody who is not an active colleague (done with the parts rework, 2026-09-2x; the finding was never struck). What was open was one level down: Supabase grants EXECUTE on new functions to `anon` and `authenticated`, so nineteen security-definer helpers written to be called only from inside other functions could be called with the public key, among them `ops_notify` (a notification in any colleague's bell), `ops_log` (history rows) and `ops_task_json` (a task whole, by id). None is called by a page, a policy, a view, a constraint, a default or an invoker function (checked live), so `internal-helpers-stay-internal` revokes them from everybody but their owner; `sql` and `ops` prove them closed to a browser and a comment from a browser still filing its history through them. The finding in `STANDARD.md` is replaced by what was checked, and a rule in `CLAUDE.md` makes every new helper revoke itself.
- **2026-09-27 — Push notifications.** The user: "Mobile notifications for all, client-facing esp on the creator selection; creator portal once it receives updates (like requested changes / ready to post etc.); admin internal team mostly on those that is now active notifications alr". Built on the standards alone (RFC 8291 aes128gcm, RFC 8292 VAPID) in plain WebCrypto, because a push library leans on Node's crypto and a send that fails in the edge runtime fails silently on a phone nobody is watching; `tests/pushcrypto.js` opens what it seals with Node's own crypto and verifies the signature. A device follows what its page proves, never a free-text address: the console by the signed-in colleague, the selection page by the campaign's token, the creator's page by the creator's code. The first draft keyed a device to one follow, which would have silently stopped a client's first campaign when they turned on the second; a client's device now follows any number of campaigns, while a console and a creator's page stay one-to-one (a shared device must not keep telling the last person). An endpoint anybody could type would have turned `push-send` into a relay that posts to any site on every campaign event, so only the four push services' hosts are stored, and the function checks again before it posts. Only a forward move is told: a Revert back into Reviewing is not a new draft, and telling the client twice about one draft teaches them to ignore it. Refresh app unregisters the worker and with it the subscription, so the page keeps a flag and subscribes again on the next load without a prompt. iPhones deliver only to a page on the Home Screen, so the client pages carry a manifest with no `start_url` (the Home Screen copy opens the very link that proves the campaign), and an iPhone in Safari is told how instead of being shown a button that cannot work. This narrows the 2026-09-25 rule that no client page carries a manifest: the client portal and the review page still carry none, and the two pages that offer notifications carry only their own, scoped to themselves, never the console's. Verified: `sql: ok` (the follow rules, the forward-only triggers, the queue closed to the API, a write that still commits without `pg_net`), `pushcrypto: ok`, `push: ok` (all three pages, the heal, sign-out, iPhone, blocked). The live delivery to a real phone is confirmed by the user.
- **2026-09-28 — One search across the console.** Approved by the user on 2026-09-27, replacing the "deferred until the lists are in the hundreds" line in `STANDARD.md`. A control in the console head (the word and a glyph at a desk, the glyph on a phone) opens a panel; Cmd / Ctrl + K and "/" (outside a field) open it too. It is a sheet through `js/sheet.js`, so Escape, the trap and the hand-back are every sheet's: it hangs under the head at a desk and comes up from the floor, full height, on a phone; the field keeps the caret while the arrows move the answer (a combobox), and typing marks it clean so the scrim still shuts it. It reads the tables each section already reads, through `js/api.js` and under row level security, with `ilike` (inside `or()`, the value double quoted so an email's full stop or a bracket is not read as grammar; `"`, `\`, `%` and `*` are dropped from what is typed), five rows a section, 200ms after the last key, two characters at least, and a sequence number so a late answer to an older query is thrown away. No schema: every read works under the existing policies. Only sections `may()` grants are asked; a refused read drops its section and never the others; all refused draws `failLine` with Try again, never No matches. Letters of Offer answer to `clients.documents` and open the client's Documents tab; register documents open Documents filtered to the reference; the Creators List, Short Links, Services and Team have no record route, so a result opens the list with its own search filled (the bar opened, so the filter is on the screen). Opening the Creators List from a search drops an open campaign (`campaigns.js` enter), or the address naming the list reopened the campaign. A content set is reached through its client, which only `js/admin.js` opens, so the bridge lends `restore()` (the reload path). No recent searches and no explanatory copy, by the spec. `tests/search.js` covers every section, the permissions (never shown, never asked), one and all refused, the stale answer, the pause and the two-character floor, the keyboard, the phone sheet and every record's address; the walk and the matrix measure the panel.
- **2026-09-28 — A file leaves the bucket only when nothing points at it.** Approved by the user on 2026-09-27 ("S3 build: delete-on-remove; the IAM split and lifecycle rule as step-by-step instructions for the user in the AWS console"). Supersedes the 2026-09-21 plan of a `delete-upload` function called by the page: the page still never deletes; a daily `s3-sweep` edge function lists `content/`, leaves anything under 7 days old, asks `s3_keys_in_use()` and deletes the rest with DeleteObjects. The predicate reads every text, JSON and list column of every `public` table whole, not a list of known columns, because a list goes stale the day a column is added and the cost of staleness is a client's file lost; only `campaign_deliverables.url` is read with its own rule (in use for 30 days after a soft remove, the Undo's life). Checked live read-only on 2026-09-27: links sit today in `drive_assets.url`, `campaign_deliverables.url`, `posts.media` (url and poster) and `campaigns.invoice_url`. A dry run by default (`S3_SWEEP_DELETE` = `on` to delete); held on an implausible answer (nothing in use, or over 500 at once); every run a row in `s3_sweeps` (RLS on, no policy). The signer is our own (Web Crypto, with an RFC 1321 MD5 for Content-MD5, which Web Crypto lacks) so it runs in Node too; `tests/s3sweep.js` checks it against AWS's published SigV4 examples. pg_cron and pg_net were not installed live, so the schedule is written by the migration only once they and two Vault secrets exist. `docs/S3-STORAGE.md` rewritten as the user's click-by-click steps: two IAM users (upload only; list and delete only), the old key deactivated before it is deleted, the lifecycle rule's housekeeping half, versioning recommended as the 30-day way back, the storage-class half held until about 100 GB.
- **2026-09-28 — Performance rewards (phase 2).** The user confirmed the framework's rewards as "correct" on 2026-09-27: grades on the monthly bands; a quarter is a calendar quarter from Q3 2026, its average the mean of the finalised months; an individual prize of RM 400 (highest eligible average, a tie splits; eligible means 70 or more, no E month, no Level 4 breach in the quarter, active, on the review list); a department prize of RM 600 from five criteria management enters and a critical-issue tick (the higher total wins at 80 or more with no issue, split among the department's active reviewed members); flexible hours for the next month when half or more of the active reviewed members scored 70 or more, used by a member at 70 or more with no Level 3 or 4 breach that month; a bonus pool opening at RM 500,000 revenue, at most 10% of profit, and a company trip at RM 1,000,000, both split by units (A 3, B 1.5, C 0.5) among members with three or more B months in the two quarters, no E month, no Level 4, active; growth commission payable only on a month at 70 or more with no Level 3 or 4. **Readings taken where the rules were silent**: a tie in which one department does not qualify pays the other the whole pool (tied departments are joint winners and a winner that does not qualify drops out); a month decides flexible hours only once every active reviewed member is final, so a month in progress reads as waiting rather than as not unlocked; a Level 4 breach counts from the breach log (not voided) whether or not that month is final; the bonus filter does not ask for the review list, as the rules list four conditions and not five; a share is rounded down to the cent and the remainder stated, so nothing paid exceeds the pool; a commission is pending while its month is not final and the member reads it only once decided; revenue, profit, the pool and the trip budget are entered together by an admin, and the history files revenue and profit by name only so the rest of management never reads them; the caller's own row reaches management with its name alone, as a month leaves the caller out. **Nothing is stored piecemeal**: every figure is worked out on each read, and Confirm keeps the snapshot a member then reads; Reopen removes it and files what it was. `perf_today()` is the rules' one clock so the Postgres suite can stand after the quarters it reads. Verified by `perf: ok` (every rule edge), `perfui: ok`, `perfguard: ok`.
- **2026-09-28 — The two rewards confirmations are ink, not blue.** The user: "the performance phase 2 suddenly rendered a blue action button out of nowhere". Confirm quarter and Confirm period confirm a figure the confirmer owns; they hand nothing to anybody, so by `DESIGN.md`'s colour promise they are `.btn-primary`, never `.btn-go`. `tests/perfui.js` reads the class.
- **2026-09-28 — An admin can delete a performance month.** The user: "the team performance is missing the delete records for admins", then chose "any state, with safeguards" and "the month's row ⋯ and the review sheet". `perf_delete` asks for the performance part at Manage, a live master-code unlock and admin, and refuses an admin's own review, a missing reason (`reason-needed`) and a name and month not typed back (`confirm-mismatch`). The review, its disputes and its scores go. **The history does not**: `perf_events.review_id` became `on delete set null`, and the append-only trigger now lets the database's own set-null through at depth > 1 (it returned OLD, which silently cancelled the set-null; returning NEW keeps it). One `deleted` row names who, the month and the reason and carries no score; the Activity record reads it as Record deleted. **A printed record is a document somebody may still hold**, so `perf_deleted` keeps its reference and `/verify/` answers it as HR Letter, Void, never "not found"; a new review of the same month, once printed, is answered first. The sheet says what the member has already seen and that a downloaded copy will read Void. `tests/perf.js` §14 runs it against Postgres; `tests/perfui.js` §8 drives both entry points and the refusal of a partial name.
- **2026-09-28 — Release says whether to notify.** The user: "an optional notification tick, to notify or not to notify … So we wont need to specifically notify them when i issue for June July and August 2026", then "No add the Notify {name} as a permanent feature for all months". Beside Release sits Notify {name}, ticked by default on every month; `perf_release` gains `p_notify boolean default true` (the three-argument version is dropped first, because PostgREST cannot choose between overloads) and files `notified` in the release row. Unticked, nobody is told and the sheet says "was not notified".

Once posted, upload the post link and payment details." So the line reads "Please complete your payment details." and the block appears at Posted only, after the link, never at Scheduled.

The head follows the record head: the name with the state, PDF and the ⋯ beside it at the card's right edge, the meta under them; the button reads PDF on a narrow pane. - **2026-09-28 — The front door is a visitor card, and the one page Google lists.** The user, after Apple's Apple Park Visitor Center page: the website's directories "in all ticks", working hours 10:00 to 18:00 by appointment only, the address, and "How can we help? Reach out to our whatsapp and talk through the AI". Settled with the user along the way: no menu bar, the header stays the wordmark and Digital Portal; the ticked rows are the website's ways in (About us, Our services and Our works open adspacestudios.com's home until their own addresses arrive); Monday to Friday; "We're ADspace (optima font) no Studio (strict rule)"; the wordmark, not the round logo; one photograph, a placeholder until its link arrives; Creative Advertising Agency under the name in place of a live open line; the WhatsApp number hidden and the button on `wa.me/adspace`; Apple's wider side margin on a phone. The dated week is worked out from Malaysia time on the page (UTC+8, no summer time) and redrawn every minute and on return, so it cycles by itself. **Only `/` may be listed** ("google only can list digital.adspace.me homepage. Not /admin and others"): it drops noindex and names itself canonical with its business details (ProfessionalService, the hours, the address, the map) and never the number; every other page already carried noindex, and `robots.txt` deliberately disallows nothing, because a page a crawler may not fetch is a page whose noindex it cannot read and whose bare address it may still list from a link. `sitemap.xml` names `/` alone. `tests/seo.js` fails if any other page loses its noindex, if the number appears, or if the sitemap grows. This supersedes the 2026-09-20 and 2026-09-24 front door (a scriptless cover with the mark and a 15px name, noindex).
- **2026-09-28 — Clients sign in with the code as well as the link.** The user: "Can client signin uses otp as well? The link is not working very well." The email already carried the code (the Magic Link template prints `{{ .Token }}` for the console's code sign-in), so the client page gains the console's code step: after Send code the cover asks for the code from the email (`#signCode`, `autocomplete="one-time-code"`, 6 to 10 digits) and `verifyOtp` signs in; the link still works where it opens. Nothing on the server changed: `portal-login` still makes a login only for a live contact with access, and a refused address is sent nothing, so any code for it simply fails and reads exactly like a wrong code for a real client. I cut it into 16:9 landscape"), shown whole at that ratio rather than cropped to a band (resized to 800, 1200, 2000 and 2560 wide, WebP with a JPEG fallback, the location data stripped), and serves as the og:image; the ways in now open their own pages (/about, /services, /project, /contact). It had no tab strip, on the reading that a client reads the page once; nine sections down one column, some white cards and some tables loose on the ground, with the rail falling above the work below 1100, read as scattered. Now the record head, one tab strip (Overview, Services, Letters, Reports, Meetings, Account; the two middle ones only once there is one) and one pane at a time, every section a white card with its title inside. The Overview answers what a client comes to check (the services as confirmed and to quote, the next meeting, the latest report, the engagements), each card opening its tab, with the account manager (WhatsApp and Email) and the company beside it; the manager left the head, so the head says the company and the state once. The pane is in `?tab=` and pushes history, the strip is a keyboard tab list and a swipe region, and the panes measure their own width (`data-narrow="640"`), which is what gives the tables their phone rows: the `.rec-pane` the old page sat in was the thing `ADspaceState.fit` watched. A report's download from the Overview says its failure under its own button (`#ovRepMsg`). `tests/portal.js` (tabs, address, Back, refresh, arrows), `tests/reports.js` (the Overview's report and meeting cards, View all), the walk (every tab, with a report and a meeting seeded) and the matrix (three tabs by address).

Everything but the photograph now shares one column (`--lp-col`, `--lp-gut`), the bar's and the foot's content included, and the ticks run from the column's left edge to its right with equal gaps; at 1280 every edge is 150 from the window. The name is back, centred: ADspace in Optima over the tagline `advertising | marketing | branding` in Slate Regular (the user: "or just put our tagline"); an opening status was drafted after Apple's "Open until 6:00 p.m." and dropped before it shipped ("needs to change ... since we are not retail store"). Under a finger the bars stay opaque (`--chrome-solid`): Safari on an iPhone takes the status bar's colour from an opaque bar and passes over a translucent one (2026-09-26), so a glass bar there would bring back the grey band above it.

The portal's Contacts had been drawn as underlined words in this batch; they are `.plink` again, and `.plink` is now outlined (1px `--line-ctl`, no fill) on every page, so a way to reach somebody never reads as a state chip (tonal) or a link. A probe of every visible link and button on the Clients screens and the portal found one more bare text link, the Overview's email (and its phone as plain text): both are chips, as are the month card's meeting link and Drive folder and the Creators List's profiles (bare text since 2026-09-21; their negative margin went with it, since a chip's edge is its ink). The Overview's View all is the console Overview's own quiet button with a chevron (`.ovgo`), not blue text: blue is for handing work on. Then the corners (the user: "rather than making all buttons all cards same radius do you considered its paddings and the radii curvature", with the outer = inner + padding rule): measured, a 38px control's 10px corner is 26% of its height, but the 32px controls and the chip kept 10 (31 to 32%), the bar's icon buttons had 8 (21%), the rail's rows 9, and a ⋯ menu's rows kept 10 inside a 14px menu padded 6. Now a 32px control takes 8 (`--radius-ctl-sm`, 10 under a finger where it is 38), every 38px control 10, a menu's rows 14 − 6 and the bell's 14 − 9; all controls measure 25 to 26%. Cards (14 with 18px padding) and sheets (18) are unchanged: at that padding the corners do not read as nested.

The client page was one long column: a link panel, a fold of settings forms opening in the page, a grid of 125px set cards, a coloured-dot status and a line of its own for each post's pencil and bin. - **2026-09-28 — A client's billing details reach only the Billing part.** Found while planning the Overview: `clients_read` admits Clients, Content Review, Creator Campaigns and Reports at View to the whole row, so the registered name, registration and tax numbers, billing contact, finance email and billing address were readable through the API by people the Billing part refuses (the page hid the pane; the database did not). A policy cannot hide a column, so the table's SELECT was taken back and granted column by column for everything else (worked out from `information_schema`, so running the section again grants a new column). `client_billing(p_ids)` answers the values to Clients: Billing at View and the name and address to Documents at Work (a letter is addressed to them). Clients at View without Billing still gets `billing_missing`, the required fields left blank, because the Active gate names them and a person who could move a lead to Active before could still do so. Every page read of `clients` now names its columns (`ADspaceAPI.CLIENT_COLS`); console search no longer finds a client by its registered name. `deal_value` (the services total) and `sst_applies` (priced by campaigns) stay readable. - **2026-09-28 — The Overview.** Asked for as "I remembered we have a dashboard is it not built?"; outlined, then approved as proposed: visible to groups with Manage anywhere (and admins) as their landing page, the three schema changes, no money cards. It answers what each section has waiting rather than showing decorative totals: late tasks and who carries the open work, cold leads and the intake by month, unanswered requests, content sets the client has not decided (waiting since `batches.published_at`, a new column stamped by trigger and cleared on Unpublish; sets already published take their last post's time), active clients with no set this month, bookings past their visit or draft date, drafts waiting for the quality check, unsigned Letters of Offer, reports in review, active clients with no report for last month, and last month's reviews by step (`perf_overview`, names and steps only, like the Activity record's Performance tab). `ops_report` gained `open_by_person` (only where `ops.all` is granted) so the count is the database's, not a page's. Each card asks its own permission before it reads, so a manager of one section reads nothing of the others (`tests/overview.js` counts the reads). Money was left off on purpose: the only value columns sit behind Billing or finance parts, and the page is a working start page, not a sales board. Admin now lands here instead of Clients.
- **2026-09-28 — Migrations go through the connector.** The user: "You'll run supabase for me right through connectors". Claude applies each migration with the Supabase connector once its merge has deployed (the billing columns first, since the page had to stop asking for `*` before the database stopped answering it), then checks the live privileges or objects. The billing migration was applied this way and verified: the eleven billing columns closed, the other 34 granted, anon reading nothing, `client_billing` callable by the team only. Edge functions and dashboard settings stay with the user.
- **2026-09-28 — Chips and ⋯ centred on the title line.** The user sent an iPhone screenshot of My Work's Clients view: the month card's Ready / In production chip and its ⋯, and a task's Urgent chip and ⋯, sat below the line they belong to ("each in their own world"). Measured at 390 with a coarse pointer: the month card's chip and ⋯ were 12px below the facts line (the control group was a 44px target aligned to the top of a 20px line); a task's ⋯ was 12px below its title and the ring 3px above it, plus a stray 4px from the general phone rule `.team-act { margin-top: 4px }`. The control cells now take the title line's height (month facts 13px × 1.55; task title 22px) and the target overflows evenly: every centre within 0.3px.

TypeError: Load failed" minutes after the billing migration. The API log showed the list read answered 200 at that moment: Safari's "Load failed" is the connection dropping before the answer arrives (the installed app resuming, or signal), not the database. `js/api.js` now gives the Supabase client a fetch that retries a GET once after 600ms; a POST, PATCH or DELETE is never repeated. The same message asked that Message to client, the month card's client-facing act, be split from the meeting's facts by a border: it takes the card's hairline, 12 on each side.
- **2026-09-28 — Templates are families; a format fills the task.** The user, on the From template sheet and the New task format list: "When creating Reels up to 30s / 60s it should then come out using the Reels template already right. Else I'd be doing double triple works", then "the variants is the hours that it might take bcos checklist its the same for Reels … same goes to Graphics (Static, GIF, Carousel)", "GIF 2h / Remove 45s / Proceed all others". A template now owns the rate card formats it serves (`ops_template_variants`, one family a format), each with its own hours; `ops_create_task` fills a task from the family of its format, so New task, Bulk add and repeats need no template picked. The family never moves a deliverable off the content workflow and its "due N days after" is not used, because a deliverable's dates come from its month. The ⋯ item became Templates and only edits families (no client, no date, no Create tasks). Seeded once: Reel → Reels (30s 3h, 60s 4h, 120s 5h), Static post → Graphics (Static 1h, GIF 2h, Carousel 2h, the export line naming the FB and IG sizes), Report 1h; Short video and Carousel, now variants, set inactive, nothing deleted.

Deriving them from the Confirmed lines' start and months was proposed and declined: "No dont auto derive as i have yet decided how to proceed in the future. As for current this crm portal is supplementary. I will still need to issue a quotation and invoice with own items from my accounting portal in Bukku. Hence im worried that there are duplicate jobs needed just for any new engagement. Im trying to refine these internal actual sops which might affect existing workflows.
