- **2026-09-28 — One New sheet makes a month's pieces and their repeat; repeats run themselves; the Clients view is Months.** The user: "why bulk add still necessary? Why not just at the create / new task or content deliverable Optional repeat", then "1 and 2 proceed" (one sheet; rename the view), and "3 stays there bcos to avoid confusion whats for tasks numbering for which sections until i have a detailed task numbering section for all other sections" (Task numbering keeps its place in the ⋯). The New sheet's content deliverable is now a list of pieces (description, format, week), Add piece carrying the format and the next week; `ops_create_pieces` makes them through `ops_create_task` in one transaction, all or none, so a refusal on the fourth piece leaves no three behind. Several pieces take a tentative day inside their week, as Bulk add did, because the calendar needs a day to draw and a repeat a day to count from. The Repeat tick sets the same rule on every piece. Bulk add, its preview and Run repeating tasks left the ⋯, which is now drawn only where Templates, Select tasks or Task numbering applies (a colleague at ops Work was otherwise shown an empty menu). Run repeating tasks went because the database now runs repeats itself: a client's month confirmed makes that client's repeats for it at once, as the person confirming (a trigger, whose refusal never fails the confirmation), and pg_cron runs `ops_recurring_daily()` at 00:10 MYT for this month and, in its last seven days, the next, each rule as the colleague who set it (else its task's owner) with that colleague's own access, since every check reads the caller from the request's claims. Two faults surfaced on the way and were fixed in the same migration: a rule run for its own month copied its task onto its task's own day (the task is now the first occurrence), and a weekly copy kept its task's week whatever its date (only a monthly copy keeps it). `ops_generate_month` stays in the schema, uncalled. A read of the live database before the migration ran found the one active rule copying a task that had been deleted, with its September copy deleted too: run by hand that was harmless, but a morning run would have made the deleted copy again every day. So every date a repeat makes is now kept (`ops_recurring_made`, by rule and by the task it copies) and never made twice, even after the repeat is turned off and on again, and a task deleted stops its repeat (`ops_tasks_stop_repeat`); the migration records the copies already made and stops that rule. My Work's Clients view is named Months (`view=months`; an older `view=clients` address still lands there), and a month's ⋯ offers Add tasks, opening the sheet on that client and month. Verified by `ops: ok` (§35, Postgres: all or none, idempotence, the day guard, the week rule, the trigger, a deleted copy not made again, a reset repeat making no date twice, a deleted task stopping its repeat, the migration's backfill, the morning run as three different actors and refused from a browser), `work: ok` and the gate.
- **2026-09-28 — The Creators List's record reads on one line again.** The user sent an iPhone screenshot of the list asking "What is this layout design": the Campaigns cell read `1` over `· Sept 2026`, and the mute dash for a creator with no campaign floated below its line. The cause was a class name. The Content Review rebuild (#238) styled its client record as `.cr-rec` (a two-column grid, `minmax(280px, 1fr)` beside the main column, 12px above), and the Creators List's record cell had been `.cr-rec` since it was written; the later rule won on equal specificity, so on a phone the count and the month fell into two grid rows 12px low, and at a desk the second column's 280px floor squeezed the count to nothing (`· Aug 2026` with no number). No suite saw it: `camp` read the cell's text with the whitespace collapsed, which still said `1 · Sept 2026`, and the walk's roster seeds no creator who has worked. The cell is `.cr-record` now (`.cr-record-n`, `.cr-record-when`), and on a phone the record and the links share one baseline, since an outlined chip is 38px and a record set at the top of the line sat 9px above the chip's text. `camp` measures it at 1280 and 390: one line with the count drawn, on the first chip's baseline, ending where the fee ends; each check fails with the old rule put back.
- **2026-09-28 — On a phone every tab strip is the column's width.** The user, on an iPhone screenshot of the client portal: "Why ur tab doesnt go full width. Such awkward width now on mobile?". Since 2026-09-27 a record's and a route's strip was drawn at its own width, the Activity record's look; on the portal its five tabs stopped 11px short of the cards' edge, which reads as a mistake rather than a choice, and two-tab strips (Campaigns / Creators, Members / Performance) sat at half the column. On a phone `.rectabs` and `.tabrow` now run the column's width with their tabs sharing what is spare, as the command bar's view strip already did; a strip that does not fit still scrolls with its edge faded, a desk keeps the strip at its own width, and the Performance padlock stays after the strip at the row's right end. In the same pass the campaign list's phone line split `RM` from `8,640.00`: its parts now wrap between each other, never inside the count or the amount. `camp` measures both at 390 (the strip's edges against the column; the amount's line boxes), each failing without its fix.
- **2026-09-28 — One label, one glyph.** The user, on an iPhone screenshot of Content Review: "Why is my secondary button some are losing the icons alr?". The rebuild of Content Review (#237) redrew the review link's Copy link and Preview as words, while Creator Campaigns drew the same pair with the copy mark and the leaving mark; nothing compared one screen with another, so the loss went unseen. A census of every button on the walk found seven more labels drawn both ways: Add entry and Issue document in Documents, Copy link in a creator's record, Add task on a month card, Edit in Templates, in a report's check step and in Performance's figures, and Add link on a task's full record. Each now carries its action's glyph (a plus to add, a pen to edit, the copy mark, the leaving mark), and a form's own commit (Save, Cancel, a submit, a sheet's foot, a confirm) stays words, as it always was. No button that never had a glyph was given one: adding icons across the portal is the UX brief's batch 2, which waits on the user. `uxaudit` now records every acting button across the whole walk and fails a label drawn with its mark on one screen and without it on another. Its first full walk found three more: Download on the client portal's Latest report (the console's Reports drew the file mark), Add or Edit on a task record's Brief (the quick sheet drew the plus and the pen), and Open on a task's link (the client portal drew the leaving mark). Open now ends on the leaving mark for an address and on the chevron for a console record, which opens in the same tab. The wider Open then showed a phone's link row running its unbroken address on under the button: an address is the row's one meta line and ends in an ellipsis, the whole of it one press away in Edit.
- **2026-09-28 — The review canvas is one scroll on a phone; a set's head is a record head.** A client complained, through the user, that opening a post to review it left a third of the screen under the caption and the decision: below 860 the canvas split into two scroll areas, the mockup above and the rail below, each clipped to its share of the screen, so a Reel's own caption and the lower third of every post sat under the rail. The user suggested folding the rail or putting the caption under the preview; the second is what shipped. The canvas body is one scroll: the post whole, then the state, the copy and Approve / Request changes, and each post opens at its top. A sheet that folds would have been a gesture to discover and a second scroll again. `canvas` measures it at 390 × 700 and failed on the old layout. The same afternoon the user sent the set page's head ("The buttons"): it was the old set toolbar, a filled pen square beside the name, the chip hung off the pen, and the ⋯ beside Unpublish on its own row. It is the record head now: the name with the quiet pen, the line saying who can see it, the state and the ⋯ at the title row's right edge, Publish at its own width under them.
- **2026-09-28 — The canvas steps by swipe and its buttons read as buttons; a board column takes a drop anywhere.** The user, on an iPhone screenshot of the Review Canvas: "The button is not obvious here left right and close", and asked that a swipe step through the set. Previous, Next and Close were `.iconbtn`, the quiet mark that takes colour only under a pointer, so on a phone they read as glyphs on the bar. They are filled tonal buttons now, Previous, `2 of 30` and Next one group and Close apart at the right edge, the post's size under its name on a phone so the three keep their room (at 320 the name ends in an ellipsis). The swipe is `js/swipe.js`'s, not a second one: a region may name a Previous and Next pair, the finger moving left pressing Next as a strip's next tab and every photo viewer do; a post's video takes the swipe above its own controls. The user also asked that a board column take a drop over its whole length ("not just the first card area"): each column was as tall as its own cards, so a column holding one card took a drop only over that card. Every column now runs the board's height and a drop anywhere in it, or in the gap beside it, lands there; a card held at the board's edge scrolls the board. The new test found a real fault on the way: the first drag's release selected text across the page, and the next press dragged that selection natively, which cancelled the pointer, so the card never lifted. A card now never starts the browser's own drag and a press on it selects nothing.
- **2026-09-28 — Four posts across, the Urgent chip beside the name, record strips full width, a lighter contact chip.** From the user's desk screenshots. The review gallery ran three across in a 1240px column with cards of at least 340px; the review page takes a 1480px column and cards from 300px, so a wide desk shows four and a 1280 laptop three, no card narrower than before. On My Work's list the task name was set to grow (`flex: 1 1 auto`), which pushed Urgent to the far edge of a wide Task cell where it read as the Task Owner's ("couldnt just die hard coded inside without considering the desktop looks"); at a desk it now follows the name, and a narrow row keeps it at the end of the title line. The user asked for the record's tab strip to run the width of the card under it; it does at every width, while a section's two-tab switch keeps its own width at a desk. The user asked for a lighter solid border (#515151 or #a1a1a1) on the contact chip: it takes #a1a1a1 (`--line-chip`, dark #5f5f5f). Fields keep `--line-ctl`, which is the lightest neutral that holds WCAG's 3:1 for a field's edge on the page ground; the chip's words identify it, so its edge need not.
- **2026-09-28 — Field borders to the lightest neutral that holds 3:1.** The user asked for lighter field borders; #a1a1a1 measures 2.4:1 on the page ground and fails WCAG's 3:1 for a field's edge, so the offer was the lightest grey that passes, and the user took it. `--line-ctl` is `#8e8e8e` (3.0:1 on `--page`, 3.3:1 on `--card`), neutral where it had been warm `#8e8a80`; dark follows at the luminance it had (`#858585` for `#898480`), so no dark pair moves. The swipe direction on the review canvas (finger moving left is Next) was confirmed the same day.
- **2026-09-28 — The gate runs what a change touched, never `all` by habit.** The user, on a full gate started for one border colour: "one small border u need run the full gate? seriously … it is wasting my tokens and credits". `css/portal.css` left the shared-file row: a style change runs `ui` (the walk measures every contrast and boundary on every page, which is what a token change can break), plus `geom` for a layout change, plus the suites of any script it touched. The standing "`all` before every merge that changed behaviour" is gone: a merge runs the union of its rows, and `all` is kept for a shared script, the stand-in and a schema change.
- **2026-09-28 — Client rate, never Fee.** The user, on the Creators List: the column headed Fee "is actually not the actual fee to be provided to the creator. it is our marked up pricing". The stored `rate` is the client's price with markup (which is why `get_creator` never sends it), so the Creators List, the campaign's client selection and its Overview bookings head it Client rate, and the two fields that set it read Usual client rate and Client rate for this campaign. The bands keep their ranges.
- **2026-09-28 — An inline control's fill hugs its line.** The user, on a task's due date lit on hover as a grey box twice the height of its line: "should these be just text link instead of such huge invisible button?" A plain text link would give up the target (38px at a desk, 44 under a finger) and read as the underlined word the portal forbids. So every word or value that acts inside a line (`.linkbtn`: Change, Open the month, Edit; `.tdate`, `.tdate-src`: the dates; `.tinline`: the row's owner and due; `.serial-copy`: a reference) keeps its whole target, and its hover fill is a 26px `::before` centred on the text. Icon controls (⋯, ticks, pens on their own) keep their square fill, which is their shape.
- **2026-09-30 — A meeting is booked from the next half hour; Claude deploys the functions.** The user: "limit the book date, cannot be before today … 3PM now, you can only start booking from 3.30PM". The content meeting's date field starts at the next slot's day and, on that day, the time field at the slot (`nextSlot()`, the next :00 or :30 strictly after now); a time before it is refused on the sheet by name ("Choose a time from 30 Sept, 3:30pm."). A meeting already held keeps its date and time when the sheet opens to change its note or link, so the floor applies only to a new time. Page-side only: `ops_engagement_set_meeting` still takes any date, as a held meeting's record may be corrected. The same day the live `meet-create` was found a version behind the repo (the 24 Sept Meet-link retry) and, at the user's word, Claude deployed it through the connector (version 16, Verify JWT off); functions are deployed that way from now on.
- **2026-09-30 — A landscape share card.** The user, on a WhatsApp preview of a Content Review link: the card showed the square favicon (`img/icon-512.png`) filling the whole preview. Every page but `/` now shares `img/share-1200.png` (1200×630, `summary_large_image`): the ADspace wordmark in Optima (#404040, the favicon's own grey) over Digital Portal in Slate Regular, as the console's bar reads, on white, drawn from `css/OPTIMA.TTF` and `css/SlateRg.TTF`. The square icon stays the favicon and home-screen icon.
- **2026-09-30 — Why a review's reels play only in Safari.** Read from the files themselves: every video in Content Review is an iPhone QuickTime (`.mov`, brand `qt  `) holding HEVC (`hvc1`), served as `video/quicktime`, with its index at the end of the file. Safari decodes HEVC; Firefox does not play HEVC and refuses `video/quicktime` outright; Chrome and Edge play HEVC only where the computer's hardware and system codec allow it. The remedy is H.264 (`avc1`) in MP4.
- **2026-09-30 — Every video gets an H.264 copy the pages play first.** Following the finding above (every Content Review video HEVC in QuickTime), the user chose automatic conversion over refusing HEVC at upload, having been told the cost (about US$0.015 a minute of 1080p through AWS Elemental MediaConvert, available in ap-southeast-5). A Lambda (`workers/video-convert/`, set up by the user from its README) on the bucket's ObjectCreated events under `content/` reads each video's boxes by range (never the whole file), leaves an H.264 MP4 already indexed first alone, and otherwise asks MediaConvert for `name.web.mp4` beside it: H.264 at QVBR 8 up to 8 Mb/s, AAC where there is sound, turned upright, colour forced to Rec. 709 because an iPhone records HDR, the index at the front. No row changes: the name is the link, so `js/media.js` gives every player the copy first and the original second, and a browser falls through a missing copy to the original. Safari plays either; Chrome, Edge and Firefox play as soon as the copy lands. The function can read and list `content/` and create jobs, nothing else; MediaConvert's own role reads and writes `content/*` and cannot delete. `{ "all": true }` converts the files uploaded before, once. The daily S3 report counts a copy as in use while its original is, so the copies never read as unused files.
- **2026-09-30 — Every copy fits 1080 × 1920 at up to 60 fps.** The user: Edge "shows the running loading but cannot play". Read from the copies: 19 of 21 were 1080 × 1920 at 60 fps (H.264 level 4.2); the two uploaded that morning were 1920 × 3414 at 60 fps (level 5.2), made from originals of that size, since the copy kept the original's size. That frame is beyond what many Windows hardware decoders take. The user asked for the cap at 1080 × 1920 and 60 fps and whether a 4K upload is brought down to it: it is now. The Lambda reads the video track's size (turned by its matrix) and rate from the movie box, and the job scales anything larger to fit 1080 × 1920 either way up, never enlarging a smaller one, and asks for 60 fps above 60. An H.264 MP4 over the cap is converted too. `{ "all": true, "redo": true }` remakes only copies over the cap, under the same name (the original is never touched); CloudFront keeps the old copy up to a day unless its path is invalidated. The cap also keeps every job off MediaConvert's 4K rate.
- **2026-09-30 — A reel plays in its card, under one play button.** The user, after the copies were fixed at 1080 × 1920: Edge "shows the running loading but cannot play", while the same copy opened on its own played on a click; in Safari a press on play "popups". The cause was the page: a press on the video's own play control was also a press on the mockup, which opens the Review Canvas and moves the post's blocks into it, and moving a video element that has just started stops its load (Edge spun) while Safari showed the canvas over the playing reel. Every video in a mockup now waits on black under one play button, the same in every browser, in the platforms' own style (a translucent dark disc with a light ring, so it reads on black and on a frame); a press plays it where it is and only then does the browser's bar appear. A press on the button or on the playing video is the mockup's, never the canvas's, and Enter on the button is the button's; the rest of the card, and Enter on the card, still open the canvas. Close carries a playing video on in its card; stepping to the next post leaves it stopped. The same day the cover image card lost the black COVER IMAGE band under its frame (an uppercase label restating the card's own head) and no longer stretches to a neighbour's height, which had left a blank above its decision.
- **2026-09-30 — A file is uploaded when it is handed in, not when it is picked.** The user, after a listed company's form that holds attachments in the window until Submit: a file picked and then removed had already gone to S3, where nothing is ever deleted, so every change of mind stayed in storage. Content Review's Add assets, the creator's hand-in and the team's hand-in for a creator now hold picked files on the device (object URL previews, the video's poster drawn locally) and upload only on Add to set, Submit / Update, or Hand in, with progress; a file taken off before then costs nothing. Only uploaded drafts are kept across a reload, since a file on the device cannot be; leaving with one held asks first. A failed upload keeps its file held and hands in nothing, so the creator's step never moves on half a delivery.
- **2026-09-30 — A story carries no caption on its frame.** The user, on an iPhone, sent an S P Setia Instagram Story whose whole caption (contacts, address, hashtags) filled the video top to bottom, the play button under the words. The Story mockup had laid the caption over the frame as a sticker since the review page was first built; over a picture it read as a dark box, and over a video now waiting on black it read as the story itself. Stories have no caption on the platform and the card already carries the words under Copywriting, so the sticker is gone (`tests/canvas.js` seeds a story with a long caption).
- **2026-09-30 — A post is revised in place, and both sides sort by stage.** The user, on a thirty-post S P Setia set: approved, pending and sent-back posts sat in one list, and the only way to revise a post was to delete it and upload the new file into a new set, which lost what the client asked and what changed. Now a change after the client decided on a round is the next round of the same post (trigger `posts_revision`), the round it replaces is kept in `post_versions` for the team alone, and the client's card reads Revision N with what they asked. Both sides list posts under the stage strip with each stage's count (the user asked for the secondary sliding strip with the count inside); the client's opens on Pending, and a decision repaints its card but moves it only on the next load (the user: an approved post should not jump away on the press). The client may edit the copy inside Request changes; the user chose a suggestion over an edit that applies at once, so nobody's spelling or hashtags go live unchecked, and the team presses Accept copy. The old workaround (delete and re-upload into a new set) is retired; posts already moved that way cannot be linked back.
- **2026-09-30 — No outlined buttons, and the audit now says so.** The user, on the review page: the stage strip hung part way across a phone, and an outline button looked "a little huge" ("why are there these design inconsistencies still happening"). The button was Copy text, the review card's own control from before the design system, outlined at a full control's height; a probe of every page and state found it the only outlined button left. It is now the small tonal button with the copy mark. The cause of the recurrence: uxaudit checked card outlines but not buttons, so a page-local button class could keep a border unnoticed; `btnline` now fails any button outlined on all four sides but the contact chip (the × on a file tile and the red billing gate keep their edges). Its first walk found two more: the ⋯ and the console's Back drew a grey ring on hover and while open, where the ⋯ is to carry only a fill; both now shade only, their hover inside `@media (hover: hover)`. On a phone the stage strip is the column's width with Changes requested reading Changes, so all four stages fit without scrolling.
- **2026-09-30 — The caption is edited in place, under a pen beside Copywriting.** The user, on an iPhone: the Request changes box drew Edit copy and the Name field on one line ("wtf with this user experience"), then: "The button edit copy is different size and doesnt actually tell … what should i edit until its clicked. Why not just build a pen beside the copywriting to edit directly", and "Copy use the word Copywriting / Caption". Edit copy is gone. A pen beside the Copywriting heading (the quiet icon button of a record's name) turns each caption into a field where it is read and opens Request changes under it for a note and the name; Cancel puts the words back, Send request sends the edit as the suggestion the team accepts. The pen leaves once the post is approved. The box itself is one column, 12px steps, the note and the name its full width, measured at 390 and 430. Every user-facing noun reads caption: Show full caption, Suggested caption, Accept caption, Caption edit suggested, the publish warning ("has no caption"), the upload line ("Add the caption below"), and the activity detail (`· caption edited`). Copy text stays: it names the act, not the thing.
- **2026-10-01 — While a request is open, the decision pair steps away.** The user's iPhone recording: after editing a caption under the pen, Approve and Request changes still sat above the note box, and pressing Request changes did nothing, since the send was Send request below it, under the keyboard. Two controls named for the same act competed. While Request changes is open the pair now leaves (`.approve.is-requesting`) and the request starts the decision block: the caption field, the note, the name, then Cancel and Send request. Cancel brings the pair back with the caption as it was. The request and the edit now stay open until the answer is in, so a failed send loses nothing typed (before, the box shut on the press). `revise` checks the four controls' visibility at 1280, 390 and 430, the block's start, Cancel, and a refused send.
- **2026-10-01 — A revision says who asked, not which round.** The user, on an iPhone screenshot of a revised S P Setia post: "You asked:" ran straight into a note that was itself a whole caption, so the label and the words read as one, and "Revision 2" is the team's word. The client's card now heads the block `Changes requested by {name} · {date}` (12.5/600, mute) with the client's words under it in ink, 8px apart; a caption edit adds "Caption edit applied." The console keeps Revision N on the set page, where the team counts rounds.
- **2026-10-01 — Confirm internally for Content Review, Edit request, and equal halves.** The user asked for Creator Campaigns' Confirm internally on each Content Review post, "so we could proceed for client", and placed it in the console's post ⋯ beside Edit and Delete, never on the client's page. It is a `reviews` row with `source` team written by `review_confirm` under the colleague's name (Content Review sets at Work, a published set, a round not already approved), filed as an approval with "confirmed internally". The client's page names the client, not the colleague: "Confirmed by {client name} on {date}" (the user's words), since the client gave the yes; the console names who pressed it. The way back is Revert confirmation: the row stays, marked `undone_at`, and the post falls back to what stood before, a client's request included; only the team's own approval can be reverted, and a client's approval is asked again with Request re-approval as before. In the same message the user asked why a sent request could not be changed within its round: the Request changes button now reads Edit request while a request stands and reopens it as sent (the note, and the client's own caption edit, which the feed now returns on the standing decision since they are the client's own words); sending again replaces it. Asked what happens when a client approves over a standing request: the approval replaced it silently and the team lost sight of the ask, so Approve now arms first in place ("Approve as it is", "Approving withdraws your request for changes.") and the console row keeps "Earlier request: …". The user also asked for balanced buttons: Approve had 1.35 shares against Request changes, and Send request its own width against Cancel; both pairs are equal halves.
- **2026-10-01 — Content Review flow audit.** At the user's request ("audit the flows too") every state a post can hold was walked from both sides: pending, a client's request (with and without a caption edit, edited again, approved over), a client's approval (asked again with Request re-approval), a team confirmation (from pending and over a request, reverted), a revision after each, and a stale client page acting after the team. Found and fixed before merge: the activity record named every approval "Client approved", a lie for a team confirmation, so the label is Approved and the actor says who; Revert confirmation was filed as "Resent for approval" though nothing is sent, so it is its own tag, `review.unconfirmed` (Confirmation reverted), filed under Content Review by `activity_section`; the feed carried the colleague's name on a team decision, though the page never showed it, so a team decision goes out with no reviewer; Confirm internally was offered on an unpublished set only to be refused, so it shows only on a published one; and the post ⋯ items carried no permission, the Delete among them, so each now names its part (`review.sets:work`, Delete `review.sets:manage`). Left as designed: a stale client page acting after the team decides is the latest word and wins, which is the client's own decision; a post team-confirmed and then edited is the next round, like any decision.
- **2026-10-01 — A reel's console row shows its cover frame.** The user's iPhone screenshot of a set page: every Instagram Reels row drew an empty grey box. The row drew the video bare (`<video muted>`), which iPhone Safari leaves blank until it plays, while the upload had kept the reel's cover frame on its media (`poster`) all along. Every console thumbnail (the post row, its edit form, the upload tiles) now goes through `thumbOf()`: the cover frame where there is one, else the video asked for its first frame (`#t=0.1`, `playsinline`, `preload="metadata"`). `revise` checks both.
- **2026-10-01 — Client links carry an eight-character key; the long ones still open.** The user found the review link's key (24 to 32 hex characters) too long, took eight characters when offered ten, and asked that the current long links be retired but keep working. Keys are eight characters from the creator code's alphabet in lower case (about 850 billion; guessing any one of 25 links at 1,000 tries a second takes about a year, and a client's passcode still applies on top). The three copies of the key maker in the console (one with a `Math.random` fallback) are one, `ADspaceAPI.accessToken()`. The migration gives every client and campaign a short key and keeps the long one as `moved_token`; the review and selection pages, finding no feed for a key, ask `link_moved`, put the new key in the address and load, so nothing already sent breaks and anything bookmarked from then on is short. A moved key opens nothing by itself and answers one exact key with one key. Reset access link clears it by trigger, since a reset exists to retire links. A readable slug was ruled out as the key: it would be guessable, and a guess would open the client's content. Push subscriptions follow a campaign by its id, not its key, so they are untouched.
- **2026-10-01 — The quarter is ranked, totalled, and confirmed only on final months; the bonus runs over halves.** The user asked for a thorough audit of Performance ahead of keying in July, August and September 2026: whether a quarter's average, ranking and rewards follow from the months, and how Q4 carries on. The arithmetic held (the mean of final months, the monthly bands, the prize rules, flexible hours, units, commission). Four gaps were found and closed in `2026-10-01-performance-quarter-ranked.sql`: (1) a quarter could be confirmed while a month in it was still released, disputed or a draft, so an average missing that month would have been kept and paid; Confirm now refuses `months-open` with the count, and a month an active reviewed member has no review for is counted (`missing_months`) and asked about before confirming, since a colleague may have joined mid-quarter; (2) the list ran eligible first, so it never read best to worst; it now runs by average with a shared `rank`; (3) a person's reward was split across two tables (the individual prize on the person, the department prize on the department); each row now carries Individual, Department and Total; (4) the bonus period could start on any quarter, so Q3 and Q4 2026 and Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 could both be confirmed and Q4 paid twice; a period is now a half of the year (`perf_half`, `perf_company_period_half`). A member's own quarter leaves out the rank: the ranking is management's. Readings left with the user: a quarter average from fewer than three final months stays eligible (as the rules state), and a department prize is shared among the department's active reviewed members at the time it is worked out. Verified by `perf: ok` (§17) and `perfui: ok`.
- **2026-10-01 — Date of evaluation, and 7 days to dispute.** The user, keying in July to September 2026: "Date of evaluation is the date that this report and numbers are reported to the team member. It should reflect in the report", and asked for the dispute window to show there, choosing 7 days over the framework's 3 when asked. `perf_reviews.evaluated_on` is filled by release where empty (the day in MYT) and corrected by management until final, in or after the month reviewed and never after today; a correction is filed from and to. Release now sets `dispute_until` 7 days on; months released before keep their window. The review sheet's Improvement and review card leads with it, and the printed record's head reads Review month, Date of evaluation, Dispute until, Reviewed by. `perf: ok` §18, `perfui: ok`.
- **2026-10-01 — Every colleague picker starts on the person creating.** The user: a new content month's Manager, and every pick of members or managers, should start on whoever creates it, changed only where someone else takes it. Task Owner and a new month's Manager already did; a new lead's and a new campaign's Person in charge started on Unassigned, and editing a month with no Manager silently showed the first colleague in the list (saving them). All four now start on the signed-in colleague. `crm`, `camp`, `work` assert it.
- **2026-10-01 — Three suites read the calendar.** On 1 October `work` failed: its report reads the stand-in's September 2026 stage records against "This month" (one day long), and its Months checks named "Sept 2026". The report's pages now run on a fixed clock of 28 Sept 2026 (`clock.setFixedTime`), and the Months checks read the month the suite runs in. Pinning the whole suite was tried and broke checks dated from today. `phone`'s centring check needs a row with a priority chip, and its urgent task sat in September's card, shut from October; the seed now puts it in the month the suite runs in.
- **2026-10-01 — The department prize is the department's; Review by is Follow-up date.** The user: the individual prize rightly reads the average of the months ("one performs badly in a month still have chance to improve"), and the department prize is not split by the portal: "the amount to split is to determine by the team leader of each department. U need to show which dpmt won". The quarter's departments now carry only their `share` (no per-member `each`), the department table reads Department, Total, Grade, Critical issue, Prize with the head naming "{Department} won · RM 600.00", the ranking drops its Department and Total columns, and a member's own quarter reads "{Department} won · RM 600.00" or "did not win". Asked what Review by meant (the date the improvement plan is checked again), it is renamed Follow-up date, and the card Evaluation and follow-up.
- **2026-10-01 — Date boxes read DD/MM/YYYY.** The user: "Can all dates be DD/MM/YYYY? More consistent", then chose date boxes only, written dates staying `1 Oct 2026` (never misread as a US date). A browser draws its own date box in its own language's order and cannot be told otherwise, so `js/form.js` §7 turns each desk date box into a DD/MM/YYYY text box whose `value` property still reads and writes YYYY-MM-DD (an own property over the prototype's), so no page changed; typed digits take their slashes ("Ddmmyyyy possible to type it in straight?": yes), and the calendar mark opens the browser's picker through one hidden native field laid under the box. Phones keep the system's box (it follows the phone's region, not US order), and My Work's in-place pickers, which open the calendar at once and leave with it, keep the native box (`data-native`). The field's own min and max, which a native picker greyed out, are now held on blur.
- **2026-10-01 — The Ads Manager import reads an ad as Ads Manager does.** The user, building September's advertising report: the hook rate imported from the .xlsx did not match Ads Manager; every ad read 1 to 30 Sept rather than the days it ran; the result type read `actions:post_engagement`; and "is it possible to add bulk select in the ads". Found: a Hook rate or Hold rate column was taken from the last row read, so an age breakdown kept the 65+ band's rate; it is now weighed by impressions across the rows (a worked-out rate already summed plays and impressions and was right). Reporting starts and ends only repeat the range exported, so they are now the last resort: an export by day or week dates each ad by the first and last day with impressions, and leaves reach to be typed (a daily reach counts a person again each day, so it cannot be added up); without one, the ad's own Starts and Ends are read and held inside the report's period, Ongoing reading to its last day. The day no longer splits an ad into one row a day (the key is name, objective and ad set). Ads Manager's result keys read as words through `ADspaceSmReport.resultWord`, on import and for rows already saved. Select on the Ads step puts a tick on every row and the `.bulkbar` over the list: Move to objective and Remove (asking, naming how many), each with Undo. The stand-in deletes and refuses by `.in()` as PostgREST does. Hold rate stays ThruPlays over impressions until the user decides on ThruPlays over 3-second plays.
- **2026-10-01 — Import: one ad one row, dates by the day, Ads Manager's own rates.** The user: Starts read May for a September report (the live page did not yet hold Starts inside the period); "switch hold rate, i need the figures be the same in the ads manager"; and whether separate ads with the same creative and objective are added together. Hold rate is now ThruPlays over 3-second plays, matching the PDF's own reading guidance. A custom rate column's formula is found by testing the row figures against each plays-over-impressions, reach or plays pair, then worked out on the ad's totals, so an ad's rate reads as Ads Manager's for that ad; weighting by impressions remains the fallback. Two ads are never added together: Ad ID keys an ad where exported, else name, objective and ad set. A daily export for an ad already in the report changes only its dates, keeping the monthly figures and reach (two exports: the month for figures, the day for dates); a name more than one ad shares is left out of it. A formatted table's total row is used for the figures, its bands only for the split. Rows from two ad accounts are refused, as a report is one client's. Meta's Ads Reporting tool exports the same columns and can hold several accounts in one saved report, so its export is filtered to one account before pasting.
- **2026-10-01 — A report row is one creative and one kind of result.** The user, on the live Like Mee report: two 2609W1_FishBeeHoon ads, one counting Post engagements and one Interactions, were added into one row (89,119 results). A row is now the creative (name without the creator code), objective, ad set and result type; an ad with no results joins its creative's one kind of result. Without Ad ID the rows of an ad with no results name no type, so a name with two types is refused, naming the column. Ads Reporting's raw export does not combine ads by name, so the portal combines copies (Calia: 127 ads, 38 rows); reach summed over age rows came out a few percent under Ads Manager's per ad, so a paste naming ads already in the report updates them with what it holds (no age: the figures and reach; by age: the split; by day: the dates).
- **2026-10-01 — Draft with AI.** The user asked for AI to draft a report's commentary and chose to build it in the portal (the Meta Ads MCP serves a chat, not a page). The `report-draft` edge function reads the report under the caller's access and sends Claude only the figures (period, totals, previous period, each ad or post), the creator code stripped; a structured tool answer gives exactly the report's four fields, and the page fills them for the team to edit. The model id is a secret (`REPORT_DRAFT_MODEL`) beside the key, so no model name sits in the repository. API credits are billed apart from a Claude.ai plan; the user tops up and sets the key.
- **2026-10-01 — Creative cards.** The user: one creative runs under several objectives (Calia: 38 rows, 25 creatives) and should read as one ad with its objectives, from one uploaded cover. The rows stay as they are (creative, objective, result type), so no figure is added across result types; the PDF's Ad performance keeps a ranking table per objective (always, even for one ad) and the per-ad cards are replaced by Creative performance: one card a creative, its image, a line per objective, and the age split and video figures from the line that spent most. An image added on one row of a creative is copied to its other rows. The console's Ads list keeps its objective groups, which Select and Move to objective work on.
- **2026-10-01 — Draft with AI in the house style.** The first live press failed: the model the user set refuses a forced `tool_choice` (400), so the four fields are now held by structured output (`output_config.format`), with room for the model to think first, and every refusal is logged with the API's own reason and named on the page (no credit, a model name not known). The user then shared four approved ads reports to set the tone. They share one shape (a summary paragraph on spend, delivery, the objective and the strongest ad; What worked by objective with sub-points; What to fix with the reason; a focus with budget splits and the ads that continue), and their best lines rest on context the figures cannot carry: budget moved to Google Ads, a lead form made longer, ads paused, unspent budget carried forward, next month's budget. So the house style is written into the function (with an invented example, since the repository is public), tightened where the reports were loosest (a reason and an action for every fix; cost per result compared only like with like; no "RM 0.00"), and the page gains Notes for the draft, sent with the press and kept only in the browser so it can never reach the client's copy. The client's last finished report's commentary is sent too, so a month follows up on what it promised, with the client's name masked as "the brand". The accounts report has the same button on its own fields. In the ads ranking the result's word now sits whole under its count (Post engagements had broken between letters in a narrow column), a one-month run reads 1 to 30 Sept 2026, and a cost under ten sen shows three decimals.
- **2026-10-01 — Meet clashes, the meeting sheet, the calendar's place.** The user: after Save the Content meeting sheet stayed open while the month behind it had already updated, and the date calendar opened at the window's top left; and the shared calendar holds other plans, so a Meet should be refused only where another meeting link overlaps. `meet-create` now counts only events carrying a Meet link or a Meet, Zoom or Teams address (the sheet had stayed open because Google answered `slot-taken` for an ordinary event, and its reason sat under Led by, below the sheet's fold; a refusal now scrolls its line into sight). The DD/MM/YYYY box's hidden calendar field now takes the box's own place and size, and its layout is read before `showPicker`, because Safari anchored a first open to where the 1px field had last been laid out.
- **2026-10-01 — Accounts report read platform by platform; captions as written; the foot on the margin.** The user, from a live PDF: findings combined across platforms confuse ("all platforms algo work differently"), the Top posts page ranked every platform's posts together beside each platform's own top five, captions came out crumpled with blanks where the emoji were, the Draft line lifted the foot, and Post engagements broke between letters. The accounts report now reads Executive summary, then Insights and recommendations with one block a platform (its line, Highlights, Areas to improve, Recommendations, from the account's own fields), then each platform's page with its top three posts ranked on that platform alone (`topOf`) as cards carrying the figures, the caption on its own lines and the remarks; the mixed ranking page is gone. The Commentary step edits all of it in one place: the summary, each platform's four fields and its top posts' Why it stood out; the old across-platform fields stay in a fold so older reports lose nothing. Draft with AI answers the same shape, in a more formal client-facing voice, with platform-specific reasoning allowed only as recommendation. The blanks were a timing fault: emoji images were embedded after the pages were drawn, so every emoji is now embedded first. The foot's Draft or version line is removed and the foot sits on the margin's line. Post engagements reads Engagements everywhere (a second paste still matches rows stored under the old word, through `resultWord`).
- **2026-10-01 — Draft with AI is rate limited in the database.** The user asked for a limit on drafting. Every press is claimed through `ai_draft_claim` before Claude is asked: 5 a report, 20 a colleague, 60 the team in any 24 hours, counted under one advisory lock so two presses cannot both take the last; a press that fails is marked failed by `report-draft` (`ai_draft_done`) and not counted, so an outage does not use up the day. The count lives in `ai_drafts` (RLS on, no policy, every grant revoked), not in the browser, so it cannot be cleared from a page. The user also floated one free draft then an admin master code for the rest; recommended against a shared code (it travels and cannot say who used it) in favour of these per-person caps, with the console's monthly spend limit as the hard ceiling.
- **2026-10-01 — One draft a report; again is an admin's.** The user chose it over the master code: a colleague who is not an admin has one draft a report (a failed one does not count), and an admin drafts it again, to 5 a report in 24 hours; the per-person and team caps stand. Keyed on the login (`team_members.is_admin`), so nothing is shared or passed around.
- **2026-10-01 — Each ad row keeps its Ad IDs.** The user saw two ads with no results and could not tell which ads in Ads Manager they were, and was still dating ads by hand. An export's Ad ID and Account ID columns are now kept on the row (`sm_report_ads.ad_ids`, `ad_account`): the IDs show under the name as copy controls, the ⋯ opens the account in Ads Manager with those ads chosen, and a second paste (a day export for the dates) matches rows by Ad ID before the name. A blank result is Meta's own: the ad had no results of that type in the period. The IDs are the team's reference and never reach the PDF.
- **2026-10-01 — A paid draft is never lost; the ads PDF reads tighter.** The user asked that leaving mid draft warn, since the tokens are spent. Closing or reloading the tab mid draft now asks (the browser's own question; a page cannot word it), and moving to another step or screen inside the console no longer loses anything: the answer is kept for its report and filled in on return, so no in-console warning is needed. In the ads PDF a result reads as a count and one short word (47 Leads, 15 Messages, 84,660 Reached), never Ads Manager's long name broken mid word, and an ad that spent with no result reads 0 Leads (the user changed None to this the same day; one result reads 1 Lead), the console rows reading the same short word; Interactions and Reactions keep their own words so two result types are never shown as one. Points in the commentary tables move whole to the next page. Each objective's ads are listed in the console as the PDF ranks them. A top post card no longer repeats its date under a name that already carries it, and its figures run the column's width.
- **2026-10-01 — A creative card carries each objective's spend, under a table head.** The user found the card's labels and figures hard to tell apart (both small, Book and Regular) and asked whether the spend per objective belongs on it. It does: the card's head states the creative's total, and each line's cost per result is read against its own spend, so each line now has Amount spent. The labels sit on the shaded band in Slate Regular that every other table in the report uses. The ranking table and the cards stay: the table ranks the ads within an objective, the card reads one creative across objectives with its age split and video figures.
- **2026-10-01 — Drafts in Chinese, shorter, and never against our own work.** The user asked for Chinese clients and found drafts long and, worse, critical of the content, planning and creatives the agency itself made, which undermines the client's confidence. A language segment (English / 中文) sits beside Draft with AI, prefilled from the main contact's preferred language and kept per client; Chinese is composed as Chinese, not translated. Every draft now keeps to a few points a field and one sentence a point, and reads a shortfall as what the audience showed and what we will test next, never a fault in our work. The ads report's What to fix is now Areas to improve, as on the accounts report. A fully Chinese PDF (headings and labels) is a separate step, not done.
- **2026-10-01 — Preview before Issue; a letter is never lost to a stray click.** The user asked to see a document before issuing it and found the Issue document sheet closed on a click outside it, losing the letter. Preview draws the sheet as it stands on the letterhead without writing anything or spending a number (reference PREVIEW). The register's own sheets had their own outside-click handler that ignored the rule every other sheet keeps; they now close on it only while untouched. A save-as-draft for letters was not built: Preview answers the need to check before issuing without a second state to manage.
- **2026-10-01 — A client letter is ACL/{Client ID}/{YYMMDD}{NN}.** The user set the reference for a letter to a client: ACL (ADspace Cover Letter), the Client ID, the date as YYMMDD and the day's index from 01. `issue_document` builds it from the letter's own date (today unless the sheet gives another, so a dated letter and its reference agree), takes the lowest number free for that client that day, and holds an advisory lock on the base so two issuers cannot take one number. The engaged `SA/` distinction and the type code are dropped from new references; letters already issued keep theirs and /verify/ answers both (`2026-10-01-client-letter-serial.sql`).
- **2026-10-01 — Signed or not is a tick on the letter.** The user asked whether a computer-generated letter needs signing and chose a tick per letter over changing types. To be signed is prefilled from the type; unticked, the issuer is still named (straight under ADSPACE PLT, no signing space) and the foot says no signature is required. `issue_document` takes `p_signed` (the twelve-argument shape dropped first, `2026-10-01-letter-signature-tick.sql`).
- **2026-10-01 — Overview reads a section at a time.** The user found the Overview one long scroll and asked for the tabs and swipe the other pages use. Each section is a tab over its own pane, counting what its list cards hold and warn where something is late, so the glance across sections survives in the strip. It opens on the first tab rather than jumping to a late one once the counts arrive, which would move the page under the reader; the warn count points at it instead.
- **2026-10-01 — A report can be issued in Chinese.** The user asked for Chinese clients, in professional corporate Chinese and never word for word, then narrowed it: the cover and the file name stay English (the report is known by one name), the titles and table heads inside read Chinese. The language is the report's own (`sm_reports.lang`), set beside Draft with AI and taken first from the main contact's preferred language, so the draft and the PDF always agree. Every fixed string of the PDF is answered from one hand-written dictionary at the drawing primitives, counts read with their measure words (47 个潜在客户) and dates as 2026年9月1日 (7月5日至6日 in a table cell, the year being the report's). The Chinese face moved from Noto Sans TC to SC, since a Simplified report on the Traditional subset can miss glyphs. Spreadsheet pastes now read quoted cells, so a caption copied with its line breaks keeps them.
- **2026-10-01 — In a Chinese report the template stays English.** On the first Chinese PDF the user asked that only the context and table titles read Chinese and the template stay English: Executive summary, Insights and recommendations, Ad performance and every page title, the header and the foot. The page titles and the furniture are drawn with the Chinese layer off; table titles, column heads, notes and dates stay Chinese. A table's period sits on one line (7月12日至18日), the year being the report's.
- **2026-10-01 — Ad IDs open on a press; the commentary reads one way in both reports; titles in title case.** The user found eighteen-digit Ad IDs listed under every ad "a whole mess" and asked for a small control that opens them, and for them in the Edit ad sheet beside Cancel and Save. A row and the sheet's foot now name how many (2 Ad IDs) and open a card listing each as a copy control, with Copy all (comma separated, as Ads Manager's search takes them) and Open in Ads Manager. From the PDF: the ranking table's narrow Ad column broke 2608W4_OldOwnorInvest mid word while the figures had room to spare, so the name now takes the widest column, and an ad with no result shows a dash rather than Not available. The accounts report's insights were a label column beside the points; they now read as the advertising report does, a shaded head over numbered points. The report is a formal document, so its page titles and block heads take title case. The user also asked how the tables are ordered: each objective's ads cheapest cost per result first, those with no result after by spend; the creative cards below are a separate read, the creatives by total spend, most first.
- **2026-10-01 — The language switch is the draft's; the PDF stays English; new ad reports compare with the month before.** On a Chinese PDF the user found it read like a mainland report, while Meta's own terms are English and their clients read the commentary, not the labels, in Chinese. They had switched to 中文, drafted, switched back and exported, and asked that this be the behaviour: the switch now decides only the language Draft with AI writes in; the template, headings and labels print in English, and the commentary prints as written. The Chinese layer of the PDF is kept but switched off. The user also asked that First month of ads not be ticked by default, since most clients advertised before the portal: a new report starts unticked, and with no earlier report the month before is prefilled for its figures, which the team types from Ads Manager; a client's true first month is ticked by hand. On a running client, a finished September report's period and totals carry into October by themselves. The late count on the Overview tabs became a small warn chip: bare warn ink measured 4.50:1 on the tab track.
- **2026-10-01 — The Chinese face is TrueType in the repo, embedded as a subset.** Two Chinese PDFs from the user drew garbled in a strict viewer and weighed 7.4 MB. The face came from a CDN as OpenType with CFF outlines; pdf-lib cannot subset CFF and embedded the whole 8 MB face, declaring it TrueType (CIDFontType2, FontFile2) though it is not, which lenient viewers forgive and strict ones do not. The same Noto Sans SC 2.004 (OFL) was converted to TrueType outlines with fontTools and committed under css/. A first conversion still drew gaps: fontkit's subsetter joins glyphs without padding and writes short offsets, so any glyph of odd length shifts every one after it; the face is saved with its glyphs padded. A page of Chinese now adds about 10 KB, and letters with a Chinese block draw from the same face.

### 2026-10-01 · Ads PDF: results as a count alone
The user: the result words (Leads, Messages, Engagements, Interactions) in the Ad Performance, Results by objective and creative tables are technical terms; clients read the cost per result. Each result cell now prints its count only (0 where an ad spent with none); the ranking table's Results column narrowed to 0.11 and the ad name widened to 0.31. A mixed objective (Engagements beside Interactions) still never marks a cheapest across kinds. The console keeps the short word on its rows.

### 2026-10-01 · Ads report: the export's summary row, one cost head, the creative card's head
The user: an Ads Manager export's first row (no ad name) holds the account's reach, impressions and amount spent, and was being skipped. It now fills Step 1 where empty (reach counted once across ads and days, which adding up the ads cannot give; checked against the user's ad-level and day-level exports, which carry the same row); a figure the team typed is kept and the sheet says so. Frequency stays worked out as impressions over reach. "Per 1,000 reached" was an awkward head: every table now heads Cost per result, a reach result reading `RM 1.33 / 1,000`, explained once in the first month's guide. The creative card's name band and its table head were the same grey and ran together; the card's head is now white over a hairline.

### 2026-10-01 · Accounts report commentary speaks the advertising report's words
The user asked what Highlights and Recommendations meant, and whether Highlights belonged to each post: the three posts' Why it stood out sat under Recommendations in the same block with no head. A platform's fields now read What worked, Areas to improve, Focus for next month (the PDF: What Worked, Areas to Improve, Recommended Focus for the Following Month), as the advertising report reads; the posts' remarks sit under their own Top posts label. The Across all platforms fold shows only on a report that holds some of it. The Edit account sheet's Remarks fold duplicated the Commentary step's fields for the lead account (the same columns, which Draft with AI fills); it now shows only on a non-lead account that already holds remarks, so they can still be edited or cleared.

### 2026-10-01 · The top post card leaves the caption to the appendix
The user: the caption on a top post card is unnecessary, and the appendix's caption, cut at two lines with no mark, read as a bug. The card now carries the figures and Why it stood out only. The appendix (every post with its figures, kept) shows the caption as one excerpt, its lines run together, cut to two lines (one beside a remark) and ending on an ellipsis where it was cut, measured on the drawn width and cut by character, never inside an emoji.

### 2026-10-01 · Placeholders are generic examples; an account is named by its handle
The user: placeholders should show what a person is expected to type, as generic examples (@adspace.advertising, John Doe, email@adspace.me), never a client's name or anything related. Every typed-value field across the console and client pages now draws from one set (DESIGN.md §6): names John Doe / Jane Doe, emails email@adspace.me (colleagues email@adspacestudios.com), handles @adspace.advertising, the Facebook page ADspace Advertising, phones 012-345 6789 and 07-123 4567, https://adspace.me, codes AC001 / AD001, the reference AQL/AC001/260901; fields with no natural example keep their short hint. The report's Account name, which only labelled the account inside the portal and defaulted to the client's name (three identical rows), is now Handle, prefilled from the client's Brand handle for the platform picked.

### 2026-10-01 · Histories run one line an entry
The user, on the Activity record: the two-row entries suit a short log, but a long one becomes a page to scroll through; they asked for the earlier left and right reading, compact. A history with room (720px and over: the Activity record, a client's and a campaign's Activity) now reads one line an entry, as a table: the time, what happened and on what, the detail cut at the line's end, and who at the right edge, a hairline between entries; about nineteen entries fill the Activity record's view where eleven did. A rail or a phone keeps two lines, each cut at its end. Any cut entry opens whole on a press, as a long detail did.

### 2026-10-01 · Access levels renamed; a colleague's access ends on a date
The user asked for the levels to read No Access, View, Manage, Full Access, and for an expiry per colleague (all set to 31/12/2027 unless extended; no access after). The stored keys (none, view, work, manage) and every policy stay; only the words changed. Expiry stands a colleague down rather than adding a date check to `allowed()`: every policy, function and picker already refuses an inactive colleague, so one daily job reaches all of them, and the date is checked in Malaysia time. The last admin with access is skipped so the console can never lock itself out; nobody sets their own date; extending an expired colleague's date brings them back at once.

### 2026-10-01 · The filters are behind one button at a desk too
The user approved the open item: filters behind one button on every desk bar. The phone's Filters mark now draws at a desk as well, after the bar's last select, and opens a card hung from it (`#cmdPop`, laid by `ADspaceMenu.pop`) holding the same select elements, moved in and put back, as the phone's sheet does; a card rather than a sheet because a desk has the room to keep the list in sight while it is filtered. A select that is a view rather than a filter (`data-nofilter`: whose work, the workflow, Performance's month, quarter and period) stays in the bar at a desk, where it is read all day. The badge, Clear and the "not drawn over hidden selects" rule are the phone's.

### 2026-10-01 · The Activity record has a Reports tab
The user: the Activity record was missing Reports; only the essential steps, not every action. A report's eight steps (started, submitted, returned, confirmed, published, revised, unpublished, deleted) were already filed, under Clients; `activity_section()` now files them under Reports, a part of the record (`activity.reports`) that follows the record's own level unless set, drawn after Documents as in the rail. The user then asked for the saves (Save, Save and continue) and every Draft with AI to be kept as well: the page files `report.saved` after each save that the database accepted (the step or the sheet, and what it held: Commentary, Figures, Account added: @handle, Post edited, 12 posts imported, Imported from Ads Manager: 3 ads added, 2 ads updated) and `report.ai_drafted` or `report.ai_failed` for every press, with its language or the reason, under the report's client, period and version. Runs of saves by one colleague fold in the record's display as every run does; each is a row of its own in the log.

### 2026-10-01 · On a phone a sheet is pulled down to close, and a half-filled form is kept
From the UX brief's batch 2, the one item the user approved ("Sheet swipe-down + drafts"): swipe down to close, backdrop tap to close, and a draft of a half-filled sheet restored on reopening. The pull starts only on the sheet's head (a grabber drawn there), so a body that scrolls is never fought; past 90px or flicked, it presses the sheet's own close mark, so each section's own way out runs. A draft is kept against the exact state the sheet opened in (every field's value, hidden ids included), so an Edit of another record, or of a record changed since, is another form and restores nothing; only the fields the person changed by hand are kept, never the section's own filling. Pressing the sheet's action ends the draft whether the save succeeds or not: a failed save keeps the sheet open with its fields, and a saved Add must never fill the next one (a duplicate record). Because the typing is kept, the scrim closes a touched form sheet on a phone; a desk keeps no drafts and keeps the rule that the scrim never closes a touched form, where a stray click is rarer and the suites' Cancel-and-reopen paths stay as they were.

### 2026-10-01 · Draft with AI shows how many are left
The user asked to see the drafts left beside Draft with AI, simply ("1 left", "5 left"). `ai_draft_left(p_report)` reads `ai_drafts` as `ai_draft_claim` counts it and writes nothing: one draft a report for a colleague who is not an admin, five a report a day for an admin, twenty a colleague and sixty the team a day, failed presses not counted. The line shows the least of the three, so it is the figure the next press meets; at 0 the button rests and the message line says which limit and when the next is free. Read when the Commentary step opens and after every press.
Then, against an accidental click (the user): every press asks first, Draft with AI? on empty fields and Replace the commentary? over written ones, each saying it uses one draft and how many are left.

### 2026-10-01 · The Handbook: the internal files in the console, behind a minute-long link
The user asked whether the Employee Handbook and other internal files (SOPs, policies, templates) belong in the portal rather than an HRMS app, then chose to build it here: named Handbook, no read-and-accept step, admins alone edit. The portal already holds the team's sign-in, the activity record and the permission checks, so a file gains an audit trail and nothing to sign into twice; acknowledgement, leave and payroll stay with an HRMS if one is taken up later. The files sit in a private Supabase Storage bucket rather than S3: S3 is public through CloudFront and keeps everything, while a policy document must be readable only by a colleague who is signed in, and must be removable when it is withdrawn. A file is opened through a signed link that lasts 60 seconds, made at the press, so a copied address stops working a minute later. A version is a new object and a new row, never an overwrite, so the version a colleague read can always be shown. Archive is the soft remove; Delete takes the title typed back. The Handbook is not an access section with levels: every colleague reads it, and an admin's flag is the only write.

### 2026-10-02 · Migrations through the connector: what it holds back
Applying the 2026-10-01 batch, `apply_migration` and `execute_sql` timed out on every statement that held `drop` or `delete`, a function body included (`drop trigger`, `drop policy`, `handbook_delete`), and nothing was written; the same work in small pieces without those words went through at once. The user's first run of the whole Handbook file in the SQL Editor then rolled back entirely at the storage step. Two separate runs (the function, then the storage policy) applied cleanly. Working around the connector's confirmation by assembling the words at run time was ruled out: the hold is a safety gate, and the held part goes to the user.

### 2026-10-02 · Audit after the October features: PDF libraries on demand, four fixes
The full walk at 1280 and 390 (both themes), a field scan of every input, the database calls each route makes on load, and console boot timing at a phone's CPU speed. Performance: pdf-lib and fontkit (1.1 MB) loaded in the head of the console and the client portal on every visit and cost about 600 ms of a phone's load; they are now fetched on the first drawing (`ADspaceDocs.lib()`), and first paint went from 796 ms to 284 ms, DOMContentLoaded from 1177 to 666 ms (4× CPU, warm). Database calls per route showed no waste: Overview's seven `ops_report` calls are the six-month trend, My Work's three task reads are open and finished work. Fixes: the closed rail drawer cast its shadow as a grey band down every phone screen's left edge (the shadow now belongs to the open drawer); Team's bar ⋯ took the small button's 92px minimum and stood apart from Add member (it is the bar's 32px square, as My Work's); the lead form's Industry used the filter idiom, so a new lead showed a blank select and Not set saved the word "all" (no live client held it); four email fields for other people (contact, finance, colleague) offered the signed-in person's own address and no longer autofill. Considered and left: `defer` on the console's own 41 scripts (a smaller gain against a boot order every suite depends on) and one read of `clients` for Overview's five cards (each card asks its own permission first).

### 2026-10-02 · Posts imported from a Meta export read as Meta writes them
The user imported a Facebook page's September export and got 330 posts dated 9 January, each titled with its caption: the importer read 09/01 day first, took Title as the name (Meta fills it with the caption) and made a post of every row of the daily export (one row per post per day). Meta's exports are month first, so a paste carrying Post ID and Publish time is read month first, and any other paste decides by its own dates (a part over 12 says which), falling back to day first as Malaysia writes. One Post ID is one post. Asked whether to download the lifetime export or have the daily one added up: both are taken. Lifetime carries Views and Reach and is taken as it is, but counts to the day it is downloaded, so it is the one to use when downloaded on the 1st of the next month; the daily export has no Views or Reach, its days inside the report's period are added up, and reach stays blank because people reached on two days are counted once, not twice. A re-import matches posts by their link and refreshes them. The bad rows already imported are cleared with Select on the Posts step (asked for the same day), which ticks posts and removes or moves them with Undo, as the Ads step does.

### 2026-10-02 · A long list is searched
The user asked why no dropdown could be searched by name or by code. A browser's own list jumps only to an option whose label starts with what is typed, and every client and colleague is offered code first (2026-09-26), so a name never matched and a code only when typed fast. A select of ten options or more now opens a finder: a field over the options, every word typed matched anywhere in the label. It was built as an interception, not a replacement: the select stays on the page as drawn, focused and read as before, and only the press, the keys that would open the browser's list, and a typed key are taken, so no page and no suite that sets a value changed. A custom button in the select's place (as the segments do) was weighed and left: it would have moved every form's layout and every suite's `selectOption`. Ten is where a list stops being read at a glance; Status, Format and Priority stay the browser's. A stage select is left alone (a move asks its own questions). On a phone the finder is a sheet of the screen's height, since the iPhone's wheel is slow over forty clients; the tap that opens it must not be the end of a scroll.

### 2026-10-02 · The date box's calendar no longer stays on the page
The user's screenshot: the Timeline's date saved and gone, Safari's calendar still over the record. The DD/MM/YYYY box opens the browser's calendar from a hidden date field that is never focused, so nothing Safari watches ever closes it. After a day is picked, on a press elsewhere and on Escape, the hidden field's type is cycled (`text`, then `date`), which takes the calendar down in every browser. Chrome's own popup eats Escape before the page sees it, so the suite holds the pick and the press elsewhere.

### 2026-10-02 · A report's PDF says it is not final; an admin may confirm their own
The user asked for a watermark on a report's PDF while it is a draft or in review, removed once confirmed and published, after an example of light text repeated on the diagonal. It reads DRAFT (INTERNAL USE ONLY) or PENDING REVIEW (INTERNAL USE ONLY) from the snapshot's status, at 16% of a mid grey on a 35° grid, drawn last on each page so a shaded table head cannot hide it, and faint enough that it hides nothing. Confirmed reports carry none, since confirmation is the team's sign off; the client only ever reads published versions.
The user then asked why an admin who submitted a report could not confirm it ("the hierarchy is not built here?"). The four eyes rule stays for managers, but an admin is the top of the hierarchy and a report waiting on nobody else was stuck. `sm_report_confirm` lets an admin through; the page asks first, saying no second person will have checked it, and the activity record names who confirmed.

The row now carries the audience and the dates only; the objective heads the table on both sides. A lone reference reads "1 Ad ID", so it reads as a count and not a label.

### 2026-10-02 · Interactions and engagements filled from Meta's parts
The user's reports read engagements and interactions as unavailable after the Meta import. Instagram's export carries Likes, Comments, Shares and Saves but no interactions or engagements column; Facebook's carries Reactions, comments and shares (read as interactions) but no engagements. An account shows whichever figures it was set to, so one of the two always came out empty. The import now sums the parts into interactions where no column gives them, and takes engagements as the interactions where none is given: for an organic post Meta counts the two the same. A report imported before this is put right by importing the same file again, which matches each post by its link.

The user asked for Results by objective to be filled after an import, as reach, impressions and amount spent are, with each objective's amount spent beside its results as in their earlier hand-made reports. Writing the sums into the fields was weighed and left: a stored copy goes stale when an ad is edited, removed or imported again. On a report in review the read-only Figures and Commentary drew every label over its value at the card's edge: the facts were not wrapped in their rows and the card had no section padding. They now use the record's facts card, one section a group, label beside value, a written point a line.
The walk then caught the picker's edge as a card outline (it is a menu, which keeps its edge) and showed Escape closing the sheet under the picker once focus had left its field; Escape now shuts the picker first wherever focus is.

### 2026-10-02 · Invoice PDFs behind five-minute links
The user asked whether the S3 short-lived links ("batch 2" of the Documents register) had been built: they had not. Of the two scopes offered they chose documents only, since signing every media file would touch every page that plays a video. The one document the portal stores on S3 is a campaign's invoice PDF; letters and reports are redrawn on Download and never stored. A new invoice is now signed under `private/` (`sign-upload` with `private: true`, the console only, a PDF only), the row keeps the key, and `sign-download` hands a link that works for five minutes, finding the file from what the caller may already read: the campaign row read with a colleague's own token, or what `get_campaign` gives the client's link (which withholds the invoice until a creator is confirmed). The browser never names a path. An older invoice keeps its public address, because moving it would mean deleting the original and nothing deletes from S3; Remove PDF and upload again closes one. The code ships behind `privateInvoices` (off) because the bucket needs two hand steps first (the upload key may write and read `private/`; a bucket-policy deny shuts every other reader out of it); the switch is turned on once the user has done them. The user also asked about deleting the files the daily report names as unused: 18 files, 198 MB, about USD 0.005 a month. The report stays report-only, as the user decided on 2026-09-28.

### 2026-10-03 · Content Review and creator media behind a pass
After the invoices, the user asked that Content Review files and creator drafts go private too, worried a client or creator would open a review and find the video gone. Files are never moved or deleted, only the way in changes, so the worry is a link expiring under an open page. Signed URLs per file would have rewritten every media address on six pages and broken the browser's own cache with a new address each visit. CloudFront's signed cookies do neither: one policy over `content/*`, signed by `media-pass` after the page proves its review link, selection link, creator's code or sign-in (the same functions that answer those pages), set on `adspace.me` so `mycdn.adspace.me` receives them, for twelve hours. Every stored address and every link stays as it is. A file that fails with the pass gone asks again once and loads, a video from where it was, so a tab left open overnight recovers on its own; a refresh always does. The `.web.mp4` copy not yet made fails by design and never asks. The key pair is made by the function and kept in `app_secrets` (as the push keys are), so no private key passes through the chat; the user pastes the public half into CloudFront. The pass covers all of `content/` rather than one client's folder (a CloudFront policy holds one statement, and the selection page draws files from several folders): a raw address alone answers 403, and an address is a random UUID no page shows. Cost: no charge for signing; delivery as before through CloudFront, with the cache key free of cookies. Cut over in two steps so nothing breaks: the pages carry the pass first while CloudFront still answers everyone, then the `content/*` behaviour trusts the key group; undoing that one setting makes everything public again.

### 2026-10-03 · Private invoices switched on
The user added the upload key's `private/*` permission and the bucket policy's Deny (`docs/S3-STORAGE.md` §5a, §5b), so `privateInvoices` was turned on: every invoice PDF uploaded from now on is kept under `private/` and opens only through a five-minute link. Invoices uploaded before keep their public address until they are removed and uploaded again.

### 2026-10-03 · The media pass switched on
The user created CloudFront's public key and key group (`docs/S3-STORAGE.md` §6a). Its ID was stored as `cf_media_key_id`, the live function was seen signing a twelve-hour policy over `content/*` with it, and `privateMedia` was turned on, so every page carries the pass while CloudFront still answers everyone. Requiring it (§6b) is the user's next step.
