At a desk the DD/MM/YYYY placeholder now waits under Not set until the field is reached, where the two had printed over each other.

### 2026-10-03 · Digital namecards
The user had been building a digital namecard by hand on adspacestudios.com and asked for one shaped like a card, the wordmark first. Designed on a canvas over four rounds: a card, not a page; white by default; Apple and Aesop rather than a template; no rule under the wordmark and no location line; never true black or true white for the brand's text, only its five tones (#ffffff, #f5f5f5, #a1a1a1, #1a1a1a, #cfd0d4) with the wordmark #1a1a1a on white or #515151 on #f5f5f5; then the office number, the address and a QR a client can scan from the colleague's phone. The user then asked whether a #f5f5f5 card on white would be better (it is: the card reads as an object with no shadow) and sent the signboard as the one lockup for the tagline: measured, the tagline runs about 1.64 times the wordmark's width, so the card sets it at 0.46 of the wordmark's size in Slate Regular. Asked that the mobile read in the office's format (+60 12-345 6789) and that every colleague have a card they can reach from their own account. Built as a page of the portal, not the website: `team_members` gains `mobile`, `card_email` (two of ten colleagues sign in with an address that should not be on a card) and `card_key`. The address carries a key rather than the name (`/card/?k=`): a card is public by intent, but a readable address would let anybody list the team's mobiles. The key never changes, so a printed QR keeps working, and the card goes the day the colleague's access does. No photograph and no Chinese name for now; either is a column and a line when wanted. A friendlier address (hi.adspace.me/name) is a Short Link pointed at the card.

### 2026-10-03 · Invoice links on mycdn.adspace.me
The user opened a private invoice and found the address bar reading `myadspace.s3.ap-southeast-5.amazonaws.com`. The five-minute link was an S3 presigned URL because `private/` is closed to CloudFront. `sign-download` now signs a CloudFront URL instead (a canned policy for that one file, five minutes, with the key CloudFront already trusts for the media pass) once CloudFront serves `private/*` behind the key group and the bucket policy lets the distribution read it (`docs/S3-STORAGE.md` §5d). The switch is `cf_private_ready` in `app_secrets`, so turning it on or off needs no deploy.
Switched on the same day: CloudFront's OAC was already on the origin, but the bucket policy let it in by name only once the Deny's placeholder carried the distribution's ID. Checked live: the signed link answers the PDF from mycdn.adspace.me, the address without its signature answers 403, and the S3 address answers 403.

### 2026-10-03 · The namecard's lockup on the golden ratio
The user, on the live card: the wordmark can be smaller, check the golden ratios of the spacing. The signboard already sets the tagline about φ times the wordmark, so one sequence fixes both: the card's width is φ² wordmarks and φ taglines (a 326px card: wordmark 124px, tagline 201px), sized from the card's own width so the console's sheet and every phone hold it. The lockup was moved to the golden point, 38.2% down the card, and the user put it back: it is centred, both ways. The suite measures the ratios and the centre.

### 2026-10-03 · A short link for every namecard
The user asked for a short link for every card and chose one made from the name and edited per person in Team, over a name with three added characters (the safer choice: a name alone lets anybody guess a colleague's link and read their mobile). `team_members.card_slug` is made from the name with no space as a colleague is added (Xue Yi reads `xueyi`, numbered where taken; the user changed it from `xue-yi` before it shipped), never follows a rename, and is edited in the Team sheet's Namecard section and by the colleague in My namecard (`namecard_save_card`, with the mobile; `namecard_save_mine` stays for a page from before). The card's slugs live in their own column, not as rows of `links`, so the Short Links list stays the team's campaigns and a card's link is governed by the card (On/Off, active) rather than by Pause; one slug is never both (`slug-taken` either way, `links_card_clash`). `link_resolve` answers a card's slug with the card's own address, so the `hi.adspace.me` Worker needed no change. My namecard shows and copies the short link. The QR on the card keeps the card's key address, which never changes, so a printed code survives an edited slug.

### 2026-10-03 · The mobile on the card is the colleague's to show or hide
With the short links built, a card's name link let anybody read the colleague's mobile. Three ways were weighed with the user: a second link with a three-character code that showed the mobile for 24 hours (mocked up), the same with a QR that followed the choice (the user: a QR shown in person exists so the other person can save the number), and the one the user chose: one card, one link and one QR, with Mobile on card Show / Hide as each colleague's own setting ("mobile numbers is more of personal preference"), turned on before a meeting and off after. It is far less to build and nothing expires or confuses. The trade-off, accepted: while shown, everyone holding the link sees the number, and hiding it does not take it back from a phone that saved it. Hidden, `namecard_get` sends no mobile at all, so the number never reaches the page. Every card starts shown, as before (the user said go ahead without choosing; shown keeps today's cards as they are). `namecard_save(p_mobile, p_slug, p_show)` is a new name rather than a third argument on `namecard_save_card`, because PostgREST cannot choose between overloads and dropping the old one needs the SQL Editor.

The row now reads name and ID, then where they sit, then the address; the ID stays searched. Active and Card on are true of nearly every row, so they say nothing (the accent rule); the state column names the exceptions alone: Inactive, Access expired, and now Card off for a colleague still working. The Overview is what a person oversees, so a section's cards now ask Full Access on it or its part; the granted parts (Reports and Open work in My Work, Performance in Team) keep asking their own grant. ### 2026-10-03 · Leads, past clients and a group's own clients
The user: leads and past clients are more the managers' and sales' portion; "give option to view or manage or x leads, same goes to past clients", designed together; and Own clients only (listed as not built since September) "covers all at this moment", with a lead nobody holds seen and taken. Two parts under Clients, Leads (Lead, Contacted, Proposal sent) and Past clients, each No Access, View or Manage, narrow the section and never widen it, so a lead is read at the lesser of the two. The Members group starts at No Access on both (the user's choice), filed in its history once; every other group is as it was. Own clients only is a group's choice in the Clients fold (`client_scope`): the colleague sees the clients they are Person in charge of and any lead nobody holds, which Take lead makes theirs. The rule is one function, and the database holds it wherever a client's work is kept: a restrictive read policy on the client and every table under it (a restrictive policy narrows the ones already there, so none was dropped), a guard trigger on each that also catches the security definer functions writing them, My Work's two visibility helpers (a colleague's own tasks always; the whole queue and the months only on clients they may see), the task-create check and the report, and the four functions that read past the policies. Person in charge moves from one colleague to another only at Clients Full Access, so nobody scoped can hand themselves a client; Team Full Access may too, because standing a colleague down and renaming one carry onto their clients (and Team Full Access can already change any group). Not covered: the Activity record (one log; its tabs are their own parts), and a client's own pages, which read through their token functions. A removal is held by the read policy and the delete functions' own checks, not by the trigger, because the connector holds any statement carrying the word; a group at Clients Full Access with Past clients at View could still remove a past client's contact.

### 2026-10-03 · Audit rounds 2 and 3: writes that read as done, removals past the scope
The user said fix the round 2 and 3 findings in the next batch. Fixed together:
- A refused campaign write read as done: PostgREST answers a refused update or delete with 204, and about forty writes across Creator Campaigns, Clients, Content Review and Team took no `.select()`. Each now names a refusal ("Not saved." / "Not deleted. The database refused the request."); Confirm creators counts the bookings not confirmed before it moves the campaign on. Deleting a campaign, or a creator from the Creators List (labelled Remove, though permanent), now takes the name typed back.
- A client page printed the database's message (the selection page's cover, a draft decision, the portal's Unable to load, a request or a withdrawal); each now reads the page's own words in English and Chinese (`W.notSent`).
- A campaign's Edit details and a set's Rename were offered at View.
- A link to a client outside the colleague's reach dropped to the list as if nothing was asked; it now says so. Under Own clients only a new lead's Person in charge is fixed to whoever keys it in, since a lead handed on leaves their sight the moment it is saved.
- The five delete functions (client, Letter of Offer, document, report, month) are security definer and asked the section's level only, so a colleague on Own clients only, or with Leads or Past clients below Manage, could remove a record they could not open. A guard before each removal on those five tables asks the scope rule at Manage (`client_scope_removal`); this also closes the gap recorded for Past clients at View. It holds the word delete, so it is the user's to run in the SQL Editor.
- `invite-member` let a client invite through on the retired `can_clients` column, which answers for nobody under the levels, so only an admin could invite a contact. It now asks the caller's own Contacts level at Work and reads the contact as the caller, so a client outside their reach invites nobody.

### 2026-10-04 · The gate never reads stale
Twice in one round a result was read from the wrong run: a suite run by hand during a gate tested the gate's frozen snapshot (8899 served it), and `/tmp/gate.txt` still held an earlier commit's result while the next gate ran. `snap.sh` now serves its snapshot on 8898 with the copied suites rewritten to it, keeps 8899 on the repo (restarting it if it was left on a snapshot), and writes `running: <sha>` into the result file at the start; the final file opens `snapshot: <sha>`.

### 2026-10-04 · Field audit: every write checked against the live schema
Every insert, update and call in `js/` was read out (117 writes, 183 calls) and its literal columns and arguments compared with the live database: all 609 exist. The 57 payloads built in a variable cannot be read statically, so the stand-in now holds the live map and refuses an unknown table, column, function or argument the way PostgREST does; every suite that drives a save now proves its fields land somewhere real, and a field renamed on one side alone fails its suite. The database's 44 limits were compared with the forms: performance scores stepped by 0.5 where the database and the refusal both allow 0.1 (now 0.1), and a Handbook link typed `HTTPS://` passed the page and was refused by the database (now saved as `https://`). The Short Links save still runs on the older `stub.js` in `run.js`, which has no map.

### 2026-10-04 · A named reviewer for every report; Draft with AI counted by subject
The user asked for a reviewer to be assigned on submit (never oneself), an admin override, and a notification. Submit now asks for the reviewer from the colleagues at Reports Full Access (or an admin), defaulting to the client's last; only that reviewer or an admin confirms, an admin's confirmation filed "in place of" the reviewer; the reviewer, an admin or the submitter sends it back; Change reviewer covers leave. Notifications ride the existing bell and push with a new report link. The report's contents are checked before the reviewer, so a submit with nothing in it says what is missing first. Separately, the user found that deleting a report and starting it again freed a second Draft with AI: a draft was counted by its report, whose link a delete clears. A press now keeps the report's client, kind and period, and the one-draft rule counts any report of the same client and kind whose period shares a day with it (overlap, so a custom 1 to 29 Sept cannot slip past). Members still hold Reports Full Access, so every colleague can be picked as reviewer; narrowing it is the user's decision.

The user found that a draft not saved before moving away was paid for and lost. An admin now has Draft with AI usage in the Reports bar's ⋯: the team, each colleague's default and every colleague who may draft, with the last 24 hours, the last 30 days and the allowance a day, each changed to Default, Stopped or a number (`ai_draft_limits`, filed under Team). A report's one draft and an admin's five redrafts a day are unchanged. Check and submit now ends in Key dates (started, submitted to, confirmed by, published, each with the time it took), the report's own timeline; linking it to the month's tasks waits on the month-to-report proposal.

### 2026-10-04 · A month owes its reports; a report waits on its month
The user: some clients take content only, some ads only, some both; some months start on the 16th; a report is due seven days after the month ends; a late one must say why; admins and Full Access may go past. A month now ticks Accounts report and Advertising report and names the day it starts (prefilled from the month before, so a change carries forward). From October 2026, Submit for review checks the client's month: it asks for the report, holds its task, and holds the planned content tasks (a client with none planned and the meeting not needed passes). A report outside it is refused unless an admin or Full Access gives a reason; a late one asks why. Both reasons stay on the report and in the record. Earlier reports are not gated, so September's are submitted as before. Checked live before shipping: the Report template sets a first draft four working days from creation, which for a month saved late falls after the report's due date and would refuse the save; a report task's first draft is now two days before its due date. A month saved for any other reason no longer resets a report task's dates, so an extension stands.

An outlined option was weighed and refused (it reads as a field, and the contact chip is the one outlined control); a pill was chosen: every segment (`.seg`) is round, track, options and thumb, while tab and view strips keep their corners, so round chooses and rectangular acts. The draft row now names the language at the left and puts Write draft at the right edge with its count before it. The same day: a task deleted from its sheet was read again and the sheet printed "Cannot coerce the result to a single JSON object" (the logs showed two 406s on the deleted id). A read with no row now closes the sheet or record and the list says the task is no longer available. The stand-in had answered `.single()` with null instead of PostgREST's refusal, which is why no suite saw it; it now refuses as PostgREST does. The client record's read of its content sets asked for a `state` column `batches` never had, so every read failed and was drawn as none; it reads `published`. Key dates under a report ran its rows on the card's bare edge, the last letter clipped; they sit in the card's own section.

### 2026-10-04 · A phone's empty month
The user: My Work's Calendar was missing. On a phone the calendar is a list of the days that hold work, and with the test tasks deleted October held none, so nothing drew under the month's bar. A month with no work now says "No tasks this month." on a phone; the desk's grid already showed the empty month.

### 2026-10-04 · Finding a finished task took three controls
The user could not find #WT00001 (finished 27 Sept) as an admin until they set Whose work, the view and the period, and even All tasks showed nothing. Two faults: the period, which bounds finished work, was drawn only after the Filters card was shut and opened again, so All tasks quietly meant this week; and a search looked only at what the view had loaded. Now a search asks the database for any task the colleague may see, by title, code, description or number; the open card follows a control the page shows or hides; the period is named Completed; and an empty list says what it holds back in a few words (No open tasks. Show completed). The user asked for short copy without interpuncts.

### 2026-10-04 · Fewer filters, each saying something new
The user asked for the filtering flows to be audited and simplified: finding every task took Whose work, a fourteen-option view, Group by and a period. My Work's Show now has three answers (Open, Completed, All); where the work has got to is Group by's question. Following left Whose work (reviewers and contributors are named on the task). Six filters repeated the cards they sat over (Creator Campaigns state, Documents kind, Team group, Services category, Handbook category, Short Links state) and are gone. Group by and Sort ride in the Filters card as views (`data-view`): no badge, and Clear leaves them. Options for all read All stages, All people, All platforms.

### 2026-10-04 · Initiatives and the monthly reflection
The user asked how the portal could help the team grow and take initiative without micromanaging, in a small agency. Initiative and improvement is already scored (10 points), so an initiative a colleague logs (Proposed, then Adopted or Not now, then Done) is shown beside that score in the review; the reviewer still scores it. A reflection (Proud of, Found hard, Want to learn) reaches management only once its author shares it, and is never scored: growth data belongs to the person. Both sit behind My performance's fresh proof, and management's side behind the master code, as every review does. A growth page and an AI read of change notes wait for real data (My Work held one task that day); the AI check before AQC review was dropped because AQC happens on WhatsApp.

### 2026-10-04 · Pick up work: Open to take
The user approved pulling work as one of the growth items: a colleague with room takes a task instead of waiting to be handed one, and a Task Owner who is overloaded says so without naming who should take it. The task never goes ownerless: the owner offers it (Offer to the team), anyone at My Work Work who may see its client takes it, and the taker becomes the one owner in the same write, the owner before told. Any other change of hands ends the offer by trigger, so an offer never outlives the owner who made it. Offered tasks head every colleague's own queue in an Open to take band rather than a separate page nobody opens.

### 2026-10-05 · A client paused or past says why, and its open work is delivered
The user asked for a required reason when a client moves to Paused or Past, so the coming Sales view can say why clients leave, and for the client's unfinished tasks to be marked for urgent delivery: Paused and Past follow the invoice period, and revisions and reviews often run past it. The reason is kept on the stage move itself (`stage_log`), not on a separate table, so it travels with the history the Timeline and the dashboard already read. Urgent delivery is worked out from the client's stage on every load and never written to a task, so moving the client back to Active clears it and nobody's own priority is overwritten. Each owner is told once by trigger. The chip is red, as the user was told; Overview's card asks `ops.all` because it reads the whole team's work.

### 2026-10-05 · Sales: the Clients list read as figures
The user asked for a sales view for admins and Clients Full Access, with money for admins only. A lead and a client are one record at different stages, so the view is a second reading of the Clients list rather than a section of its own, and every figure comes from the stage history each client already carries: nothing new is typed, so nothing can fall out of date. The words were settled with the user: Churned is a move to Past, Paused is at risk and counted apart, Paused back to Active is Resumed and Past back to Active a Win-back; a lead moved to Past is Lost, never churn. Each figure opens the clients behind it, because a count nobody can check is not trusted. Value is the confirmed lines' monthly figure by market, never a sum of RM and S$.

### 2026-10-05 · Sales reads as a summary, not a report
Asked whether the first Sales view was the best summary, the honest answer was no: the figures a director looks for sat as rows in a nine-row table, nothing said whether a figure was better or worse than before, the clients needing a call were counts rather than names, and the desk's width was spent on one long column. The user chose to ship it and rework next. The view now opens on four headline figures with their change on the period before (the same days of last month for a month in progress, so the comparison is fair early in a month), then the period's leads step by step beside a named Needs attention list, the two month charts side by side, and the breakdowns two across. The money stays an admin's, stated once.

### 2026-10-05 · One draft a report, one figures check a version
The user found the figures check could run twenty times a day on one report: it counted only against the colleague's day. The rule is now the report's own: one draft a report from colleagues, its revisions drafted inside that one, and one figures check a version, so each revision earns one more check. A colleague has ten AI uses a day, drafts and checks together, so five reports set up with a draft and a check each; an admin twenty, with five drafts and five checks a report a day. Every number is a setting under AI usage, as before.


### 2026-10-05 · Reward figures are settings; the individual prize has no minimum
Asked where the quarter's individual and department amounts are entered, then: "please do not make the figures hard coded. what if i need changes the next quarter. do you remember agile?" Every figure the rewards use (both prizes, the department's qualifying total, flexible hours' share and month, the bonus pool and trip revenue gates, the pool's share of profit, months at B, units by grade, the commission floor) moved into `perf_settings`, each from a quarter on, seeded as the rules stood from Q3 2026. An admin changes them in Reward settings (Quarters and Bonus bars) behind the master code; a change never reaches into a confirmed quarter or half, is filed from and to, and every calculation returns the rules it used so a confirmed snapshot keeps its own. While it was being built the user set the prize rules: the individual prize (RM 400) has no minimum average, the highest eligible average takes it whole; the department prize (RM 600, variable) goes to the winning department's leader, who splits it, as already built on 2026-10-01; everything else stays. The minimum-average setting was dropped, so a quarter now reads 13 settings. The standing rule went into `CLAUDE.md`: no business figure the team may change is typed into code; the remaining fixed figures are swept and listed for the user next.

### 2026-10-05 · AI by colleague, report and day; the notes box fills its row; the step strip slides
The user: "just give per pax per report per use per day ... if they do the report last minute ... they need to be responsible for the late until next day. same goes to the figure check. resets daily". The limits moved from one draft a report and one figures check a version (whoever pressed) to each colleague's own: one draft and one figures check on a report a day, an admin five of each, all inside the day's ten uses (an admin's twenty), reset at 12:00 am MYT. A revision the same day brings no new check. `2026-10-05-ai-limits-per-person-day.sql`. The same screenshot showed Notes for the draft narrower than the fields under it, and a sideways drag of its corner widening the card past the screen: every typed box now runs its row's width and stretches downwards only. Asked why Reports was not the sliding tab style, the report's step strip became the view strip: the tonal track with the raised surface sliding under the chosen step, scrolling sideways on a phone.

### 2026-10-05 · The AI's notes sit with Write draft; Preview PDF; Edit limits in three pairs
On a screenshot of the Commentary step the user asked whether Notes for the draft, which reads as part of the report's own text, should look different or sit with Write draft. It is input to the AI and never saved with the report, so the language pill, Write draft and the notes became one shaded block above the hint and the fields. Asked whether the PDF could open in the browser rather than download, Preview PDF now opens a tab at the press (a tab opened after the drawing would be blocked as a popup) and fills it with the drawn file; a blocked tab still downloads. On Edit limits ("do you really need a whole long list for this, for real"): the form listed every colleague under the six standards. It is now the six standards in three pairs, and a colleague's own limit is set by pressing their row on the usage page, the one place their figure is already read.

### 2026-10-05 · The quarter is ranked as its months are released
With July and August 2026 released and September to come, the user found Q3's ranking empty ("12 months not final") and asked for the average and the order now, final only once all three months are. The ranking read final months alone, and a month becomes final only after its dispute window and acknowledgement, so the order stayed blank for most of a quarter. It now reads every month released to its member: the same figures the colleague already sees. A draft never counts, since its scores are still being entered. The quarter says it is provisional, each row says how many of its months are final, and whoever is ahead reads Leading. Confirm is unchanged: it still waits for every month to be final, so what is paid is never provisional. The bonus period and flexible hours still read final months only.

### 2026-10-05 · The business figures the team may change are settings
After the reward figures, the user chose the rest to make editable: the client follow-up limits, SST, the term adjustments, the grade bands, the breach deductions, the dispute window and the report deadline (now one date, seven days after the month ends, with no separate first draft: the user, "one date, 7 days"); the Creators List price bands stay fixed ("NO NEED"). Each was asked to sit where it is used. Performance's figures are effective from a quarter, like the rewards, and are edited in the same sheet, renamed Performance settings and reachable from the Months bar too. The rest are effective from a day (`app_settings`), never one already past, so a letter redrawn later is priced with the SST and terms of the day it was issued: Follow-up limits on the Clients bar, Tax and terms on the Services bar, Report deadline in My Work's ⋯, each an admin's. Pages read the figures once when they open (the client pages too, as SST is on every quote) and wait for them before drawing money. The taxes Meta charges on an ad account, printed in the ads report, stay as words: they are statutory and not ours to set.

### 2026-10-05 · A bar of three plain actions folds on a phone
Performance settings joined Review list and Print month on Performance's Months bar, and the three overflowed a phone by 51px at 390 (120 at 320); the walk's next press then timed out under the overflow. The ⋯ existed only beside a primary, so a bar of plain actions had nowhere to fold. `js/cmdbar.js` now keeps the first plain action and folds the rest once there are three; two still sit side by side, as Clients' and Documents' bars do.

### 2026-10-05 · TikTok in the Advertising Report
The user noticed the Advertising Report read Meta alone and chose one report for both platforms, the export to follow. The structure was built ahead of it: an ad names its platform, TikTok's account figures sit apart from Meta's (a person reached on both counts on each, so reach is never totalled), the objectives split by platform only where a report holds both, so a Meta-only report reads exactly as before, and the tax note is Meta's alone (TikTok's billing wording is the user's to give). TikTok's column names (Cost, Result, Ad group name, 2-second and 6-second video views) were taken from TikTok Ads Manager's standard export and are marked provisional; the first real export decides the mapping. The next report carries each platform's figures forward on its own, and Draft with AI is told the two are separate platforms.

### 2026-10-05 · Date of evaluation prefilled with today
On the review sheet's Evaluation and follow-up card the user asked for the date of evaluation to be prefilled with today. While a review holds none, the field now opens on today (Malaysia time), inside its own bounds (in or after the month reviewed, never after today) and editable; saving keeps it. Release still fills the date where it is empty, so a month released without the sheet's save is unchanged.

### 2026-10-05 · Performance history in plain words
The user found the History lines too hard to read ("Department scores saved: Creative, on-time creative delivery not set to 18 … critical issue cleared"; Started listed below its own first save). A first save now reads "Scores entered" with each figure after its name ("Role output quality 19"), a later save names only the move ("Role output quality 19 → 22"), a removed figure reads "removed", the criteria keep their own case, Critical issue is named only when it was ticked or cleared, and Started sorts below an entry filed in the same moment. The department and company reward entries read the same way.

### 2026-10-05 · A shorter ⋯ on a task
The user found the task ⋯ too long (six items: Open full record, Offer to the team, Change Task Owner, Make a copy, Repeat on a schedule, Delete). The row's ⋯ now holds only what the row cannot already do: the offer (and Take where the row shows no Take button) and Delete. The name opens the task, where the full record, a copy and a repeat are; the owner cell changes the owner; the stage select moves it. The sheet's ⋯ loses Open full record, which is already the sheet's last line. A row with nothing to hold draws no ⋯.

### 2026-10-05 · My Work defects from the simplicity audit
The audit (the My Work Simplicity Audit doc) found three defects, fixed together. Adding several posts at once hid the dates and worked them out: each got a tentative publish day in its week and a due date two working days before it, so four Week 1 reels added on 5 Oct came out due 29 Sept to 2 Oct. The user ruled that dates are planned by people, never worked out: every post line now carries its own due date, a blank one reads Not set, a passed one is refused, and `ops_create_pieces` asks `ops_create_task` to take dates as given (only a repeat still takes a tentative day to count from). Moving a task into AQC review from the list kept its owner while the task's own button asked who takes it; both now ask. The board's bars filled from logged hours, and none had ever been logged; they now fill from the estimates of open work due this week. The results check moves from 3 to 7 days after posting (the user: 3 days is too short to read performance). Decided at the same time: AQC review keeps its name, Approved and Scheduled become one column, anyone at My Work level adds tasks.

### 2026-10-05 · Plain words across Performance and My Work
The user, reading Performance: "why use evidence. Sounds like some criminals. Could you reaudit all the phrases or terms used in the portal?" The words were the company policy's legal register (Evidence, Breach, Dispute, Upheld, Released, the grades Needs Guidance and Performance Review, the Accountability path, "Management review is triggered") and, in My Work, ticketing jargon the team does not use (Task Owner, Content deliverable, Pieces, Scope, Content month, Deliverable format, Final due, First draft, Scheduled publish, Engagement, Readiness). Performance now reads Notes and Reference, Issue (Add issue, Issues this month, Withdraw), Query (Raise a query, Query raised, Query answered, Queries until), Agreed / Partly agreed / Not agreed, Shared (Share with {name}), Needs support and Improvement plan, the recovery path, and "Management will follow up."; the PDF follows, and its repeat and late points now come from the month's settings instead of a typed 5. My Work reads Assigned to (Reassign, Unassigned, Keep it assigned to me, Assign to), Content task, Posts and Add another, For, Month, Format, Due date, Draft due, Post date, Checklists, and the task type Retainer. Only words moved: every stored key (`released`, `disputed`, `breach`, `upheld`, `engagement`, the notice kinds) stays, so history, reports and permissions are untouched. The two notices a query sends are written by the database, so `2026-10-05-performance-plain-words.sql` restates perf_decide and perf_dispute with the new words alone (`tests/perf.js` §23 proves they are otherwise their last definitions). Kept on purpose: Request extension (the control only asks, a recorded rule), Release to client and Void (the team's own trade words), the stage names stored in the workflow (the board batch), and the master code. The server's unassigned label in Open work by person still reads No task owner; every task has someone assigned from creation.

### 2026-10-05 · The performance record in the report's design
The user: "The performance report pdf is also not very golden ratio arranged. Doesnt really follow the report's beauty and design system". It was built on the letter's own layout before the report found its system: nine hand-picked sizes (7.5 to 16pt), gaps of 6 to 22pt, capital headings ("1 · RESULT THIS MONTH"), loose columns ruled at a guessed x, five outlined grade boxes with the grade in solid black (the heaviest thing on the page), Reward eligible and Confidential each said twice, a role name wrapping its facts row, and page two a spill of two sections over a page 70% blank. The letterhead stays (the record is an HR Letter, listed and verified as one); everything under it is now drawn as the Social Media Report is: one scale, title case, the report's tables, the grade shaded in one quiet row, the final score the one bold figure, page one the result and page two the follow-up by design. The issue areas lost the word "risk" (Client and account, Delivery and execution, Compliance and platform, Assets and finance), and the PDF's last old words (Dispute, Breach removed, accountability path) went with the plain-words batch. `tests/perfui.js` reads the file back: title case, PRIVATE & CONFIDENTIAL on every page, and nothing past the 54pt margins.

### 2026-10-05 · The build at the rail's foot
The user asked for the web app's version in very small words under the Activity record, "based on how IT would name the version number". A site deployed several times a day has no release train to number by hand, so it carries a calendar version and the commit, as IT tooling names continuous builds: `v2026.10.05 · 1a2b3c4`. Nothing is typed: `/version.json` has Jekyll front matter, so the GitHub Pages build writes the commit (`site.github.build_revision`) and the build time into it on every deploy, and the console turns the time into the Malaysian day. A hand-kept number was rejected: it would drift the first time a push forgot it. The line tells support which build a colleague is on (before and after Refresh app) at a glance.

### 2026-10-05 · Who reassigns a task; audit round 7 continued
The user decided the question left open by the My Work audit: "the owner can reassign task and user groups above it". The assignee (My Work at Work, their own task) and any group above them (Manage, an admin) now reassign and hand over; naming helpers or the reviewer stays a manager's. `ops_assign_task` had never asked whether the caller may see the task at all; it now asks `ops_may_see_task` as every other task write does. Audit round 7, continued over a month's edits: a cancelled month left its report tasks live in My Work (deleting the month cancelled them); a reopened month never asked for them again; and a new manager left the month's report tasks with the one before. Each now keeps the rule deleting and unticking keep: only a task still at its first stage moves. The Cancel month question says so.

### 2026-10-05 · The Reports list as tabs, Published held to a period
The user: "reports section still missing the sliding tab", and "after 35 months and 10 clients each month … a long run down list of 350 items reports". The list was four stage cards on one page, Published shut but holding every report ever published. It is now the view strip every other section uses, one tab a stage with its count, the reports under it in a card a report month, newest first. Published shows the last three months unless another period is chosen, with only its newest month open, so a year on it reads the same as today; a search ignores the tab and the period and finds any report by client, code, month or type. The type filter's empty option read Every type; it reads All types, as the rule for every filter's all option asks.

### 2026-10-05 · A meeting outside a month
The user: "sometimes we also create adhoc meeting (not new month) but to record inside as extras adhoc. Whats the best option". Three options were weighed: a meeting on My Work (a task is work owed, not a meeting), a second meeting on the month (it would tie an ad hoc call to a month it is not about), and a Meeting entry in the client's Calls and visits, which already held the kind and is where the team records what was said. The last was taken ("Ok"). A Meeting entry now takes a time in Malaysia, a length and a link; Create Google Meet books it through the same `meet-create` and the same shared calendar as the month's content meeting, with the same check that no other online meeting holds the slot. The two functions it asks run as the caller, so the table's own policies (Clients: Calls at Work, the client scope) decide. A moved time moves the event, and an entry changed to another kind takes the event off. The client's Meetings tab (renamed from Content meetings) lists it as Meeting with its time and the link while ahead; what the team wrote about it never leaves the console. Removing the entry keeps its calendar event, since Undo brings the entry back.

### 2026-10-06 · Three dates a post, and the revision push
The user, from the New task sheet: "Why is there schedule and due date three different date. And when added multiple it only has due dates showing. Instead we have expected first draft date, due date, publish date three for each content (Dont hard coded). First draft is the date ready for AQC review. Due is for client review. After client review if have revision, due automatically pushes next day. Publish is posting". The sheet kept a Schedule section for one piece and a due date a line for several. Every line now carries Draft due, Due date and Post date, typed and never worked out, checked on the line (nothing passed, draft before due, post on or after due); `ops_create_pieces` passes all three for every piece. The push is a trigger on the move into Revision (Client), so the row, the board and the task's button reach it alike; it only moves a due date later, and is filed as a client request. "Next day" is a setting (`revision_due_days`, 1) beside the report's days under My Work's Due dates, since the user rules out hard-coded figures. Shipped on the user's call without the full gate ("I dont have that luxury of time"); the touched suites passed and the gate followed after the merge.

### 2026-10-06 · An import that came out empty
The user: "the accounts report still wouldnt populate data from import". The CSV they shared read whole (17 Facebook posts, every figure; their Instagram one 24). The saved requests showed what had happened: the import at 11:09 sent only date, caption, link, format and interactions, because the file was Meta's daily breakdown, which carries no Views, Reach or Reactions columns. The parser was right and the file was the wrong one, found only after the posts read empty. The Import sheet now names a file with no Views or Reach column in warn before anything is imported, and says Meta's Lifetime export carries them.

### 2026-10-06 · A report moved to its own client
The user: "Can our team create the report under temporary client first then change it after? We still have a few client list pending." A report's client was fixed from creation (the guard refuses it outside a function), and a report starts only under an Active client, which needs its billing first. Re-creating the report under the real client and re-importing was weighed as enough for a few cases; the user chose "build for admins". Move to client is in a draft's ⋯ for an admin alone: the target must be Active and hold no report of the same kind whose period shares a day (the unique index would refuse the identical period anyway), the accounts, posts and ads follow because they hang off the report, the AI uses move with it so the day's limits still count on the right client, and the move is filed under both clients. A report past draft keeps its client: a reviewer or the client may already have read it under that name.

### 2026-10-06 · Meta's times are Pacific
The user, with Meta Business Suite beside the report: "The timing looks like a bug?" It was. Both Meta exports write Publish time in US Pacific time, whatever the page's own zone: "Celebrate Malaysia Day" reads 09/14/2026 21:03 in the file and 15 September 12:03 on Meta's screen. The import took the written day, so every post before 3 pm (4 pm in the US winter) Malaysian time landed a day early. The date is now the Malaysian day of that Pacific moment, daylight saving included. The same comparison showed Facebook photos with no caption: Meta puts a photo's words under Title and leaves Description empty, and the import ignored Title. Title now stands in for an empty Description, still never as a title. A re-import corrects posts already in a report, since a post is matched by its link.

### 2026-10-06 · Key dates name who did each step
The user, on a report's Check and submit: "submitted by who is missing". Key dates named the reviewer a report went to and who confirmed it, but not who started it or who submitted it. Both are now named (`created_by`, `submitted_by`), on their own line under the date: two full names beside a date and a duration ran past the card on a phone, measured at 390.

### 2026-10-06 · Transfer client, in the team's words
The user, on the Move to client sheet: "the terms and glossary used in the portal shouldn't be such weird language. what do you mean Move to client, why not just use Transfer Client". The menu item, the question and the result read Transfer client, Transfer to another client?, Transfer and Transferred to {client}; the function and its rules are unchanged.

### 2026-10-06 · Partners' logos on white-label reports
The user: ADspace subcontracts white-label work from partners; one partner (Partner A) hands over several of its own clients, and their reports should carry Partner A's logo, landscape, never the client's round mark. Setting the brand on each report was weighed and set aside: every report of such a client wants the same logo, and a per-report switch leaves a month to go out as ADspace by mistake. A partner holds its clients instead, edited from the Reports bar's ⋯ (an admin or Reports Full Access, as the user asked). Only the head mark changes: the user kept the ADspace glossary link and the rest ("they know its adspace doing the works, just that report wise we courtesy do fit for our partner"), so the client portal and the file's properties are untouched. The logo is kept as a PNG data address in the database, not a file on the CDN, so drawing the PDF never waits on a media pass, and the snapshot carries it, so a published version keeps the logo it went out with. The client link has no foreign key: a cascade would put the word the connector holds into the migration, and a row left by a removed client names nobody.

### 2026-10-06 · Partners would not open; a campaign never completed
The user, an admin, opened Reports → Partners and read "This needs a higher access level". The partner-client table's scope rule called `client_seen`, which a login may not run, so every read failed whoever asked; the smsql fixture let a login run it, and no check read that table as a login. The rule now asks `client_scope_free` / `client_scope_ok` like every other table (fixed live in place, `alter policy`), the fixture holds the live grants, and smsql reads the table as a login.
The same hour the user asked how to mark a creator campaign completed. Nothing did: `syncCampState` only ever moved In production back to Open, so 988 Ultimate Stage, its three creators Completed, still read In production. Completed is now derived as In production is: every booked creator Completed completes the campaign, a revert puts it back, filed `campaign.stage`.

### 2026-10-06 · A content set shows its own counts, never the last one's
The user: opening another client's set in Content Review still showed the previous client's pending counts and videos. `loadPosts` cleared nothing until its answer came, a set with no posts returned before redrawing the strip, covers line or progress, and a slow answer for a set already left could paint over the next. A new set now clears them first (`clearPostView`), an empty set clears them for good, and an answer for a set no longer open is thrown away. `tests/crswitch.js` reproduced both before the fix.

### 2026-10-06 · Platforms in their own colours on the report's chart
The user, releasing a report: Views by week stacked its platforms in three greys, the third repeated past the third platform, and two accounts set to Other both read "Other". The brand stays monochrome; the platforms are not: each takes its own colour (Facebook #1877F2, Instagram #E1306C, the two together Meta's #0866FF, TikTok #111111, rednote #FF2442), a platform with none or one already used takes the next of a set kept apart from them, and only the marks are coloured. An account on a platform the list does not name is named by the account, in the chart and every table.

### 2026-10-06 · White label is chosen on the report; platforms by name
The user corrected the Partners model: the partner (The Peakle Creative) is the client, billed and in the portal; ADspace services the partner's own clients (SKS City Mall JBCC) under the partner's name. So a partner no longer holds clients: the report, still under the partner, chooses in its ⋯ the partner's logo and the brand it covers (`sm_reports.partner_id`, `brand_name`, `sm_report_white_label`), and the snapshot names the brand with no client logo. The partner sheet is a name and a logo.
Asked for Douyin, Pinterest and 大众点评, and for Other to take a typed name: the platform check would need a statement the connector holds for the SQL Editor, so the names are kept as `other` with `platform_name` and named everywhere through `platWord()`.

### 2026-10-06 · HR letters reach the colleague they name
The user asked whether a team member sees a letter issued to them, and chose: shared by default, a tick to keep a sensitive draft back, visible once issued, a notification, and only for letters issued to that person. They then asked whether this needed a wider permission, since most groups hold no Documents access. It does not: the colleague reads through `my_letters()`, a security-definer function that answers only rows naming the caller and sits behind the same fresh proof as their reviews, and the PDF is drawn in their browser from that answer, so no group gains a read of the register. Sharing stays with HR Letters at Work. The bell was drawn only for My Work readers; a notification row was always its reader's own (`ops_notifications_read`), so it is now drawn for every colleague, or a letter's notice would never reach somebody without My Work. Acknowledgement was offered and not taken up, so a letter is read and downloaded only.

### 2026-10-07 · One gear for every Performance figure, in plain words
The user asked why the quarter read "18 months not final" for Q3 2026 and what "Department total at least" meant, and found the settings sheet hard to read. The count was right but the word was wrong: it counts reviews, one a person a month (6 people over July to September), so it now reads reviews. The sheet's labels now state each rule (Department score needed to win, out of 100; Score each person needs; Share of the team that must reach it; Shares of the pool by average grade, never "units"), and the sheet is in four parts matching the views, opening on the one in front. The user also asked why the settings were three buttons in three bars when they cover all of Performance: one gear now sits beside the padlock. On a phone the padlock had already cut the Team tabs short, so the gear and padlock close the Performance view row there. The Performance histories had kept the old two-line list; they are now the one records list.

A report's White label offers only Active clients ticked so, beside the brand it covers, and the function refuses any other (`bad-client`). The logo lives once, on the client, and unticking a client takes its logo off every unpublished report. The Partners list and its sheets are gone; the live database held no partner, so nothing moved. `report_partners`, `partner_id` and `sm_report_white_label` stay unused rather than dropped, since the connector holds any drop.
The user then found White label shown to nobody but admins and missing from the user groups: it is now a permission, Reports: White label, granted like Performance reviews (an admin holds it, any other group once it is set), switched on for Managers and set in the group panel for anyone else. Members, who hold Reports Full Access, no longer see it by that alone.

A report is now its client plus a brand from a saved list on the client's Brand (chosen over typed names, so one brand never splits into two by spelling), each brand its own report a period, its own carry-forward and its own AI quota, and the list marks white-label reports. The user asked for the console's version to read simply, `v26.10.06`: the rail drops the commit and the four-figure year; go-live reports keep the commit beside it to tell two deploys of one day apart.
The same hour the user asked that each brand choose whose mark heads its reports: the client's wide logo, or ADspace's own wordmark (some of a partner's brands go out under ADspace). Set once per brand, not per report, the client's logo by default; a published version keeps the mark it went out with. And asked whether the PDF's link can be shared: it cannot (Preview opens a passing `blob:` address; reports are never stored), so the report's ⋯ gained Download, saving the file under its own name to send.
The same hour the user found the report chart's Instagram pink loud: the platform colours are now one Pantone family of equal depth (modern, minimalist, elegant), each still its platform's hue. And asked for a simple Mark as sent with a date on a published report.

Kept as an admin's by design: the AI allowance an admin is given (a role's allowance, not a permission), the last admin with access never expiring, and the reports' "in place of the reviewer" overrides, which name a person's role rather than an act. `handbook_delete` holds a word the database connector stops on, so it is run in the SQL Editor.

### 2026-10-06 · Every sliding strip measured, so a short strip cannot come back
The user, on an iPhone screenshot of My HR: "As always again? The sliding tab", then: "Seriously always found it but still repeatedly makes mistakes on this multiple times. Could you properly audit ALL sliding tabs". It was the third report of one fault: a strip stopping short of the card under it on a phone (the client portal's tabs on 2026-09-28, Content Review's stages on 2026-09-30, My HR's views now). Each fix covered one kind of strip, and the checks measured those strips by name, so a strip of another kind (here the Activity record's view strip, reused by Performance and My HR, which keeps its desk width) was never measured. The cause of the recurrence was the missing check, not the CSS alone.
The audit: a `strip` check in uxaudit's in-page checks, which matrix borrows, measures every strip on every screen of the walk at 1280 and 390 and at matrix's seven widths and 200% zoom. On a phone a strip must run the row it stands in, up to whatever shares its line; a record's strip, a strip inside a card and a report's steps must at every width; a strip that scrolls must fade an edge; the sliding surface must sit on the chosen option (segments too). The walk also lists every strip it meets and fails one on its list not met at both widths, or one it meets that the list does not name. Run on the code as it was, it found My HR's views 37px short at 390 in every state, and Performance's views 57px short of the padlock and gear at 200% zoom; every other strip passed. The census found three strips the walk had never reached: the reel and cover tabs on a client's review set (the walk read the demo set, which holds no pair), the Activity record's tabs (never opened), and a task record's tabs on a phone. The last was a fault in the walk itself: after Months, My Work holds two bars and two Filters marks, the walk's question about them threw, and on a phone it never reached a task at all, so the task's sheet, its record, its step sheet and its menus had never been measured at 390. The walk now presses the visible mark, opens the Activity record, and reads a review set with a pair.
The fix: a view strip standing on its own row (`.actstrip`) is the column's width on a phone with its views sharing what is spare, and beside the Performance tools it runs up to them.

### 2026-10-06 · A letter's Preview is marked draft; the foot is one row; the verify address is the portal's own
The user, on an Issue document Preview: a watermark like the report's on the preview, the footer's verify sentence on the same row as Page x of x, and every go.adspace.me/verify changed to digital.adspace.me/verify. The Preview now carries DRAFT (INTERNAL USE ONLY) over every page, the report's own wording (the user wrote "DRAFT (FOR INTERNAL USE ONLY)"; one vocabulary was kept and the choice reported), drawn by one function both PDFs now share (`ADspaceDocs.watermark`, moved out of `js/smreport.js`). The foot is one row: the reference on the left, the page count on the right, the verify line centred where it clears both, else in the room between; measured in Slate Book, the unsigned line (358pt at 7.5pt) beside the longest HR reference (74pt) and the page count does not clear both with 12pt gaps on a 487pt line, so that row alone drops to 7pt, the same on every page. The address is `ADSPACE_ORG.verifyUrl` (now digital.adspace.me/verify) in the letters and in the performance record, which had its own typed copy. Letters already issued are redrawn on download, so they take the new foot at once; copies already printed or sent still carry go.adspace.me/verify, which stays a live short link. The body's bottom limit was left where it was, so no letter's pages break differently.


### 2026-10-06 · Sent to the client? asked where the PDF is drawn; Select on the Reports list
The user: Mark as sent sat inside the report's ⋯, out of sight; could a quick button follow the PDF, or one that opens WhatsApp with the PDF and a chosen group? Then: "go but no need the share button just mark as sent line". A web page cannot hand a file to a chosen WhatsApp group (WhatsApp's links carry text only, and the phone's share sheet is the person's own), so no Share button was built. Once a published report not yet marked sent has its PDF drawn (Preview PDF or Download), a line under the head asks Sent to the client?, one press marking it sent today, with Undo where it happened; the ⋯ keeps Mark as sent for any other day. The line stays through the report's own repaints until it is answered or closed, so returning to the tab after sending finds it waiting.
The same message asked where Select is worth adding ("esp Report"). The Reports list gains Select reports in its ⋯: each row of the tab becomes its tick under a bar offering what the tab's reports can take together (Download on every tab, Mark as sent on Published, Publish to client on Confirmed), each through the function one report uses, so every check a report has still applies and a refusal is named against its report. Mark as sent asks one day for all, and Undo puts back each report's own day. The user also confirmed the draft mark's wording, DRAFT (INTERNAL USE ONLY). Content Review sets and Clients follow in their own batch.

### 2026-10-07 · Each published report keeps its PDF as it went out
The user: "if we keep adding or making changes to the pdf, does all created and completed ones still follow the previous or will follow the most latest version? ... if our client received version at (2026/10/06) but in the future maybe 2026/12/12 there is a new version, i no longer can fetch back the 2026/10/06 version only the latest one. is it the proper way of doing?" It was not: a published version kept its data (the frozen snapshot) but the file was redrawn on every download with the drawing of the day, so a later change to the PDF changed what an old download looked like, and the console offered no earlier version at all. Now, once a version is published, its PDF is drawn from that snapshot and stored once in the bucket's private folder for its client; every download of that version, the team's and the client's, hands over that file, read back as bytes through `sign-download` (never a link that could be forwarded), and the head's ⋯ Versions lists every version the client was given with its own Download. A version published before this, or whose first keep failed, is drawn from its own snapshot the next time anybody downloads it and kept then. A version taken off the portal and published again keeps the next file drawn, since its content may have been corrected in between. Stored on S3 rather than Supabase Storage: a report is about 1 to 3 MB, a few hundred a year cost cents on S3, and Supabase Storage's free allowance is shared with the Handbook. Nothing is ever deleted from the bucket, as before.

### 2026-10-07 · Select on Clients and on a content set's posts
The same message that asked for Select on Reports asked where else it is worth it. Two places act on many records at once often enough to need it: handing a book of clients from one person in charge to another (a colleague leaving a portfolio, a new hire taking leads), and deciding a content set's posts together (the client agreed to the whole set on WhatsApp; a set sent back for one reason; a set's discarded drafts). Clients gains Select clients in the bar's ⋯, at Clients Full Access, the one change offered being Change person in charge; a set gains Select posts in its ⋯, offering Confirm internally, Request re-approval and Delete. Each act runs one record after another through what one record takes, so every check a single record has still applies and a refusal is named against its record; the reversible acts carry Undo where they happened (owners put back one by one, confirmations reverted), and Delete takes the count typed back, as My Work's bulk delete does. The tick sits where nothing moves: inside the client's name cell, and in place of a post's ⋯. Other lists were weighed and left: Documents, the Creators List, Short Links and Services rarely take one act on many records, and My Work and the ads and posts of a report already select.

### 2026-10-07 · The stand-in hands a page fresh JSON, as PostgREST does
Testing the kept PDFs found the client's download and the Versions sheet handing over a newly drawn file rather than the one kept. The kept file's key was written, but the stand-in's stored copy stopped updating at that moment, so the next page load read the database from before it. The cause was in the stand-in, not the page: a read handed the page the stored row's own objects, the report engine notes its groups on the snapshot it draws (a reference back to the posts, which no JSON can hold), and from then every save of the stand-in's database failed in silence. PostgREST sends fresh JSON on every read and every function's answer, so the stand-in now does the same; a page changing what it read can no longer change, or break, the row.

### 2026-10-07 · A bulk bar in a narrow pane lays out as on a phone
A content set's posts sit beside their rail, about 550px wide at a desk, and the bar's phone layout keyed on the window, so at a desk the three acts and Done wrapped with Done alone on a second line, and on a phone two labels broke inside their buttons. The bar now states its own line (`data-narrow`): narrow, Done shares the count's line and the acts take the next as equal parts that never break a label, a third that will not fit taking a line of its own. An act is drawn once a ticked post can take it rather than greyed (Request re-approval only for a post the client approved), so a usual selection shows two acts.

The fields are not listed separately: they are the words the wording holds in braces, the convention the user was already asked to mark in their files ({intern name}, {from}, {to}), so writing the letter is declaring its fields and the two cannot drift. Issue refuses a field still in braces; Preview draws what stands, so a half-filled draft can still be read. A type is never removed, only no longer offered, because every issued document names its type; the Activity record files each change under Document types, as settings are filed. The checklists the user will send are forms, not letters, and are left for their own design.

The purpose card gained a head: the ⓘ in the action colour beside the section's name, and a close mark in place of the Hide button under the sentence, so the card says what it explains before it explains it. ### 2026-10-07 · Health check-ins, by consent, with Team: Health for admins
The user asked for a health section (physical, mental, body, sleep) and answered: management alone sees everyone's answers ("only admin can view everyone else health data … we need users to declare they accept"), a check-in every two weeks with the month's reminders, holding the check-in and Ask for a talk, marked in Pantone pink or red with health icons. Health is sensitive personal data under PDPA 2010, so nothing is asked before a colleague agrees, every agreement and withdrawal is kept, and withdrawing takes their answers out of management's view at once (the function sends them only while the agreement stands). The view is a granted part, `team.health`, an admin's by itself and grantable to any group, never a role word. Pink, not red: red already means refuse and destroy in this portal, and a colleague reporting a hard week should not read as an error; Pantone 214 C marks the heart glyph and the chosen answer only, and a low answer is warn with its word. The answers never reach a performance review, the Activity record or a notification's words.

### 2026-10-07 · The Health view: the answers across, warm words, one step between blocks
The user, on a screenshot of the check-in: "So empty are you sure this passed uiux audit on spacing". It had not been gated (it is in the next batch), and it would have passed: the cards stood an even 24px apart because the list's grid gap (12) and each card's own margin (12) added up, and the audit's `stack` check tests that the gaps in a section are even, not that they are the 12 the rule asks for. The box's children now give up their margins, so the gap is the only step. The five answers, stacked label over value in a column that left most of a wide card empty, now run across the card (two across in a narrow pane, the fifth taking the row), the day of the check-in sits under the card's title instead of a green line above it, and the agreement, a card holding one sentence, became one quiet line at the foot. The user also asked for heartwarming words before agreeing: that ADspace cares about their physical and mental health at work and at home, that answers be honest, and that they seek help where needed. The card speaks as the company there (the one place the console says "we"), beside the terms shaded on the record's 1.618 : 1, and the check-in sheet opens on There are no right or wrong answers. A low answer, with no talk already asked for, opens Talks with a word of care rather than a second Ask for a talk button.

Short Links now lists every card's slug under Namecards, between Live and Paused, with whose it is and the card's own address, Inactive or Card off as a chip, Copy and Open and no ⋯ (it is edited in Team or My namecard); the count and the search take them in, and the add sheet refuses a card's slug before anything is written. The card names `hi.adspace.me/{slug}` under the name; the QR itself still holds the card's own address, which never changes, so a printed code survives an edited slug. ### 2026-10-07 · Late is red, waiting is amber
The user: "most of your urgent / late those danger colour ure using amber instead of warnings kind of red ... is it suitable to continue using this amber instead or like pantone red?" Since 2026-09-21 amber had been caution and red refusal or destruction, and overdue sat with caution. The answer given, and built: red for what is past its date, because it needs acting on today; amber for what waits or is due soon, because it needs watching. The red is the existing `--err` (#b3261e, close to Pantone 1805 C; #e8837a in dark), so no token was added and every contrast already held. Red now: My Work's Overdue band and its counts, an overdue date on the row, the board card, the calendar and the task, the Late line, a month's "n late", the report's overdue figures and the open-work chart's note, and Urgent (Urgent delivery already was); Clients' Overdue stage and its count, an overdue next action and follow-up; Sales' leads going cold; the Overview's late figures, late counts and tab counts; a report's Due row past its time. Amber stays for pending, reviewing, waiting on a person, a board column over its WIP, a person over capacity, a report check not met, an Important announcement and the creator's countdown before it passes. The word always rides beside the colour (Overdue, late, Urgent), so the mark survives greyscale.

### 2026-10-07 · A first-visit guide in every section
The user: "i noticed new sections (first time users they dont usually know what to do each) is it possible to add a one time tutorial kind into the entire portal?" The portal's rule had been no explanatory copy, with the route's one-sentence purpose behind the ⓘ as its only exception, and that purpose never opens by itself (it would land over the command bar). A guide is the second exception, kept as small as it can be: up to three steps a section, each one sentence pointing at a control the person can see (a step whose control a permission hides is left out), on a card rather than a cover, hung from that control at a desk and docked at the foot on a phone, so the command bar is never covered and nothing under it is locked. It opens by itself once, when nothing else is open, and again from the ⓘ card's Show me around. Skip, Escape, pressing the ringed control (doing what the step says) or leaving the section ends it and counts it met. A colleague's guides met are kept in the database (`guide_seen`), so a guide met at a desk is not met again on the phone; a client page keeps its own in the browser, in English and Chinese where the page has both. The card is never a `.kmenu`, because the pages shut every open menu on any press elsewhere and a guide has to survive a press on the page it is explaining.

### 2026-10-07 · The guide steps aside
The full gate on the guides found three faults before they shipped. The guide never opened by itself in the console: the Access denied cover, kept in its hidden shell, counted as an open cover, so every offer waited for ever; a layer now counts only when it is drawn. On the review page the guide took Escape while a post was open over it, so Escape no longer closed the post; now whatever is drawn over the page hears Escape first, and opening something over the guide (a sheet, a menu, a post) ends it as met, since the person has moved on. The Clients guide counted its steps before the list had loaded and left the row step out; an offer now waits until no loading rows are drawn and the steps it can point at read the same twice. A scroll had also pulled the page back to the ringed control; it now re-lays the card and never scrolls. On the screenshots, a row's ring was clipped by its table (a line at a desk, nothing on a phone), so a row's ring is drawn inside it; and on a phone the review page's Approve sat under the docked card, so a step on a phone brings its control to the middle of the screen. The walk's Team: Health state had measured the sign-in screen (it reloaded without signing in) and now measures the pane.

### 2026-10-07 · Bold letters in a caption print as words
The user sent the appendix of an accounts report where a caption pasted from Instagram, 𝗙𝗿𝗼𝗺 𝗶𝗻𝘀𝗶𝗱𝗲 𝘁𝗵𝗲 𝗺𝗮𝗹𝗹, printed heavy and uneven. Those letters are not bold type: they are Unicode's mathematical letters (U+1D400 to U+1D7FF), which captions borrow to look bold. Slate has none of them, so the shaper drew each as an emoji picture: one image a letter (18 for those three words), off the line, able to break inside a word, and unsearchable in the PDF. Each is now read as its plain letter (Unicode's own compatibility form) and joined to the word around it; a bold one is drawn in Slate Medium so the emphasis the client chose survives, an italic or script one in the body face. `tests/reports.js` pastes the three words and reads them back as text in the heavier face, with the appendix's pictures down to its real emoji.

### 2026-10-07 · Function hygiene: the Security Advisor's warnings
The user sent Supabase's Security Advisor: 0 errors, 430 warnings. Read against the live database, none was an open door: 257 were functions a signed-in caller may run (how the console works; each checks its caller), 135 functions with raised rights anon could run, 35 functions naming no search path (none with raised rights), pg_net in the public schema (Supabase cannot move it), leaked password protection (the portal has no passwords) and 34 notes on tables read only through functions. Of the 135, 53 showed no standard caller check; each was read: a public page's own function, a trigger (not callable), or a check of the caller's sign-in or grant inside. The user chose the tidy-up as a second lock. `functions_tidy()` names `search_path = public` on every function that names none, and takes EXECUTE from anon (and from PUBLIC, through which Postgres opens every new function) on every function with raised rights but those the public pages call, giving signed-in callers and the server back exactly what they had. The list of public functions is the one the schema already stated, its 28 grants to anon; the suite holds the two equal so the fact has one home. A function named by a policy anon meets would have stayed open (none is: every policy asking a function is for signed-in callers), and no edge function or page calls a closed function without a session: each edge function treats a refusal as denied. A function added later is closed by the next run, which every migration adding a function ends with.

The user: "can you swap Douyin to 抖音", beside 大众点评, which the list already named in Chinese. The word is kept on each account (`platform_name`), and a report past draft refuses row edits, so the saved rows were left as they are and both scripts read the old word as the new (`RENAMED`). The first build named its helper `nameOf`, which `js/reports.js` already declares further down for colleagues' names; the later declaration won and every named platform read Other. The suite caught it, and the helper is `platName`.

An HR letter filled only {first name} and {role} by itself, so a full name would have been typed on every issue. {name} now fills itself with the colleague's full name on an HR letter, as {first name} does.

### 2026-10-07 · Re-audit: a console that could not read who you are, and a report file a scope refused
The user asked for a full re-audit. Two defects were fixed. (1) `loadMe()` treated an error from `me()` (an expired token answers PGRST303) as "no row" and the legacy branch drew the console whole with no access read; the old `activity_viewers` path is gone, an error now asks once more after `refreshSession()`, and one still standing reads Unable to load with Try again (`tests/signin.js`, two contexts, both failing on the old code). (2) `sm_report_keep_file` asks the client at View, but `client_scope_guard` asked the version's update at Work, so a colleague whose group sees past clients at View alone had the download work and the file go unkept, its upload left unnamed in the bucket. Relaxing the guard for the file columns was rejected: a browser's direct write could then point `file_key` at another client's file. The function marks its own write for the transaction (`adspace.keep_file`, cleared after), and the guard lets only that update of `sm_report_versions` through; a page cannot set the mark, since PostgREST reaches functions in public alone (`tests/smsql.js` §9y2). Also found and left with the user: the retired `video-probe` edge function, 77 foreign keys without an index, 11 unused indexes and three duplicate read policies (performance advice, not faults), and 401s from tabs left open on an expired sign-in (their minute checks answer null and change nothing).

### 2026-10-07 · A PDF keeps its name on an iPhone
The user: "when saving / exporting to iphone, the pdf will become unknown.pdf", and "It will follow the usual pdf name right". Every save drew a `blob:` address and either opened it in a tab or clicked an anchor with `download`; Safari on an iPhone keeps no name for a `blob:` address, so Save to Files offered Unknown.pdf. Six copies of that code (letters, client letters, performance records, the register's preview, reports, the client portal's report) became one, `ADspaceDocs.save`: on an iPhone or iPad the file goes to the share sheet as a `File` under the desk's own name; Safari starts a share only close to a press, and a file drawn or fetched too long after one is refused (`NotAllowedError`), so the page then asks File ready · Save, whose press shares it. A Preview on an iPhone therefore opens the share sheet rather than a tab (Markup and Print there show the whole file). A service worker serving the file at a named path was weighed and set aside: the client pages have no worker, and the console's caches nothing by rule. Not provable in the sandbox (no Safari): `tests/savename.js` stubs the share and asserts the name.
